# Śrī Vyāsa-pūjā 1979

## The Meaning of Vyāsa-pūjā by Haṁsadūta Swami

Vyāsa-pūjā is the appearance day of the spiritual master. It is the day when the representative of Śrīla Vyāsadeva is worshiped. Śrīla Vyāsadeva is the incarnation of Godhead who compiled all the Vedic literatures five thousand years ago. The person who can perfectly present the viewpoint of Śrīla Vyāsadeva is called *guru,* or spiritual master, and only such a person is qualified to sit on the seat of Vyāsa, otherwise known as the *vyāsāsana.* The spiritual master is not only the representative of Vyāsadeva, but he is also the representative of the Supreme Personality of Godhead, Śrī Kṛṣṇa.

The spiritual master is worshiped on Vyāsa-pūjā day not because of his ability to speculate or give opinions or fashionable interpretations on Vedanta philosophy. The spiritual master is worshipable because he has perfectly heard, assimilated, and realized the words of his spiritual master, who has, in turn, heard from his spiritual master, and so on, all the way back through the chain of disciplic succession, until we come to Śrīla Vyāsadeva, the author of all Vedic literatures.

Śrīla Vyāsadeva, however, is not an author in the mundane sense that he manufactured something as modern fiction writers write novels. Śrīla Vyāsadeva received knowledge from his spiritual master, Nārada Muni, who received the knowledge from his father. Lord Brahma, the first created living being within the material universe. Lord Brahmā, being the first creature to be born within the universe, was enlightened directly by Lord Nārāyaṇa, or Kṛṣṇa Himself, through the heart. This system of transmitting the Absolute Truth, or the science of God, from master to disciple is called *guru-paramparā,* or the chain of disciplic succession.

The aim of human life is to become perfect by hearing the Vedic wisdom from a bona fide spiritual master in the disciplic succession coming down through Vyāsadeva. It is the duty of a human being to be initiated by a bona fide spiritual master and live his life in accordance with his orders. Even Lord Kṛṣṇa Himself, although the Supreme Personality of Godhead, accepted a spiritual master and learned from him just to teach the people in general that one has to accept a spiritual master to understand God. Śrīla Vyāsadeva, the author of the Vedic literatures, also accepted a spiritual master and learned from him. In this way, we understand that accepting a bona fide spiritual master is the key, or the secret, that unlocks the door for the conditioned soul and frees him from the cycle of birth and death and allows him to return back home, back to Godhead, where life is eternal, full of knowledge, and full of pleasure.

An ordinary human being cannot give us perfect knowledge, because a conditioned soul is imperfect by four defects. He is working with imperfect senses, he makes mistakes, he is subject to be illusioned, and he has a propensity to cheat others. The spiritual master is above the four defects of the conditioned souls because he is enlightened and simply delivers the message of Kṛṣṇa as he has heard it from his spiritual master in disciplic succession, just as a postman delivers a letter from a friend in a far-distant place. The postman does not write anything personally, but he delivers what has been written by our friend. In a similar way, the spiritual master acts as the emissary, or the representative, of God. As such, the disciple worships the spiritual master with the same awe and reverence with which he would worship the Supreme Personality of Godhead, especially on the appearance day of the spiritual master, which is known as Vyāsa-pūjā.

The qualifications of the bona fide spiritual master are that he has perfectly heard the conclusions of Vedic literatures, he can explain them to others, he also abides by those teachings, he is perfectly situated in *brahma-nistham,* in Absolute Truth, and he is coming in the line of disciplic succession from Kṛṣṇa.

The conditioned life of the living entity in the material world is compared to a forest fire which is raging out of control and cannot be checked by any human endeavor. Only if there is a downpour of rain can the forest fire be extinguished. Similarly, the cycle of repeated birth and death cannot be stopped by any material adjustment such as science, medicine, economics, politics, and so on. But the cloud of the mercy of the bona fide spiritual master can extinguish the blazing fire of material existence, namely birth, death, old age, and disease. Therefore, the first business of the disciple is to embrace the lotus feet of his spiritual master and always depend on his mercy. The spiritual master is not bringing his own concocted mercy, but he is bringing the mercy from the ocean of mercy, Kṛṣṇa. Kṛṣṇa is known as the ocean of mercy, and the spiritual master draws from that ocean of mercy, just like the sun evaporates the water from the ocean and forms it into a cloud which then distributes the water all over the land without discrimination.

According to authoritative *śāstras,* the spiritual master is supposed to be nondifferent from Kṛṣṇa, just as a leaf is nondifferent from a tree. A leaf is as good as the tree, but the leaf is only part of the tree. And as the leaf gets its strength and nourishment due to its intimate connection with the tree, similarly the spiritual master also gets his strength and mercy from his intimate connection with Kṛṣṇa. Because the spiritual master is so intimately connected with Kṛṣṇa, he is therefore considered to be the mercy incarnation of the Lord. The spiritual master never says that he is the Lord, but that he is the servant of the Lord. In this way, the spiritual master acts as an instrument of the Lord for delivering the conditioned souls from the cycle of repeated birth and death.

Our beloved spiritual master. His Divine Grace A. C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda, is indeed the modern-day Vyāsa, because he has broadcast the message of *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* and *Bhagavad-gītā* to the whole world through the medium of books, and thus he has opened the door to liberation for the entire world. As Śrīla Prabhupāda became great because of his translation of *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam, Bhagavad-gītā,* and other Vedic literatures, his disciples will become great by their success in the distribution of his books to all people of the world in every town and village on the face of the earth. All glories to Śrīla Prabhupāda!

Haṁsadūta Swami

## Introduction by Harikeśa Swami

It is the opinion of some devotees that Śrīla Prabhupāda was a *śaktyāveśa-avatāra* who descended on this earth to fulfill the mission of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu by bringing Kṛṣṇa consciousness to the West and by creating this ISKCON Society, which is meant to spread the holy name of the Lord to every town and village in the world. Certainly this opinion is most logical and will stand throughout time as more and more people become familiar with the books of Śrīla Prabhupāda and hear about his glorious activities while he lived with us on this planet. The activities of Śrīla Prabhupāda are like a transcendental symphony orchestrated at the highest level by the supreme maestro, Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu. In all symphonies there is a major theme initially stated and expounded within the context of the work, which is surrounded and embellished by minor themes. These subordinate themes do not stand alone within the context of the symphony and are totally dependent on the major theme. Factually one may say that these subordinate themes are created and controlled by the major theme, which is again created and controlled by the composer. The major theme is subordinate to the control of the composer, who has created many minor themes for the glorification of the major theme. These themes work complimentarily with the motif while maintaining a pleasing order within the composition. The same composer will often use the minor theme as a basis for the creation of other symphonies or smaller works whereby they also become major themes embellished by other subordinate themes, which are also appreciated by listeners.

In the context of our symphonic example, let us understand more deeply the obvious and implied meanings of the word *śakty-āveśa.* Broken down into its composite parts, the literal definition of the word *śakty-āveśa* is *śakti,* "energy," and *āveśa,* "empowered by." One only speaks of *śakty-āveśa* in terms of a part and parcel of the Supreme–a living being. And therefore one who is *śakty-āveśa* is a personality who is empowered with the energy of the Supreme. In other words, Śrīla Prabhupāda was empowered with the energy of the Supreme Lord and thus he was able to perform wonderful works while he appeared before us. We may also judge the absolute nature of that empowerment by seeing the ongoing result of his works–the ever-expanding manifestations of his energy.

When we look at the implications of such an understanding, we must think about it deeply, lest we fall into some sentimental trap. We should not think that Śrīla Prabhupāda could do what he did because he was an empowered incarnation who descended on this world for our benefit and leave it at that. Of course, the statement that he is an empowered incarnation is a fact, but this is not the major theme which was stated by the life's example of Śrīla Prabhupāda himself, neither was this theme ever emphasized within his books. Rather, ws see from the life of Śrīla Prabhupāda a more real and relevant example for each and every one of us to follow.

The example which I personally take from the life history of Śrīla Prabhupāda is not at all dependent on his functioning as a *śaktyāveśa-avatāra* within this world. In fact, I personally find such a definition of Śrīla Prabhupāda as a disturbance in my relationship with him. Naturally, I am emphasizing the fact that this is my personal opinion, just as it is our personal opinion that Śrīla Prabhupāda was a *śaktyāveśa-avatāra.* But in order to further churn the ocean of the nectar of devotion, I would like to put aside that point in favor of a more important point. I consider the life of Śrīla Prabhupāda to have a *greater* meaning than that implied by his being a *śaktyāveśa-avatāra* from birth.

Śrīla Prabhupāda himself stated the source of his personal potency many times, both publicly, privately, and within his books. He did not wish to hear about his being a *śaktyāveśa-avatāra* and neither did anyone ever dare to mention such a thing during his lifetime. I don't think I would have liked to have been the one who would have mentioned that he was born an *avatāra* of the Lord, although now that I think back on it, offering such a statement in love would have certainly brought some kind of internal pleasure to Śrīla Prabhupāda as it demonstrates the highest regards and respect for one's spiritual master *(bandoṅ mui sāvadhāna mate),* although externally he might have chastised the praiser as being without scriptural intelligence. Why we never thought to mention such a thing to Śrīla Prabhupāda is not so easy to understand. But I think that he never allowed us to think beyond the platform which he wanted us to think on in relationship with him, for he was most concerned with our own spiritual advancement.

Śrīla Prabhupāda never told us who he was spiritually, neither did he ever consider that knowledge as important for his disciples. He never encouraged attempts to extract such confidential information from him, and when we once did, he even said, "You would never believe it," thus ending a tense moment of expectation when we thought that he might answer our inquiry. Why didn't he tell us? What was the harm in it? We would have increased our loving devotion to him even more, wouldn't we? Actually–I don't think so. Therefore Śrīla Prabhupāda never encouraged such attempts, neither did he ever cultivate the mentality within us. Other so-called *svāmīs* or *yogīs,* all desiring fame and distinction, hint at and blatantly say that they are incarnations and *avatāras* of all types and descriptions. But their artificial attempts at glorification often end up with them wiping the pie cream off their faces when someone determinedly proves their mystique to be another public-relations trick.

What exactly is the meaning of the word *empowered?* First of all, one may be empowered *before* taking his birth in a particular place or one may become empowered due to fulfilling certain qualifications after birth within particular situations. When one is empowered before his appearance, he is sent within this world to fulfill a certain function and his success is already guaranteed and his glories are already set and determined by the Supreme. If one is not empowered at birth but rather fulfills a certain set of prerequisite qualities during his life, he may become empowered by the mercy and grace of the higher authorities, namely *guru* and Kṛṣṇa. Can we say that becoming qualified within one's lifetime to accept the energy of the Supreme is already determined by the Supreme before one's birth? Or should we say that it is simply a function of the desire and activities of the living being who qualifies himself during life? Actually, no one can answer this question but Kṛṣṇa Himself, who might reveal the situation in each particular case as He likes. We cannot speculate and reach an absolute conclusion. Of course, speculation is possible, and sometimes, as in the case of our attempt to understand the glories of Śrīla Prabhupāda, it is actually inspiring our love for him, but it still remains speculation. Can anyone definitively state that he is or is not *śakty-āveśa?* Yes, we can, on the authority of the statement in the *Caitanya-caritāmṛta, kṛṣṇa-śakti vinā nahe tāra pravartana.* Without *kṛṣṇa-śakti,* no one can spread the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement throughout the world. From this we can understand the actual position of Śrīla Prabhupāda. It is definitely decided that he is empowered with *kṛṣṇa-śakti.* Then, one might ask, "What is the meaning of all these statements and what is the need of your symphonic example?" Good question, and we shall clarify immediately.

I personally do not think about these points, but I saw something disturbing occur due to the discussion of these topics. I heard someone say, "Śrīla Prabhupāda was a *śaktyāveśa-avatāra.* Therefore he did what no one else could ever do. Neither can we ever hope to do anything like it. Therefore why should we endeavor to attempt to follow in his footsteps? Simply we should follow the path of unobtrusive devotion and be satisfied with whatever little we attain." I thought there was something wrong with this. Why do we place Śrīla Prabhupāda in a position whereby we disqualify ourselves from following his instructions and similarly becoming empowered? Śrīla Prabhupāda wanted us to follow his example and attain preaching success. He never put himself out of our reach at any moment.

Let us consider the word *empowered.* It means that someone acts in such a way that he becomes empowered. We may also understand that someone can become causelessly empowered, but this is not relevant in the example of Śrīla Prabhupāda. The example of Śrīla Prabhupāda screams at us from the rooftops at peak volume: "Act in such a way as to become empowered!" Śrīla Prabhupāda himself said hundreds of times, "My only credit is that I perfectly followed the instructions of my Guru Mahārāja. I had faith in his instructions. I never cheated my Guru Mahārāja. I always depended on the mercy of my Guru Mahārāja. I knew that although I was unqualified, still by his mercy I could become qualified."

*mūkaṁ karoti vācālaṁ paṅguṁ laṅghayate girim*  
*yat-kṛpā tam ahaṁ vande śrī-guruṁ dīna-tāraṇam*

**"By the mercy of one's spiritual master a fool can recite poetry, a lame man can cross over mountains ..."**

What is the meaning of being empowered within this world? I see it as nothing more than becoming the perfect servant of one's spiritual master. Can anyone do this? Yes, certainly. Why not? Does this mean, if I am not empowered from birth, that I too can attain a very great height within this world simply by surrendering to the lotus instructions of my spiritual master? Yes. But as long as you are thinking of attaining some height or glory within this world, that height will remain a long way off. Just surrender yourself totally, and whatever comes, whatever energy the Lord wishes to grant you, this is to be used in the performance of your service to fulfill your mission. What is that mission? Following the orders of your spiritual master. Then you will definitely become empowered to spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness all over the world.

It is in this way that I like to meditate on Śrīla Prabhupāda. I like to think of him as the only one who perfectly surrendered his body, mind, intelligence, time, energy, and deepest heartfelt desires to the lotus instructions of his spiritual master. And thus, because he simply attempted to do this, all perfections of life and all successes of spiritual achievements came to him as his maidservant. I don't like to think, or rather, I don't like to meditate on Śrīla Prabhupāda as a descended incarnation, for this impairs my vision. But I most eagerly embrace the form of Śrīla Prabhupāda getting on that steamship fourteen years ago, undergoing many hardships and heart attacks on that trip to America, feeling alone and sometimes discouraged, even by his own Godbrothers, yet steadfastly maintaining the desire to fulfill the desire of his spiritual master.

And thus when the Supreme Lord desired to see his efforts become fruitful, He granted Śrīla Prabhupāda the opportunities he so prayed for, for the satisfaction of his Guru Mahārāja. This form I like to see. Let me always remember and meditate on this form of my spiritual master, as he always prayed to and meditated on the same form of his spiritual master, who revived the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement in India and empowered his perfect disciple to carry the mission to the Western world. This method of not being satisfied until one's spiritual master is satisfied is the method I wish to follow.

It is all right to think, now and then, that Śrīla Prabhupāda was an empowered incarnation of the energies of the Supreme, but somehow that thought cannot find any resting place within my mind. I don't see its relevance, neither do I find that this is in accordance with the way in which Śrīla Prabhupāda wanted us to think of him. I remember many times he said the secret of his success was his simple attempt to follow the instructions of his spiritual master, and thus everything manifested almost automatically. Certainly there were hard times and hard struggles, but simply by maintaining that steadfast attachment to the lotus feet of his spiritual master, Śrīla Prabhupāda overcame these difficulties and attained the success he so much deserved.

Therefore I see the major theme in the symphony of this Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement as the *constant attempt* of Śrīla Prabhupāda. The composer of this symphony is none other than the supreme merciful Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, and the leader of the orchestra is Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Prabhupāda. One may argue that Śrīla Prabhupāda himself is the leader of the orchestra, yet I think that Śrīla Prabhupāda himself would disagree. What is the constant attempt at satisfaction of the order of one's Guru Mahārāja without the order and direction of one's Guru Mahārāja? Certainly the orchestrator of Śrīla Prabhupāda is his Guru Mahārāja, and this is neither diminutive to nor undercutting the absolute position of Śrīla Prabhupāda's glory. Rather it is strengthening and supporting his position and the position of his movement as well.

Surrounding this theme, which is stated and restated in every phrase of this great symphony, is the creation of Śrīla Prabhupāda: ISKCON, the BBT, and all his disciples. There is no part of this symphony where the theme of Śrīla Prabhupāda's attempt is not to be found. Prabhupāda attempted to start ISKCON, and it happened; and he maintained it throughout all moments of economic crisis, or personality crisis, or crises coming from the demoniac elements of the world, like a father who is protecting and nurturing his children in his home while going out every day and doing his own work. Śrīla Prabhupāda created each and every aspect of ISKCON, as well as the BBT, while writing so many books, which was his main work, and expanding the preaching by his words, which we learned, copied, cherished, and heard again and again simply to cleanse our consciousness in order to be able to emulate the attempt of Śrīla Prabhupāda to attract more and more souls to this movement. He attempted to create farm communities, and suddenly they arose. He attempted to distribute books, and although we had no idea what to do, soon the *saṅkīrtana* was expanding and doubling every year. He attempted to build big, unbelievable projects in India while battling with demons, government, and even disbelievers within his own ranks. And always he would succeed. EVERYTHING THAT HE ATTEMPTED TO DO SUCCEEDED. This is the most wonderful thing about Śrīla Prabhupāda. He attempted, and thus he always succeeded.

Therefore the attempts of Śrīla Prabhupāda are the main theme which supports and forms the basis of this symphony. Therefore I say that it is orchestrated by Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Prabhupāda. Śrīla Prabhupāda had no subordinate themes to orchestrate. He had to create everything himself, for we were mere incompetent babes in the hippie woods of ten to twelve years ago, and therefore he took the position of the support and form of this ISKCON movement, which will forever be dependent on and subordinate to the themes created by Śrīla Prabhupāda. He is forever intrinsically within this ISKCON movement. It is not possible to separate the creation of our spiritual society from Śrīla Prabhupāda, who at every moment remains the structure and framework of this society.

If someone a little lazy hears about the position of *śaktyāveśa-avatāra* in relationship with Śrīla Prabhupāda, he somehow manages to reduce the glories of his achievements as simply due to the inexplicable mercy of the Supreme and not directly related with the qualifications of the empowered person. The lazy man thinks, in other words, that although one may be empowered, all glories go to the empowerer, for he who is empowered is merely a tool in the great plan of God. How one gets empowered is a point which is neglected by those who are lazy, for they would rather be satisfied with their own acquired qualities. And thus satisfied, they sit back and perform what "little" service they can, thinking that to expand beyond this point is only possible for those who have become inexplicably empowered from above. But such lazy fellows miss the whole point of the example of the life of Śrīla Prabhupāda and thus the whole point of how one attains the mercy of his *guru* and Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu. It is not a foregone conclusion that one devotee will succeed in his attempt at spreading the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement because he is empowered, while another will fail because he is not empowered. This is foolishness. Rather it is an already determined scriptural conclusion that *anyone* who attempts to satisfy his spiritual master by spreading the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement throughout the world will succeed and will definitely become empowered to do so if he simply steadfastly and determinedly maintains the desire to satisfy and please the spiritual master and Kṛṣṇa, and he constantly *attempts* to succeed in spreading this movement.

Again, let us understand this most important word *attempts,* for success does not depend on some formula. It is not that one simply places himself in some situation and the result automatically comes. Just going somewhere, sitting down, and casually going about one's daily business of everyday Kṛṣṇa consciousness will not inspire the Supreme to empower him. Rather, one must get himself placed in the situation, either by choice or by direction from above, whereby one can best attempt to fulfill the desires of his spiritual master. This attempt means that he must *actively* strive to do so. Not by attempting to gain fame, profit, or distinction, but by attempting to deliver the message of Kṛṣṇa into the hearts of the fallen souls and attempting to expand the facilities of the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement in spite of all seeming inabilities or discouragements.

I always remember Śrīla Prabhupāda's insistence on buying the land at Juhu in Bombay, although the entire of ISKCON at that time had no money to spend on the project. I think back to how Kṛṣṇa arranged the money to come almost miraculously through the back door. It was being collected elsewhere for another reason, and just as Prabhupāda needed it, it was available, sent, and the land was purchased. I know Prabhupāda had no idea where the money would come from to pay for Hare Kṛṣṇa Land, but we never really felt that he was ever actually fearfully worried about it coming. Somehow he knew that this was what Kṛṣṇa wanted and that Kṛṣṇa would also send the money in due course. He did. Therefore, let us become empowered by getting off of our comfortable behinds, which are cozily situated in the opulent framework set up by Śrīla Prabhupāda's attempts, and let us attempt further to fill in the vast spaces of missing ISKCON. The whole world is like a vast empty space, the missing parts of this great ISKCON movement. One should see everything within this world as a temporarily disconnected piece of the ISKCON puzzle and relocate this piece within the total ISKCON picture.

In the great symphony, the main theme is surrounded by subordinate themes which embellish. These smaller themes or subordinate motifs are often extracted from the grand jewellike masterpiece motif and examined on their own. The major theme is a complete masterwork when it is surrounded by its servitor themes. And the whole affair can be compared to a jeweled locket, magnificently decorated and set within a broach. If one minutely examines the intricate details of the embellishments, he will further appreciate the wondrous appearance and construction of the entire jeweled piece. In a similar way, the subordinate motifs are expanded within the symphony and form most exquisite, melodic, and rhythmic patterns on their own, although they are never factually existing on their own, but are always related to the main theme of the work which acts as the base upon which all other themes are resting. Always in the fore and behind the subordinate theme one can hear the major motif setting the pace and position of all other themes.

Śrīla Prabhupāda has already set the main theme, while the subordinate themes are still developing under his direction. Those who are following in the footsteps of the *mahājanas (mahājano yena gataḥ sa panthāḥ)* are properly developing a subordinate theme, although the symphony is so vast that no one can really tell where the main themes end and the subordinate themes begin. They are so similar in nature, although slightly different intonation, that one will think that these subordinate themes are simply slight variations of the main theme, instead of understanding that they are embellishments surrounding the center pace-setting motif. Actually, is it at all important to try to make such a discrimination between the motif and submotif at this point? Is it possible to make the distinction at any point? I doubt it, although those who are subordinate in their turn to the now subordinate submotif will think in the future that this presently subordinate submotif is actually the centerpiece of its own great symphony. Certainly only a fool would argue with such an opinion; for he cannot understand that this symphony is eternal and that all melodic themes spring from the eternal source of all creation, Śrī Kṛṣṇa, and therefore there is simultaneous oneness and difference manifesting throughout the whole creation and all its spiritual theme.

In other words, when someone catches on to this concept of following in the footsteps of the *constant attempt* of Śrīla Prabhupāda, then he becomes qualified to become empowered as Śrīla Prabhupāda was empowered and as he wanted all of us to become similarly empowered. This empowerment is not meant for an elitist organization, although it might become one because of so few souls' being able to properly comprehend the mission of Śrīla Prabhupāda and even fewer following it in the same spirit of bold and fearless love and devotion. When one catches on to the flow of mercy emanating from the lotus feet of Śrī Kṛṣṇa as it is manifesting through the agency of Śrīla Prabhupāda, then he will become empowered as the subordinate theme of the great symphony of the supreme maestro, Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu. The main theme of this symphony is simply being further understood and elaborated within this short introduction.

This symphony is being created by Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu. Sometimes there are different leaders, servants of the Lord, who are carrying on the main theme, and some who are expanding the theme in length and breadth by performing this symphony on different occasions throughout the world. Each generation has a new maestro conducting the orchestra, training the musicians as they strive to develop the subordinate theme and capture the natural mood and the ecstasy of the music for the listening pleasure of the whole world. Presently Śrīla Prabhupāda has simultaneously taken the position of the supporting form and major theme of this ISKCON composition, and he remains the orchestrator as well. Soon, as is his transcendental desire, he will manifest solely as the thematic composition and supporting structure of the symphony, while those he has chosen to orchestrate the composition will continue to weave an ever-tightening net of transcendental influence over the ever-widening world of Kṛṣṇa conscious hearers.

Please excuse the necessity for so much analogy, but the sensitive reader can understand the meaning behind such guarded statements. Any one of us, the instruments of Śrīla Prabhupāda, the players within the orchestra, may become empowered by him to play his symphony as he likes. When such playing is heard, no one will be able to distinguish whether it is the composer, the conductor, or even the musicians themselves who are playing the exquisite melody, for it is not possible to separate the roles of these different members of the musical creation from the music itself. No cultured enjoyer of this very special symphony would attempt to do so, for a learned man will simply sit back and hear the flow of nectar emanating from the assembled players and will respectfully appreciate the genius of all involved in the transmission. Those who are less intelligent will sit back and criticize the work, although they themselves have no qualification to compose, conduct, or play. They cynically choose to disregard the enlivening musical effect for the sake of making sharp critical judgment, and thus such personalities will never appreciate the ambrosial, symphonic flow of bliss. Such critics may only appreciate their own foolishness, which never satisfies them, although it may increase for thousands of years.

Those who are actually intelligent are a part of the orchestra or a most respectful and qualified hearer of the symphonic creation. Or if one tries to take another position, then he misses the whole point of the show. From the spiritual point of view, there are parallel positions, for one must be either always attempting to increase the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement, or one must be respectfully helping those who are working to increase the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement. Do not try to take a third position, for that position is simply dedicated to the frustrations of *māyā.* When one takes up one of these two most satisfying positions, then the distinction (or necessity to make distinction) between the accomplishments of the different members of the orchestra is rendered meaningless, and one simply appreciates the glorious effect. (But please note that one always maintains a greater respect for the present orchestrator and former orchestrator.) If we try to separate ourselves from the group attempt, then we will simply cause a disturbance, for the orchestra stands together only. Although solos are intrinsic within the performance they never stand outside of the group setting.

If one is playing a lead, the most important qualification of the other musicians is how nicely they can support the lead to further enhance the theme he is portraying. Whenever the opportunity arises for one to take the lead and develop the theme further, all others should give their wholehearted support, mentally, physically, and verbally, until that time when another rises to further the cause, when the focus and support should be shifted to him. If we see how the attempt to spread this glorious movement should be just like this well-forged and most perfect musical synthesis, then we will be able to quickly take the position as empowered personalities working for the satisfaction of the Supreme Lord.

At the other side of our analysis, we can further understand that despite all attempts to propagate this Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement, no one will be able to surpass the most wonderful and glorious attempt of Śrīla Prabhupāda, who remains always the honorable master of this creation. But if we can work as empowered parts of his powerful creation, then we can further expand the glories of Śrīla Prabhupāda. If we perform his master work with great care and dedicated technique, then this will glorify Śrīla Prabhupāda more than ever before, just as the intricate setting work glorifies the main jewel in a crest. It will be most glorious for Śrīla Prabhupāda when this ISKCON Society is spread throughout the world, and the achievements of its founder- *ācārya* are well known by every man, woman, and child.

Harikeśa Swami

## Śrīla Prabhupāda-praṇati

*gaurāṅga-śikṣā-vitatiṁ tu bhūmyaṁ*  
*śrī-bhaktisiddhānta-suvallabhaṁ vai*  
*govinda-nāmāni vikāśayantaṁ*  
*śrī-bhaktivedāntam ahaṁ namāmi*  
*Kṛṣṇa-Upadeśa dāsa (Age 10)*

**Śrī Bhaktivedānta-mahimā**

*āmodā yat-prakaṭita-karunāt kṛṣṇacandrasya līlā-*  
*sevā-vārtā-malayaja-marutaḥ paścime deśa-vṛnde*  
*māyā-tāpa-pralaya-śubha-karā mukti-dāyāś caranti*  
*tasyādseve caraṇa-kamalaṁ bhaktivedānta-nāmnaḥ (1)*

**I serve the lotus feet of His Divine Grace A. C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda. Because of his mercy, the delightful, aromatic, and cooling Malayan breeze of the service, pastimes, and message of Lord Kṛṣṇacandra is now moving over the Western countries. That breeze destroys the distressing heat of material existence and grants liberation \[to the conditioned souls\].**

*śrī-kṛṣṇa-saṅkīrtana-yajña-vahnim*  
*prahlāda-kośaṁ kuśalaṁ janānām*  
*pāścātya-deśe samadīpayad yas*  
*taṁ bhaktivedāntam ahaṁ namāmi (2)*

**I offer respectful obeisances to His Divine Grace A. C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda, who is igniting the fire of the** ***saṅkīrtana-yajña*** **in the Western countries. That fire is the source of happiness and auspicious-ness for the living entities.**

*kṛṣṇa-nāma -puṣpasāra –dhārā*  
*mlecchajeṣu vāci moda-vṛndā*  
*yat-kṛpā-cayena kāśitā taṁ*  
*māmakaṁ guruṁ namāmi nityam (3)*

**I offer repeated respectful obeisances to my spiritual master, whose great mercy has caused the delightful honey of the holy name of Lord Kṛṣṇa to be tasted by the tongues of those born in** ***mleccha*** **families.**

*vara-syāmākāro nikhila-jana-nāthaḥ para-tanur*  
*jagannātha-svāmī nipatita-yavānāṁ bhuvam aho*  
*kurukṣetrāt tīrthāt parama-karuṇenādi-guruṇā*  
*smito yenānītaḥ prabhupadam ahaṁ naumi satatam (4)*

**I repeatedly praise my spiritual master, His Divine Grace A. C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda. Just see, he has mercifully brought Lord Jagannātha, whose smiling transcendental form is wonderfully blackish, and who is the master of all living entities, from the holy place of Kurukṣetra to the countries of the fallen** ***yavanas.***

*advaita-vallari-kulaṁ kila nāśayitvā*  
*tarkāsinā viṣa-tatiṁ hari-bhakti-vṛkṣān*  
*modena ropayati yaḥ kuśalena bhakti-*  
*vedānta-daivatam ahaṁ satataṁ nato 'smi (5)*

**I repeatedly offer respectful obeisances to His Divine Grace A. C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda. With the sharp sword of logic and argument, he destroys the poisonous creepers of the Māyāvāda philosophy. He jubilantly and expertly plants the trees of devotional service to Lord Hari \[in the hearts of the living entities\].**

*pāścātya-deśa-cala-yauvani-markaṭāḥ śrī-*  
*govinda-bhakti-para-tattva-vido bhavanti*  
*kāruṇya-mantra-vacanaiḥ paśu yasya bhakti-*  
*vedānta-daivatam ahaṁ satataṁ nato 'smi (6)*

**I offer respectful obeisances to His Divine Grace A. C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda. Just see, by his merciful instructions he has turned the young people of the Western countries from bewildered jumping monkeys into devotees who understand the transcendental truths about devotional service to Lord Govinda.**

*ramya-bhāgavata-parvata-rājaḥ*  
*sat-purāṇa-paramaḥ śruti-modaḥ*  
*sat-stavāvali-sudhā-sarid-okaḥ*  
*kṛṣṇa-rūpa-maṇi-kandara-vṛndaḥ*  
*nandasūnu-śubha-keli-vicitra-*  
*vela-bhūṣita-susundara-śṛṅgaḥ*  
*yena paścima-bhuvaṁ pratinītas*  
*taṁ namāmi satataṁ prabhupādam (7 - 8)*

**I offer repeated obeisances to His Divine Grace A. C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda, who brought the delightful and regal mountain of** ***Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*** **to the Western countries. That eternal mountain is the best of the** ***Purāṇas*** **and the most delightful of all Vedic scriptures. It is full of rivers of nectar which are the prayers of the pure devotees, and it contains innumerable caves full of jewels which are the descriptions of the beauty of Kṛṣṇa. That mountain has an exquisitely beautiful summit which is decorated with colorful gardens which are the auspicious pastimes of Lord Kṛṣṇa, the son of Nanda Mahārāja.**

*śrī-caitanya-mahāprabhor vara-dayā-nyagrodha-vṛkṣo mahān*  
*māyā-tāpa-vināśanaḥ surabhitaḥ sad-vargiṇo nandayan*  
*pāścātyaṁ bhuvam āvṛṇoti sahasā tanvan mahā-maṅgalaṁ*  
*rohan pāramahaṁsa-maṇḍala-pateḥ śrī-bhaktivedāntataḥ (9)*

**His Divine Grace A. C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda is the leader of the** ***paramahaṁsas.*** **Because of him, the great banyan tree of the mercy of Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu is now quickly covering the Western countries. That banyan tree is very fragrant, and it removes the burning sufferings of material existence. That tree delights the saintly devotees and creates great auspiciousness.**

*govinda-śruti-kīrtanāmṛta-sarit-prakhyāta-manādkinī*  
*nītā paścima-bhūtalaṁ vraja-purāt saṁsāra-sarpāntakā*  
*kṛṣṇa-prīti-saroja-puṣṭi-karaṇā saubhāgyadā yena taṁ*  
*santaṁ bhaktimatāṁ patiṁ mama guruṁ seve sadā sādaram (10)*

**With great respect I engage in the service of my saintly spiritual master, the leader of the devotees. He brought the famous, nectarean celestial Ganges River of the hearing and chanting of the glories of Lord Govinda from Vraja-pura to the Western countries. That greatly auspicious river nourishes the lotus flower of love of Kṛṣṇa and kills the snakes of continued material existence.**

Kuśakratha dāsa

## Vyāsa-Pūjā Homages from the G.B.C.

## Ādi-Keśava Swami

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept my most humble obeisances at your lotus feet, which alone can give shelter to the fallen souls of this Kali-yuga age and which are the dearmost object of any devotion we might have.

You are somewhere with Lord Kṛṣṇa now, yet at the same time you have so expertly managed to be present here, continuously guiding us as the founder-*ācārya* of ISKCON. The pure devotee of Kṛṣṇa is, in his own minute way, like Kṛṣṇa Himself. So, just as Kṛṣṇa is very near and very far away as well (as the *Īśopaniṣad* describes), you also are simultaneously with us and beyond this material world. You are like the Supreme Personality of Godhead Himself in that you can resolve all paradoxes within the transcendental nature, and if we can learn to understand this fully, surely we can remain in your association eternally.

The paradox is not only how you can simultaneously be with us and also have left us to be with Kṛṣṇa, but how you moved in this material world in the first place, stirring the material energy again and again with the transcendental vibration of *hari-nāma,* yet always remaining at Kṛṣṇa's lotus feet. Your expertise was to always remain situated in complete transcendence, without once dipping into the mire of material sense gratification. This appears even more paradoxical when we note that although you glided through the material world–a swanlike angel beyond reproach–at the same time you struggled and fought like anything to establish ISKCON under the most difficult of all conditions. Thus the concept of your transcendence is not poetic metaphor but proven fact, for your travail was very real, full of all the pains and trials that the material energy could muster.

Such activities of the *paramahaṁsa* in the mundane world also seem paradoxical to the material mind. Some might try to compare you to clerics and sages of bygone ages and the present day, who have often undertaken welfare work in the name of saving the world. However, whereas such philanthropists invariably become sullied by the filth of material existence, you, being the greatest of all varieties of swanlike entities, have not. Although your preeminence was not recognized by the vast majority of the inhabitants of this planet–recognition that would surely have benefitted one so rapt in the *saṅkīrtana* movement–your devotees will always carry your name on the crest wave of that transcendental surge.

You gave us the aphorism *tad-vijñānarthaṁ sa gurum evābhigacchet:* "To understand the Absolute Truth, one must approach a transcendentally realized *ācārya."* And when we approached you, you didn't give us only theoretical knowledge of the difference between matter and spirit. No, you clearly demonstrated your knowledge that this world is a nasty hell from any angle of vision–unless one can see Kṛṣṇa everywhere. And because we knew of your struggle and sacrifice to restore the spiritual world in the middle of this hell, we were convinced of the absolute veracity of your assertion.

Although I can express so many intense desires to preach or fulfill your will, actually I must admit that I have not yet overcome even the most simple and mundane of all problems. What to speak of overcoming death and attachment to the body, it is even hard for me to remember Kṛṣṇa when I have a headache from endless hours or days of smoothing out the ripples in the material energy which might otherwise impede the progress of the *saṅkīrtana* movement, or from climbing on and off too many airplanes as I run from place to place trying to enthuse your disciples and granddisciples to preach. I would like to know what it means to be surrendered to Kṛṣṇa, as you are, and I suppose I am asking to find out. But unfortunately, I am caught in the world of relative time and the affairs of a world bound in the chains of *karma.*

And with all this going on in the foreground, in the back rooms of my mind the thought is constantly gnawing that I don't love you enough, even though trust in you has been my only shelter for many years. By your mercy I have awakened a little affection for Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Govinda, but I cannot see Them as you do. You would speak of Śrīmatī Rādhārāṇī in such a loving way, expressing tender concern for Her health or appreciating Her satisfaction with Her *bhaktas'* service. What is that vision of such an intimate confidante of the Supreme Personality of Godhead as yourself! You have realized the full ecstasy of the pleasure groves of Vṛndāvana and the glories of the Supreme Lord's most intimate association.

The point is that, if I am permitted to say it, you alone can give me Kṛṣṇa. You have created the spiritual world of ISKCON–an oasis in the midst of the material desert, which you have filled with the cool ponds and lakes of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Indeed, by your desire Kṛṣṇa has taken birth within the womb of this movement. You always reminded us how Kṛṣṇa was present in all the activities of the *saṅkīrtana* movement and how you could see Him. Through the cracks and seams of the material veil I might see Him as well, but I can also see that you gave your mercy to those who took your orders so seriously and who loved you so utterly that Kṛṣṇa has revealed Himself to them in all His radiant glory. I want to say that I *demand* to see Him, but I know that that is senseless, for your divine mercy is there for anyone who will take it. It is simply that this foolish son has not yet been willing to open up his darkling eyes.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are accustomed to the company of the greatest *sādhus* and demigods, whose purity and substance are finer than any imaginable within the mundane brain. None of the gold or gossamer of this material world can impress one such as you, who has played with toys of *cintāmaṇi* in the eternal youthhood of Vṛndāvana-dhāma and explored the labyrinthine glories of the palatial Dvārakā fort. Because you have relished the *rasas* and emotions of spiritual love in that original source beyond creation (compared to which this universe is an insignificant, dull space), no complex mechanistic or impersonalistic philosophy, even if possessed of all embellishments of speculative scholasticism, can offer you more pleasure than would a journey through the desert under the blazing midday sun. Since you don't succumb to the folly of lapping up the alkaline water of such desert-dry academic watering holes, one can only imagine (with the help of your kind instructions) what kind of intense relish you have eternally enjoyed.

Yet somehow or other you have induced us to participate in this *saṅkīrtana* movement of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, and even the grossest fool among us has experienced the great pleasure of divine nectar. Through this *saṅkīrtana* movement you have given us an un-stemmed tide of ecstasy–a tide so powerful that it has overflowed the banks of our mundane desires, seriously undermined our belief in the capacities of the material mind, and firmly established a reservoir of faith in the ultimate goal of love of God. This one thing, by your grace, I can express not just by rote but by realization and experience. You are teaching realization, and that realization is to be found within the *saṅkīrtana* movement. And because you have given this great realization to the most fallen among all men, you are the most glorious of all the servitors of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu.

I feel guilty about having taken up your time, even for this long, so I shall return to my original point. I want to learn to love you and serve you unceasingly, whether I be in heaven or hell, because I know that wherever you are served, there also is the spiritual world, eternally.

I am hoping that you will consider my earnest request. Eagerly awaiting your reply, I remain  
*Your most fallen servant,*  
Ādi Keśava Swami

GBC–New York, Boston, Philadelphia, Puerto Rico, Trinidad, Guyana  
Co-GBC–Bhaktivedanta Institute

## Atreya Ṛṣi dāsa

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

I pray and beg you to remain in my memory and let me realize more of your fearless and forceful role within the reality of your transcendental mission.

Following your footsteps, let me remain committed to fight ignorance without relenting to profit or adoration. Let me not resign myself to the comfort of what I know and not 'forget what a great fool I am, remaining thirsty to receive a few more drops of your ocean of knowledge and mercy.

Push me to imitate you in your fearlessness, becoming convinced that I am not this lump of ignorance and that ignorance cannot destroy the real me. With your words, let me break the ideal of the modern fantasy– the scientific socio-economic-political systems–and replace them with the absolute science of *varṇāśrama-dharma.* With your compassion, let me melt the impractical, sentimental, and romantic hearts of my fallen brothers and engage them in this eternal, blissful process of *saṅkīrtana.*

O Prabhupāda, what a great joy and ecstasy you have brought to me. Please do not let me die.

*Your worthless servant,*  
Atreya Ṛṣi dāsa

GBC–Iran, Saudia Arabia, Turkey, Amman, Yemen, Afghanistan  
Co-GBC–Syria, Jordan, Kuwait, United Arab Emirates, Bahrain, Pakistan

## Balavanta dāsa

Wandering aimlessly, hopelessly waiting,  
no one to turn to, our strength was abating.

Darkness around us, perils unfolding,  
scholarship was futile, success not worth holding.

Sex life was fleeting and friendships not true,  
this earthly life was so empty and spoiled without you.

Scientific triflers, their verbal persuasions  
could not bring the soul to its true destination.

Yogic impostors were having their day,  
bewildering the people to lead them astray.

Skyscraper buildings and bright motorcars,  
their streets filled with prostitutes, restaurants, and bars.

They say it's advancement, but what is the goal,  
to gain the whole world and forget the soul?

Shouldn't millions of years on evolution's hard path  
have taught us to conquer our lust, our greed, and our wrath?

Our human life wasted, downtrodden, and poor,  
without you, who would have come to open life's door?

Lighting this dungeon, a new hope for tomorrow,  
you brought us a fresh life, an end to our sorrow.

A kind saintly person, a sagacious stern father,  
hard as a thunderbolt, soft as a flower.

Your pink lotus feet gave us the pride  
to take up this mission with you by our side.

Then life had its purpose, its meaning and mission,  
to live as your servants, to save this poor nation.

We had no other need, no other condition,  
to satisfy you was our only ambition.

That taste was so sweet, the best nectarean,  
that you'd be proud of your sons was our most cherished dream.

Your Vaikuṇṭha grin, your glistening eyes,  
could melt the whole world and fill up the sky.

Then all of a sudden, in your own unique way,  
you vanished from sight, thus darkening the day.

This universe empty, this planet quite barren,  
morose and unhappy, the joy's gone from heaven.

No light shown in the darkness, the world torn asunder,  
the jackals were howling, the skies filled with thunder.

O inauspicious life, now gone is the thrill  
that once filled our hearts, replaced by a chill.

But low a voice now deep inside is felt  
to gladden the heart and cause it to melt.

The mission is still there, and now the path's clear–  
hold fast to his message, the master is near.

Like Kṛṣṇa's surabhis and His Vṛndāvana home,  
he knows all our names–we're never alone.

Pushing, revealing, chastising, and talking,  
he's with us each moment, while sleeping or walking.

In the spiritual world he still hears the charts;  
he slipped out of our vision but into our hearts.

O happy day, so glorious and clear,  
he's with us forever, we now have no fear.

Wherever we go, he's right by our side,  
our master, our shelter, our light, and our guide.

We've nothing to hanker, for us no disaster,  
we've always got Prabhupāda, our spiritual master.

All glories to His Divine Grace A. C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda.

Balavanta dāsa

GBC–Miami; Gainesville; New Orleans; Atlanta; Anderson, South Carolina; Murari Sevak; Mississippi farm  
Co-GBC-Colombia, Ecuador

## Bhagavān Goswami

The sun traverses its course in the sky–  
Appearing and disappearing,  
Light then night,  
Hope then fright,  
Now no longer in our sight,  
Your absence becomes unbearing.

As Śrī Viṣṇu injects the spirit soul  
And brings life to dead matter,  
By your glance dead bodies danced,  
Your mercy was our only chance,  
Now no longer in our presence,  
What painful end to my existence!

Bell metal is turned to gold  
In the hands of one who knows,  
You changed so many fallen souls,  
The world can see,  
You delivered me,  
Now placed as part of Caitanya's tree,  
Only by your mercy can there be  
The energy to do as thee.  
The world had hardly knew you came,  
So humble you were–no desire for fame,  
You came, with Kṛṣṇa's name.  
The world will never be the same,  
The movements of your lotus feet,  
So sweet to Vaiṣṇavas when they meet,  
Fearful to those who want to cheat,  
Now no longer touch this earth,  
To end repeated death and birth.

If you had not come how would we know  
The reason for life and the ultimate goal,  
Bound by the modes with no moral codes,  
Our idea of perfection–a perverted reflection,  
Our ticket to hell–with confirmed reservation.  
Our Lord and master  
Saved us from disaster,  
How is it now that you have gone  
When our young love begins its dawn?  
The Supreme Lord Kṛṣṇa–  
Our well-wishing friend,  
Whose will determines beginning and end,  
To teach us surrender,  
Brought you to Him,  
To teach us love,  
Gave deep separation,  
To give us hope,  
Left your instructions,  
Thus even though you are far from this place,  
We can always live with Your Divine Grace.

Bhagavān Goswami  
Initiating *guru*

GBC–South Europe, Israel, Mauritius, Canary Islands  
BBT Trustee

## Bhakti Caitanya Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādī-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*My dear, most eternal father, Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

I beg you to accept my offering of Śrī Vyāsa-pūjā, which is like a candle offered to a great sun.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, I am very fortunate to have your association. O my lord, you are a *śakty-avatāra,* an empowered incarnation of Lord Kṛṣṇa Himself, appearing only for the benefit of the most fallen souls. Your Divine Grace descended from the spiritual sky on this auspicious day to protect us from the influence of *māyā.* We have incurred a debt that can never really be repaid, but I think this debt is a great joy for me. I am throwing myself on your lotus feet and asking for your mercy to make me able to please you with this body, mind, and words.

As a master and lord, Śrīla Prabhupāda, you know everything. In the past I could not carry out your orders because I was in ignorance. But now, by your causeless mercy, I have regained my memory and am prepared to act according to your divine instructions. Please bless me so that I can adopt the qualifications of a devotee, beginning from "kind to everyone, does not quarrel with anyone, fixed in the Absolute Truth, faultless, clean, peaceful, mild, simple, equal to everyone, completely attached to Kṛṣṇa," etc.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, your plans for Kurukṣetra are coming true. Now the people are coming and begging us to build a temple in the middle of Brahma-sarovara, the site about which you were always asking. Now it is in your hands. If you desire, please place your feet on my head so that I can carry out your desire.

You have given me the highest taste of *kṛṣṇa-prema* by awakening my consciousness through the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mahā-mantra.* O my lord, I am praying at your lotus feet to keep me engaged always as I am now.

*Bhāgavatam* has confirmed that in the age of Kali, men have but short lives, they are quarrelsome, lazy, misguided, unlucky, and above all, always disturbed. But out of compassion toward these fallen souls, you have taught us how to deliver them by giving the simple and practical formula of the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mahā-mantra.* By taking a *Bhagavad-gītā* in one hand and a bead bag in the other, you have traveled all over the world–which no one has ever done in history. All glories to Your Divine Grace.

I have always heard from your holy lips, *guru-mukha-padma-vākya cittete koriyā aikya.* Your instructions to me are always in my mind. Instead of making an offering on this auspicious day, I am coming to beg your mercy. Please pardon me all the offenses I have committed in the past. Now give me the strength to remain pure, and allow me to assist you in completing your mission.

Thanking you, and *koṭi koṭi daṇḍavats.* Śrīla Prabhupāda.

*Begging to remain your servant forever,*  
Bhakti Caitanya Swami GBC–Pakistan, Chandigarh, North India

## Bhavānanda Goswami

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Falling down in the dust of your divine lotus feet, I offer my most humble obeisances a thousand times and a thousand times again and again, in the front, from behind, and all around.

When the living entity comes into contact with the material nature, he is immediately placed in a situation of fear. As long as he maintains any enjoying spirit or desire to enjoy outside of Kṛṣṇa, that fear is his constant companion. One who takes shelter of the lotus feet of Your Divine Grace, which are situated in the fearless dust of Vṛndāvana-dhāma, that conditioned soul is given an opportunity to revive his natural position as servant of Lord Kṛṣṇa and become filled with eternality, knowledge, and bliss.

That I am able to even speak this basic truth of Kṛṣṇa conscious philosophy is due to your compassion. In 1970, in Los Angeles, you said, "The quality of mercy is not strained. It droppeth as a gentle rain from heaven." Similarly, you have exemplified this by bestowing your mercy upon such an unqualified wretch as me. I understand that I have no personal qualification for being allowed the opportunity to serve you. Simply good fortune, that is all. Please, I beg of you, help me to develop appreciation and love for you, and allow me to always be engaged in some little service for Their Lordships Śrī Śrī Gaura-Nitāi.

Falling again in the dust of your divine lotus feet, I simply remember when you called out to me one night in Vṛndāvana, "O Bhavānanda, don't ever leave me. Stay with me forever." My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, please don't ever let me go. Keep me with you forever. I beg to remain,

*Your most insignificant servant,*  
Bhavānanda Goswami Initiating *guru*

GBC-Vṛndāvana, Central India  
Co-GBC-Māyāpur, Michigan

## Brahmānanda Swami

We take this opportunity of the most blessed event, the appearance day of His Divine 'Grace A. C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda, founder-*ācārya of* the International Society for Krishna Consciousness, to reaffirm our unswerving devotion to his mission of spreading Kṛṣṇa consciousness all over the world. We are convinced that only by Kṛṣṇa consciousness can all the world's unending problems be solved on an individual and collective basis. His Divine Grace Śrīla Prabhupāda was so expert that he introduced Kṛṣṇa consciousness so that we who are so unqualified could understand it, and he mercifully gave us his association, even though by our blunders we caused him inconveniences. Now he has entrusted us to carry on this great task of saving the world, which has gone mad due to excessive sense gratification.

I pray for his mercy to enthuse me with the determination to act as he acted, not to imitate in a cheap way, but to follow in the footprints of his divine lotus feet to purify myself and then to purify others, so that pure love of God, Kṛṣṇa, will manifest in the heart. I then will be able to again reunite myself in his divine association in the spiritual world, far beyond this material world of pain and pleasure, where he eternally resides, participating in pastimes of bliss, with Kṛṣṇa, the Supreme Personality of Godhead. Śrīla Prabhupāda was always very kind upon me, so I am sure that he will not deny me this, my heart's desire. Who else is there who is such a powerful transcendental personality, who can take the most unqualified and elevate them to the kingdom of God? Therefore, I worship His Divine Grace Śrīla Prabhupāda and never tire of speaking of his glories to anyone or everyone I meet. I pray for the day when the world will come to recognize the exalted position of Śrīla Prabhupāda and join with me to bow down to the lotus feet of His Divine Grace Śrīla Prabhupāda, and also to his successor *gurus,* because on that day the world will be saved from its certain present hellish course of godlessness and suffering.

In my opinion, Śrīla Prabhupāda is understood to be an *āveśa-avatāra,* an incarnation of the Lord, as is proved by the following verse:

*set jīve nija-bhakti karena prakāśe*  
*tāhāra darśane 'vaiṣṇava' haya sarva-deśe*

**"Thus He empowered living beings \[His pure devotees\] by manifesting in them so much of His own devotion that people in all other countries became devotees by seeing them." (Cc.** ***Antya*** **2.14)**

Śrīla Prabhupāda is not an ordinary person like us, but fully endowed with the powers of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu. It is my great fortune to be his eternal servant.

Brahmānanda Swami

GBC–West Africa, North Africa, Central Africa

## Gopāla Kṛṣṇa dāsa

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

I beg to offer my most fallen obeisances at your divine lotus feet a thousand times. You are the bona fide representative of Śrīla Vyāsadeva, and hence you are entitled to receive the Vyāsa-pūjā offering every year. You appeared on this planet to establish Kṛṣṇa consciousness to save the conditioned souls, and as soon as a strong foundation was established, you decided to disappear from our eyes. This is a proof of your renunciation. Śrīla Prabhupāda, your appearance and disappearance is similar to that of Lord Kṛṣṇa. It is always *divyam–*transcendental. One of your Godbrothers correctly stated that you were a *śaktyāveśa-avatāra.*

**"We think that we have met Your Goodness by the will of Providence, just so that we may accept you as captain of the ship for those who desire to cross the difficult ocean of Kali, which deteriorates all the good qualities of a human being."** ***(Bhāg.*** **1.1.22)**

It was due to the causeless mercy of Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa that a fallen soul like myself met you in Montreal in June 1968. Completely illusioned by the influence of the age of Kali, I had no understanding of the mission of human life or that Lord Kṛṣṇa was the Supreme Personality of Godhead, though I had taken birth in Bhārata-varṣa. Prior to hearing you, I was regularly visiting religious institutions, but none gave information like you did regarding the goal of human life. The entire purport of the *Vedas* was conveyed by you in a simple manner so that fools like myself could understand the difference between *asat* and *sat.* I remember how briskly you would walk from your apartment on Prince Arthur Street to Park Avenue temple in Montreal to lecture every evening. Your were ever willing to answer questions about Kṛṣṇa and the mission of human life.

Being a pure devotee of Kṛṣṇa, your concern was to enliven fallen souls like myself. This was your mercy. You knew that without devotional service, human life is lost. From the moment I first saw you, I have only heard you say to everyone, "Be Kṛṣṇa conscious, be a devotee of Lord Kṛṣṇa." Unlike other *gurus,* you never charged a fee or asked for personal presents. In your preaching efforts you met so many opposing elements, but you were not disturbed; you were a real *sādhu-titikṣaḥ.* I remember once in July 1968, after you finished giving a lecture at a Christian school in Montreal, an Indian man criticized you in public for preaching Kṛṣṇa consciousness to Christians! The man was very insulting to Your Divine Grace. However, you did not get disturbed, and a few days later you invited this Indian man to your apartment and even blessed him.

The present age is very dangerous for the human being. The whole system of education is geared to sense gratification. Any learned man can see that the children are being sent to the slaughterhouses of so-called education. Had you not preached boldly and forcefully, there would have been no hope for the suffering humanity. Now there is hope. You worked day and night to establish over one hundred Kṛṣṇa conscious centers around the world, wrote over seventy books, and left behind thousands of recorded lectures. This *vāṇī* of yours in books and tapes is the only real treasure. At the moment it may be appreciated only by your disciples, but a day will come when these books will be the lawbooks of this planet. This was predicted by you; therefore it must happen. Already we can see positive signs in this direction. A recent Gallup poll showed that one percent of the teenagers in America accept Kṛṣṇa consciousness as their religion.

With the passing of every day, Śrīla Prabhupāda, I become more and more convinced that this Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement is the only hope for suffering humanity. Out of your great compassion for the conditioned souls who are struggling to cross over the darkest region of material existence, you explained the message of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu. In the name of religion, people are teaching irreligion. By your purity you were able to convert a crowlike person like myself into swanlike. In the material world, the swans have become crows. However, you have stated that "the reestablishment of *dharma* will gradually turn the crows into swans." Just like Lord Caitanya was able to convert tigers, snakes, deer, and other animals into devotees, you have converted an animal like myself into a devotee of Lord Kṛṣṇa. This is your miracle.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, at your feet all masters sit. You were like a touchstone; whoever came in contact with you left being favorable to Kṛṣṇa. I owe you a great debt. This debt is impossible to pay. Even if I serve you for millions of births and deaths, I will not be able to pay back even a fraction of this debt. You gave me the matchless gift–Kṛṣṇa consciousness. On this auspicious day, I pray for your causeless mercy so that I may be able to hold on to your matchless gift eternally and help convert crowlike men into swanlike men. Only this will please you.

*Your most insignificant disciple,*  
Gopāla Kṛṣṇa dāsa

GBC–Canada, Bombay, Gujarat  
Co-GBC-New Delhi  
BBT Trustee

## Haṁsadūta Swami

1\. You came like a thief in the night,  
Awakened us and then took flight,  
Left behind you your divine light  
Which reveals everything to our sight.

2\. Independence is only a myth.  
In the Vedas the Lord has said  
An account is kept of each breath.  
With every birth is born our death.

3\. Whatever we do is seen  
By God behind the screen  
Of nature's enchanting scene  
Which is no more real than a dream.

4\. You rejected everything that was dear,  
Such as gas, food, wine, and beer.  
At the time it seemed really queer,  
But now all that you have taught us is clear,

5\. Prabhupāda, my eternal guide,  
Taking everyone to the other side  
Of time's rising and falling tide,  
In your mercy 1 pray to abide.

6\. Kṛṣṇa took you away,  
It seems just like yesterday.  
Perhaps what I am trying to say  
Is that I think of you always each day.

7\. I am lowborn, foolish, and mean,  
Yet I can't help trying again  
To compose a short refrain  
In glorification of your holy name.

Your humble servant,  
Haṁsadūta Swami

Initiating *guru*; BBT Trustee  
GBC-Berkeley, Seattle, Portland, the Philippines, Taiwan, Sri Lanka, Malaysia, Singapore, Korea  
Co-GBC-China

## Harikeśa Swami

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please eternally accept my most humbled obeisances at your most glorious lotus feet. Thousands of glories showered upon you. Please help me to properly glorify you.

Your day began sometime between 12:30 and 1:30, after you inexplicably rose from bed without any assistance. There was no alarm clock, and certainly no one came in to remind you that it was time to rise, but almost as if you were never asleep, you arose of your own accord to sit behind your desk and chant the holy name. Perhaps one hour or more later, after sitting peacefully without moving, chanting in complete concentration, you opened your books, the red Bengali *Bhāgavatam* and the green one from Vārāṇāsī with the Sanskrit commentaries by the great *ācāryas.* You turned on the dictaphone, picked up the microphone, and began dictating... " *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* Seventh Canto, Third Chapter, verse 15, purport continued." And then, with great care and deliberation, you chose each and every word as if the whole creation depended upon the perfectness of your word selection. Factually it did, for you knew that for the next ten thousand years these books would be the standard of all knowledge.

You understood your audience; you knew their minds perfectly well, although perhaps you were in Bombay, Sydney, Rome, Tokyo, Hawaii, Los Angeles, New York, or any one of the thousands of places you used to visit. Sometimes those places would be very small, with only three or five devotees and a handful of members, or very large, with hundreds and thousands all waiting anxiously to hear you speak even a drop of nectar. You would never disappoint them, giving them a full share of your mercy. They would come to your room all day, and even all night, but you would never neglect them or push them away. Often you would chastise me, "Why do you prevent them from coming? Let them come!" And I, knowing that the great hardship which you willingly desired to place yourself in was a labor of love, had no power to try and protect you. However, in these wee morning hours, no one could even think of rising, what to speak of coming upstairs to bother you. Certainly we were all sleeping within the lap of *māyā,* fully covered by ignorance, while you forged the standard books of knowledge for the future.

You could read the minds, feel the hearts, and see the faces of the people you were writing for, and with great compassion and mercy you chose each word most deliberately, to elicit the reader to lose his material desires and come again into the spiritual realm of devotional service. Perhaps today you would do two verses, perhaps twenty–it was up to your own transcendental desire. Sometimes two verses took longer than twenty to compose, for this was not a material occupation, dependent on a mundane time schedule. Often we would try to speed you up, foolishly thinking that you needed encouragement to go faster, but you would chastise us, "Do you think that this is an ordinary activity? It is not simply moving the lingers on the typewriter. I have to compose these words with great deliberation. This is the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* it is not an ordinary activity." And thus we would once again feel like fools for trying to interfere with the transcendental emotional outpouring of your ecstasies within the purports.

The purports were your emotional ecstasies, you told us, and they remain your emotional ecstasies still. So filled were you with pure love of God that your love remains ever fresh today in the form of these purports. Anyone can have direct contact with your lordship by reading your purports and accepting the instructions within as their life and soul. Even if someone reads one line, even one word, as you have told us, they can become completely changed in heart and eligible to go back home, back to Godhead. Even if they touch the book or see it, their lives are changed. Who could have imagined the wonderful, magnificent opulences of your books, and who could have conceived that during these wee morning hours you would be there, dedicatedly writing your books, day in and day out? Although sometimes you were extremely sick, you would still manage to write. It is certainly a modern wonder how in ten years you managed to write more books than anyone else in history, in so many different languages.

So many different books, yet the materialists are so bewildered they can't even understand what you have done. Later on they will understand, when the seeds of these books fully manifest as trees. Now they are simply seeds–maybe a few have sprouted to become small creepers–but when the seeds manifest fully into trees, people will understand what you have done. These millions of seeds are everywhere and are the unbreakable, indestructible, eternal assets of the human society. How that manifestation of mercy will appear in the future only your lordship knows, but we can guess that we actually have little idea of what can and will develop simply from the mass distribution of these books.

After maybe two, three, or even four hours of the most intensely concentrated writing activity ever experienced in the history of mankind (I used to listen at the door, relishing every word as it went into the microphone, knowing full well that later on I would have to type it. I am trying to learn how to capture this mood and put it forever in my heart for safekeeping. That moment is eternal perfection, and were it possible to again create that moment, I would eternally recreate it into ever-present existence. However, bankrupt as I am, without any spiritual potency, I am simply trying to catch a glimpse of that moment in order to remember your holy lotus feet.), you somehow decided to come to an end. The translating day is finished, even sometimes in the middle of a sentence, inexplicably, for no one can understand from where the nectar came flowing forth, or why it stopped.

Now the flow and tide of your transcendental ecstasies were being channeled into other directions. Sometimes you were interrupted by a sudden guest, or a devotee who required counseling, for you would personally manage hundreds of lives simultaneously, as well as manage the entire Kṛṣṇa consciousness society, full of its problems and crises, without flinching. You actually never wanted all these headaches, but you took them upon yourself out of the great responsibility you felt toward the Supreme Lord and your Guru Mahārāja to expand, maintain, and spread the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement all over the world. You took this responsibility willingly, although it was intensely difficult, because overcoming these and other intense difficulties, such as the overwhelming difficulties you faced when you first came alone to preach in America, was the manifestation of the supreme greatness of your preaching triumph.

If no one disturbed you, perhaps you would catch a twenty-minute nap, recovering from the great mental strains of writing. Then the time just before dawn would come, and all *sannyāsīs,* Vaiṣṇava temple presidents, and other important devotees would assemble at your door, breathlessly awaiting your departure from your room, while your servant hurriedly tried to organize the morning clean-up crew–who would change the sheets, sweep the floor, wash whatever they could, and replace the flowers. At the same time, your servant would be carefully trying to place your cap on your head, your sweater and *chāḍa* over your shoulders, your lotus feet firmly and easily within your lotus shoes, and your cane directly into your hand, without you having to even break your stride. The perfection was to do it in one sweeping motion, while holding open the door so that you would not waste one moment of your precious time as you strode out of the room, a great, yet ever-so-gentle giant, who had just solved all the problems of the world within a few simple lines of *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam.* The crowd of devotees would immediately fall on their faces, and you would stand there patiently, while you were duly garlanded and praised with nice words. Perhaps you would laugh or look around at us sternly according to your own desire, then off you would march on your morning walk to once again tear to pieces the rascal scientists and foolish atheists of this world, while constantly showering your most merciful compassion upon all your devotees.

I think, Śrīla Prabhupāda, your morning walk is eternal. It is going on somewhere, and I wish to go on that morning walk to become one of the eternally grown men who fall on the floor like a stick, with their faces in the dust of your lotus feet. I wish to laugh with you at the slightest provocation of your transcendental humor and meditate on your raised eyebrows arched high to amplify a most perfect point of logic, or run like a madman at your command to fetch a twig or leaf. I wish to listen with ears straining to catch your lotus voice in a crowd of thousands as you lead the world onward toward the spiritual perfection of life. I wish to always be on that morning walk and to worship that walk as one worships his very breath.

Please, Śrīla Prabhupāda, grant me the wish to remain ever fully surrendered and dedicated to your holy lotus feet, to be always hearing your words rising within my ears, and to be always inspired to repeat those words for the benefit of all mankind. Please help me follow your instructions without deviation, to perfectly hear and chant the holy names of the Lord, and please enable me to perfectly please you with my service, so that you will not think that you have wasted your time with this fallen and wretched self.

I am simply waiting to receive this mercy like a faithful dog outside your door, waiting to go on the morning walk. I am still waiting till this day, and I will not leave until you come out. I am very patient.

I have nothing else.

(It is not even my words which I use to praise you on this Vyāsa-pūjā day. Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, thank you...)

*Your most foolish and humble servant,*  
Harikeśa Swami

GBC–Scandinavia, East Germany, West Germany, Switzerland, Austria, Hungary, Poland, Czechoslovakia, Yugoslavia, Bulgaria, Rumania, USSR, Lebanon, Egypt  
Co-GBC–Syria, Jordan, Kuwait, United Arab Emirates, Bahrain

## Hari Śauri dāsa

1\. Through all the time I've been in here,  
this mortal temporary world,  
Through all the different changing scenes,  
I've never enjoyed at all.

2\. I couldn't laugh, I had no joy, there wasn't any taste;  
my hopes, my dreams, my clever schemes,  
Kāla laid them all to waste.

3\. As the sun came up and crossed the sky  
and brought the end of day,  
My life dragged on, and on, and on,  
with death the price to pay.

4\. With no future seen, an unknown past,  
a present oh, so grim,  
I struggled for some meaning,  
to get some light within.

5\. It wouldn't come, the knowledge sought,  
I couldn't control my mind.  
On my own, a useless being,  
my intelligence was blind.

6\. Fully roped to material life,  
by three gunas I was bound.  
Ignorant, passionate, with very little good,  
my thoughts were not profound.

7\. I had no hope to improve my lot  
by any means of mine,  
but within my heart the Lord was there,  
His presence so sublime.

8\. Taking mercy on me,  
to set me free from my hellish existence,  
Lord Kṛṣṇa knew just what to do  
to make me sing and dance,

9\. His pure devotee, sent by Him,  
had already sung the song,  
a song of hope, a song of joy,  
a song for everyone.

10\. Flowing from his lotus lips,  
freeing Kali-yuga 's hoard,  
the mantra that Śrīla Prabhupāda gave  
was nondifferent from the Lord.

11\. Hare Kṛṣṇa, Hare Kṛṣṇa, Kṛṣṇa Kṛṣṇa, Hare Hare  
Hare Rāma, Hare Rāma, Rāma Rāma, Hare Hare.

12\. Śrīla Prabhupāda came and picked me up,  
he swept me off my feet.  
He gave me release from māyā’s attack,  
his knowledge was complete.

13\. Prasāda, kīrtana, Bhāgavatam,  
so many matchless gifts;  
I taste life eternal,  
he healed up all the rifts.

14\. "You're not that body, you're spirit soul,  
atomic part of the Supreme.  
Living in this material world  
is simply just a dream.

15\. "But it is not that it is false,  
it has its base in reality.  
Something doesn't come from nothing,  
it's like the reflection of a tree.

16\. "The shadow comes from substance,  
otherwise it couldn't exist.  
Your relationship is not with that,  
on a shadow you can't subsist.

17\. "Likewise this world of people and things  
is real but temporary;
