it has its origins in the spiritual sky,  
where things exist eternally.

18\. "And the Supreme Truth is a person,  
Kṛṣṇa is His name,  
He has all knowledge, strength, beauty, wealth,  
renunciation, and all fame.

19\. "By chanting this mahā-mantra  
you can appeal unto the Lord  
to be engaged in His service  
and be freed from all discord.

20\. "The Lord is kind to a surrendered soul,  
He brings them back to home.  
Through different planets and species of life  
you'll no longer have to roam."

21\. So, after having tried it,  
I'm feeling full relief,  
And just like Śrīla Prabhupāda  
I feel I want to preach.

22\. He's given such a wonderful gift  
that no one else could give.  
My debt to him I can't repay,  
So now my life is his.

**A Prayer at His Lotus Feet**

Please, Prabhupāda, make me strong,  
Keep me always at your feet.  
The memory of your kindness  
brings māyā to defeat.  
Make me laugh, make me cry,  
do with me as you will;  
the memory of your smiling face  
remains my greatest thrill.  
I've met no one as great as you,  
no one can compare,  
you are my lord and master,  
keep me free from māyā’s snare.  
You laughed, you danced, you sang your song,  
now you're hidden from my sight,  
but by transcendental knowledge  
you filled my heart with light.  
Please keep me with you,  
wherever you go,  
please keep me serving you,  
for you're the only one I love–  
I have nothing more to do.

*From your poor-hearted, useless servant,*  
Hari Śauri dāsa

GBC–Australia, New Zealand, New Guinea, Indonesia

## Hṛdayānanda Goswami

Let Śrīla Prabhupāda be glorified  
by the execution of his mission!

Hear his divine order!  
By vigorously preaching  
the science of Kṛṣṇa,  
the mind is fixed at his lotus feet  
and love of Godhead awakens.

Selfless service is the sign of love.  
Let those who would glorify Śrīla Prabhupāda  
cast off their great pride  
and serve selflessly his lotus feet.

Let those who would follow him  
also cry. Be merciful!  
Isn't your heart also breaking  
when you see the fallen people?

To serve our eternal father  
let us boldly declare ourselves  
servants of his servants  
and pridelessly serve them, as he wanted.

How ecstasy increases! How the heart melts!  
Serving the lotus feet of his servants!  
I am a rascal and a fool,  
still I try to please him.

O Vaiṣṇavas throughout the world!  
Hear my humble request at your feet–  
Raise your arms in ecstasy–  
Dance and dance to the big sound  
of his universal mṛdaṅga: Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam!  
Dance to the sound of his order!  
By vigorously preaching  
the science of Kṛṣṇa,  
the mind is fixed at his lotus feet  
and love of Godhead awakens.  
I see my Godbrothers  
like Pāṇḍava princes, waiting  
for the world salvation, by  
his mercy and the will of Kṛṣṇa.

Hṛdayānanda Goswami

Initiating guru  
GBC–Brazil, Uruguay, Argentina, Bolivia, Peru, Chile  
BBT Trustee

## Jagadīśa dāsa

**Trying to Deserve Śrīla Prabhupāda's Mercy**

My own intelligence cannot  
Perceive His subtle sacred scheme.  
His aim to sever the hard knot  
That ties me into māyā’s dream.

Ensconced in nescience thoroughly,  
This fallen soul cannot get out,  
But by his grace and charity  
It's possible. There is no doubt.

Lord Kṛṣṇa, please enable me  
To have the resolute desire  
To crush my sinful tendency  
And do whatever he'll require.

My most demented, mixed-up mind,  
Misled by māyā’s tempting lures,  
Suggests that I become inclined  
To seek for fame and paramours.

There is no other hope for me  
Than service to his lotus feet  
In full surrender faithfully.  
Then only will I feel complete.

Jagadīśa dāsa

GBC–Minister of Education

## Jayapatāka Swami

**The Mission**

The Kingdom of God your residence, Your Divine Grace came,  
went West to save the fallen.  
The hell in the West my birthplace–  
Your Divine Grace ordered me:  
“I have given you the kingdom of God–now develop it!”  
Now, by your divine grace, my residence is–  
the Kingdom of God.  
Oceans seven, rivers thirteen we crossed to reach holy Bhārata,  
when coming from the West.  
Your Divine Grace must have brought me for some reason–  
float me, dunk me, as you like,  
Prabhupāda, you are the personified mercy ocean.

**The Goal**

Kṛṣṇa is the Supreme Lord.  
Lord Śrī Caitanya is Kṛṣṇa.

Love of Kṛṣṇa is life's highest goal.  
Lord Caitanya freely gives Kṛṣṇa's love.

Caitanya Mahāprabhu didn't want to distribute alone–  
Śrīla Prabhupāda has come to help Lord Caitanya.

Devotees render service to Kṛṣṇa as highest truth –  
But, higher truth is service to Kṛṣṇa's devotees.  
Lord Caitanya's head aches  
to save the fallen souls.  
Śrīla Prabhupāda came to cure  
Lord Caitanya's headache.

**The Miracle**

Fallen souls with no hope  
found hope in Lord Caitanya.  
We, hopeless to find Caitanya,  
Prabhupāda gives hope to us most fallen!  
Jungle tigers, elephants, and deer,  
hearing Lord Caitanya's chanting,  
dance and cry.  
Your Divine Grace's chanting forces me and all to  
dance and cry,  
although I'm foolish, fallen, and lower than  
the animal of the jungle.

**Hope and Realizations**

Your Divine Grace eternally serves  
Lord Caitanya and the previous ācāryas.  
May Lord Caitanya and the previous ācāryas  
bless me to eternally serve  
Your Divine Grace.  
Indian culture is interesting to the West.  
Western culture is the infatuation of India.

Asleep, one is woken by calling in his ear.  
But, calling, calling, hard to wake  
the pretender from his sleep.  
Asleep, the Western people woke, hearing  
Your Divine Grace.  
How to wake the Indian people  
pretending to be asleep!?

"Self means the body to an animal,  
though it's not.  
Body is seen as machine  
to one who knows himself.

Work like a beast  
to enjoy pleasures like a hog's,  
Forgetful humans miss their chance  
for self-realization–  
they won't work.

**Fulfillment of Prophecies**

Lord Caitanya 's prophecy:  
worldwide His message will be spread.  
Your Divine Grace  
has given the world  
Lord Caitanya's message.  
The greatest mystic: Lord Kṛṣṇa Caitanya  
can do as He likes!  
Your Divine Grace's blessing  
assures us mercy of  
the greatest mystic.  
Blind man sees the stars,  
lame man climbs the mountains  
by your mercy.  
Your Divine Grace has made the perfect pair–  
placing the lame man on the shoulders  
of the blind man.  
My desire is to see the day  
when everyone will chant and hear  
Kṛṣṇa's holy name.  
When Your Divine Grace requests Lord Kṛṣṇa,  
certainly He will fulfill  
this desire.

**Srila Prabhupada's Greatness**

The ocean of materialistic illusion–  
we had no ship by which to cross.  
Your Divine Grace's books and instructions  
are the ship  
to cross the ocean.  
"Prabhupāda is gone!" some say.  
"We must do it our own way."  
Thus fear and doubt lead them astray–  
for them certainly Prabhupāda has gone away.  
I say, "Prabhupāda is here to stay!".. .  
"To help the sincere in every way!"  
on "Love and Trust" only will ISKCON grow.  
As a coolie, let us carry on our heads  
the holy orders of Śrīla Prabhupāda.

**ISKCON: Prabhupada's Nitya-Lila**

*ISKCON is your body–*  
*nothing can harm it from without!*  
Our fear is of māyā from within–  
*neglecting your vāṇī, harm will come to you: ISKCON.*  
*Higher seat, lower seat, no issue*  
*to bring one closer unto you.*  
*Your mercy's given to those*  
*whose service consciousness is higher.*

Your Divine Grace quotes, "Jei Bhaje... Sei Buro"–  
he who serves and preaches most  
is prominent and praiseworthy.  
You kindly focus us on the task at hand–  
unitedly to deliver the message of  
Your Divine Grace.  
World is full of ears;  
it's the devotees' duty  
to let them hear!  
Not to waste time loitering here,  
living in fear,  
caught in desire's whirls.  
Your Divine Grace's call is clear:  
"Try to double book distribution every year. "  
Failing this, Gauḍīya Maṭha's example's clear.  
Let us carry out your orders,  
not build castles in the sky.  
You're calling us near,  
Your Divine Grace.

**The Prayer**

Reason can't reveal  
the purpose for  
Your Divine Grace  
bringing us.  
Only we are faithful  
that Your Divine Grace  
has brought us  
for a reason!  
Conditioned souls are woodlike–  
they must be lit  
by divine grace.  
When will Your Divine Grace  
also ignite me  
to light conditioned souls?  
Glories! Glories to Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa Caitanya!  
May the whole world sing His holy name!  
Glories! Glories to Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
who has spread Lord Caitanya's name and fame!  
Let us increase Śrīla Prabhupāda's fame  
by passing out his holy books!  
Let us bring the whole world  
bowing to the lotus feet  
of Lord Caitanya  
at His birthplace, Māyāpur,  
to sing and hear His glories!  
The Jaya Patākā – victory flag,  
without Your Divine Grace  
is dead,  
like a flag without a pole!  
Only by your divine grace  
may this most fallen of fallen souls  
unfurl amongst the Vaiṣṇavas  
to serve them  
as (Kṛṣṇa's) Jaya Patākā!  
Vyāsa-pūjā comes once in every year  
to remind us of Your Divine Grace  
and bring you very near.  
Now the times are fast changing,  
the world is merely hell.  
In your service we find solace;  
we want nothing else at all  
except also to remember you  
at Vyāsa-pūjā.

Jayapatākā Swami

Initiating *guru*  
*GBC-East India, Nepal, Bangladesh, Sikkim, Orissa, South India*  
*Co-GBC–Māyāpur, Assam, Tripura*

## Jayatīrtha Mahārāja

*My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Kindly accept my most unworthy obeisances at your divine lotus feet. You come to me in a thousand ways to guide me, to solace me, to chastise me. I hear your voice within gently speaking to me, and then laughing, with head tilted back as in days gone by. And I hear you also speaking to me through my Godbrothers, through my disciples, even sometimes through the nondevotees–exposing some aspect of the truth more clearly so as to aid my realization. You speak to me through your books, your tapes, your articles–making your simple points time and time again, so that they may not be forgotten. Then again I find a new revelation never before seen in these pages, and I wonder how it is that I had never noticed it in my previous readings. Your gifts to us are limitless and, as the first storefront said, "matchless." Matchless Gifts. And you are still giving, and I, shamelessly, although having received far more than I deserve already, am still accepting more and more, and I can sense that your giving will never stop.

In light of all that you have given me, I wonder what I can offer you on this Vyāsa-pūjā day. Simple expressions of praise will not do. A mere offering of fruits and flowers will also, I fear, prove empty. On reflection, I think that the only presentation worth making will be that of a world more purified by your message. Each year we should offer at your lotus feet a world more Kṛṣṇa conscious that before, in which an ever-greater number of souls have been exposed to your teachings and are in possession of your books. Then again, we should offer you an ISKCON society in which there is an ever-greater display of purity and strength, of preaching vibrancy and harmonious cooperation between devotees. And again, we should each offer you a heart more attached to the principles of *bhakti,* less motivated by selfishness and contaminated by sinful desires–a heart in which only the pure desire for service remains and in which the vision of transcendence has ever more profoundly manifested itself. Such offerings will, I think, be worthwhile.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, please be kind upon me! Remove the steel frames from my heart, remove the proud bearing from my demeanor and the envy from its deep root in my nature.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, please be kind upon me! Just as Kali-yuga is an ocean of faults, so am I, and just as it has only one good trait– *kṛṣṇa-kīrtana–so* your mercy is my only good feature.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, please be kind upon me! You have kept me with you for so long–please do not give up on me now and cast me aside, although I am so uncooperative.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, please be kind upon me! Just give me one more drop of your mercy so that I will take your orders to heart and, rejecting all contrariness, become your unconditioned servant.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, please be kind upon me! Your separation weighs heavy upon me as I write, and I find all substitutes to be vacant. Please take this sad and lonely Jayatīrtha dāsa and make him your foot-servant.

Jayatīrtha Mahārāja

Initiating *guru*  
GBC–Great Britain, South Africa, East Africa, Detroit  
Co-GBC-New Delhi, Chandigarh, Michigan  
BBT Trustee

## Kīrtanānanda Swami

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Let me offer my humble obeisances to you again and again. You have brought to an end our futile search for sense gratification and have given us eternal, blissful engagement in the service of Kṛṣṇa. Liberation is found in carrying on your mission.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, we always remember the times when you blessed our community with your presence. You purified this land with your footsteps. Your presence here can be perceived by anyone who visits your Palace. As you once told us, "I am already living here because you want me to." Now everyone who comes sees that it is true.

Once when you were inspecting the Palace, one of your disciples asked if we were going to illuminate the Palace with jewels. You replied, "These devotees are my jewels." The spiritual master cannot be satisfied by some show of opulence. He cannot be purchased by a mountain of gold. His mercy, like Kṛṣṇa's, is only obtained by love and devotion.

However beautiful we make your Palace, it is an insignificant tribute to your glory. Whatever we speak in your honor falls far short of describing your greatness. No matter how many people we tell about you, we fail to increase your fame. Still, we have firm faith that by our humble endeavors we can somehow please you, and in so doing perfect our lives.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, our only desire is that you allow us to continue building your Palace, birth after birth, wherever we may be.

Your most fallen son,  
Kīrtanānanda Swami

Initiating *guru*  
GBC–New Vrindaban, Cleveland, Columbus, Erie, Pittsburgh

## Pañcadraviḍa Swami

*My most dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept my obeisances at your most soft lotus feet. All glories to your most compassionate Divine Grace. Were an hour to slip by, and that hour spent without thinking of Your Divine Grace, then I would consider that hour to be likened unto a fiery demon intent upon devouring my devotional life.

The rivulets of eternal time flow incessantly to the sea of devotional service, and when we enter into that sea we find the waters to be warm and soothing, and that you are there. You float on the waters of devotional service in a boat made of your instructions, and like an all-knowing captain, you graciously bid us aboard. The favorable breezes fill the sails, and as we ply our way onward, you read to us in your soothing voice from the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam.* And sometimes you coax us to read–"Pañcadraviḍa, read from the Second Canto"–as you occasionally expand the purports, giving your commentaries that fill the skies and go beyond the ten directions.

What is the meaning of existence if it is not in relation to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda? Such existence without thinking of you is like a hollow echo which laughs in our face. As a dumb animal fallen into a blind well, I cry out for help to my friends, my family, my loved ones, my society, to everyone and everything I have ever known. But my cries for help only dissipate in the wind, and the wind whistles through the lonely trees, reminding me that I am alone. But when I cry out to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are there. And you extend your hand and gently guide me to Kṛṣṇa's house. There are sweetmeats, and the Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa Deities on the altar glisten in transcendental effulgence. In Kṛṣṇa's house there is safety and shelter, something unknown in this material world. And we sit down and take *mahā-prasāda* together, taking *sabjī* with hot *purīs,* and you laugh as you tell us how your father used to buy all the vegetables that the *wālā* had and explain to your mother, "I got a very good price." And you smile at us with your eyes open very wide and tell us that we are all "damn cheap *bābās."* "You come to a place and you question, 'How much is this?' And the man says it is this much, and you say, 'Ah, that is damn cheap.' But you don't know that he has raised the price for you five times. But you are thinking it is 'damn cheap.' " And you laugh.

In Māyāpur we sit in your room, and from the temple below we can hear the *sandhyā-ārati,* with the *kīrtana* throbbing and the bells ringing so beautifully–sounds which eternally sound in the spiritual world. With you in your room in Māyāpur there is no time, no beginning or end. Everything is so pleasant, the air blows into the room, soothing us like nectar, and you lean back and challenge, "If there is something missing, simply some chemical lost from the body at the time of death, then why don't they inject that chemical and bring the body back to life? And if it is simply some chemical change that has occurred, then why do so many living organisms, worms and parasites, come out? If the body can no longer sustain life, then why do so many living entities thrive on the body after the body dies? Can they answer these things?" And trying to present some argument, I say, "Well, they say that just as before Henry Ford designed the automobile, there were theories but no one had been able to make a motorcar, in the same way they say that they have not yet been able to create life, but they are working on it." And you laugh heartily, saying, "Well, until that Henry Ford comes forward and creates life, until that Henry Ford makes a living entity, then let them keep quiet. When that Henry Ford makes a living entity, then let them speak." In this way, you shove the haughty modern science into the back seat of a Ford motorcar, and that car sputters off, never to be seen again, into the depths of the Ganges River. But just as the Ganges water can never be contaminated, so you remain so wonderfully Kṛṣṇa conscious, whether you are destroying the demon of unwarranted, blind faith in the scientists or reading to us from *Caitanya-caritāmṛta.*

As your train pulls away, I run down the platform beside your car. And you smile and wave, seeing that we are trying to keep up with you. And your train pulls away, drifting away further and further into the distance. Soon the train is gone, out of our sight. We fall flat, offering our obeisances to you. I look around at my Godbrothers, and everyone is entranced, remembering our association with you. Everyone is smiling, and like the *gopīs* they are endlessly talking: "Prabhupāda was with us here and he walked in the park and poked his cane toward a tree...." "Why this tree is bare?" "Because the soul has left." "Yes...." "Who is it?" "Rāmeśvara has arrived, Śrīla Prabhupāda...." "Ah, Rāmeśvara, take." \[As you offer *mahā-prasāda*,\] "What news is there from Los Angeles?" Time passes. "The life members have arrived from Hyderabad, Śrīla Prabhupāda." "Yes, let them come...." The train pulls away slowly, and I am running alongside of your car with the window open, and you hand me a white flower from the window, smiling, "Pañcadraviḍa, you may write to me...." As I walk into the hotel room in the Hong Kong Hilton, you are waiting, "Ah, Pañcadraviḍa is here. Let him stay here and manage Hong Kong." In Kṛṣṇaloka everyone, like Śrīla Prabhupāda, has a wonderful sense of humor. So when we are massaging your feet, you say, "You are both demigods, sent here to help me in my work." (In my case, did not Indra once take birth as a pig?\] Or, by your order, I am plowing the fields of Māyāpur, clumsily trying to uproot the weeds that are growing along with my devotional creeper, as the oxen drag me along. The townspeople come to laugh, but I am only made of your mercy, and so my folly seems to have some worth.

I take shelter of your instructions. I must remain humble and eternally offer you my obeisances. If I always offer my obeisances for all the offenses that I have committed, and which I am foolishly committing, then I will never lose sight of your lotus feet.

You are in the core of our hearts, Śrīla Prabhupāda. But with the knowledge and philosophy you have given us, by your mercy we will devastate the citadels of *māyā’s* illusion.

I pray to remain always your servant.

Pañcadraviḍa Swami

GBC–Mexico, Central America, Andhra Pradesh  
Co-GBC–Colombia, Ecuador

## Rāmeśvara Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādī-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept my prostrated obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet. It has been over one and a half years since you have departed from our association, but everywhere I look I simply see you. In *maṅgala-ārati, Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* class, *guru-pūjā,* in chanting *japa,* in the association of the devotees, in our preaching activities, in printing your books and distributing them as much as possible, in honoring and relishing *prasāda,* in worshiping Śrī Śrī Rukmiṇī-Dvārakādhīśa, in giving the evening class–in every corner of ISKCON you are alive and present. Your presence is felt even more in our separation from you. In remembering Your Divine Grace I become overwhelmed by all the gifts you have give to us. You have given your books and instructions, which very easily enable us to become perfectly Kṛṣṇa conscious. You have given us so much service to Lord Śrī Caitanya, by which He becomes very satisfied and awards us with love of Godhead. You have given us your Gurukula children to train as pure devotees so they may become the actual spiritual leaders of the earth. You have given us the most sublime association of your devotees and beautiful temples to reside in, where we can always worship and remember Kṛṣṇa and constantly bathe in the nectarean river of devotional service. I am especially feeling overwhelmed and indebted because you have given me so many sincere and serious disciples to train and offer to you for your mission. By their help I am trying to carry out all the orders you gave to me. Because I could not do this service alone, you have sent so many nice devotees to help me serve you.

You have done everything perfectly, and by your personal example we now have the most confidential knowledge–how to live as pure devotees of the Lord. And after teaching perfectly by your example, you left everything necessary for us to follow your example. My whole life has become remembering you and trying to follow you, and somehow showing my indebtedness for the unlimited mercy you have given. By your kindness you are fulfilling my desire that my life simply become your instrument for your mission and pastimes. I am very anxious to help remind the world of your words, your *kirtana,* your compassion and enthusiasm, your boldness and love for Kṛṣṇa, your perfect transcendental behavior, your pastimes, your worshiping the Deities, your honoring *prasāda–* everything you did. My position is very, very tiny and insignificant, so I have nothing of value to offer to you. All I can offer is my lowly self. I hope you will accept this lowly disciple and manifest any desire or pastime you like through me. If you would kindly accept me, then in this way the devotees may be reminded of some of your unlimited qualities, your transcendental message, and your service. I pray to have no other interest than your interest, and to become your eternal instrument and servant. This life has no value, but if you accept my humble offering, then it will be full of value. On this most auspicious celebration of your divine appearance, I offer whatever I have to you for your eternal pleasure, to do with me as you like.

*Your unworthy servant,*  
Rāmeśvara Swami

Initiating *guru;* BBT Trustee  
GBC–Los Angeles, Denver, San Diego, Laguna Beach, Japan, Hawaii, New Barshana farm

## Rūpānuga dāsa

**There Is No Other Way**

By the grace of Kṛṣṇa the world got Śrīla Prabhupāda, and by the grace of Śrīla Prabhupāda the world will get Kṛṣṇa. But to receive that grace, the general populace must actually recognize Śrīla Prabhupāda as the greatest spiritual leader of our time–greater than any saintly politician, greater than the impersonalists with their painted smiles, greater than the dead "incarnation" who had the audacity to call the holy names his own, greater than any Western ecclesiastic whose pious birth is contradicted by impious acts. At that time the masses will see the dawning of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. According to the transcendental system, first the *guru* is accepted, then Kṛṣṇa.

Therefore, it is up to us to distribute and preach the glories of Śrīla Prabhupāda with all humility, overlooking any of our own qualifications that might lead us to surmise personal greatness, and avoiding the propensity in the heart to criticize others. Śrīla Prabhupāda has written in the Fourth Canto, Chapter Twenty-one, of the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* "Anyone who cooperates with this movement or accepts its principles will get the same result as the workers who are actively propagating Kṛṣṇa consciousness." And this movement will be accepted by the masses when its founder- *dcdrya* is accepted. There is no other way. *Yasya prasādād bhagavat-prasādo yasyāprasādān na gatiḥ kuto 'pi.*

*Your eternal servant,*  
Rūpānuga dāsa

GBC-Washington, D.C.

## Satsvarūpa Goswami

***"He lives forever by his divine instructions, and the follower lives with him."***

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept my humble obeisances at your lotus feet. On this day of your appearance anniversary, I would like to recall some of your important instructions. I understand that whatever you say is coming directly from God.

You have written that of all the instructions of the spiritual master, the most important is the instruction to chant at least sixteen rounds daily of the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mahā-mantra.* Lord Caitanya said, *kīrtanīyaḥ sadā hariḥ:* always chant Hare Kṛṣṇa. But you have specified at least sixteen rounds minimum because we are not able to chant much more than this due to our restless mentalities. We are also given a minimum because we have so many preaching activities to conduct in spreading Kṛṣṇa consciousness in this age of Kali. But nevertheless, it is your most important instruction that we chant Hare Kṛṣṇa.

I want to thank you for having given us this wonderful holy name. We have recently been studying the narration of Ajāmila. So we understand that offenseless chanting at the time of death will save us from the danger of forgetting Kṛṣṇa and thus taking birth again in the conditioned state to suffer great pain, and to risk missing Kṛṣṇa yet again and falling under the spell of *māyā.* Words cannot express sufficiently the glories of the holy name, nor can my feeble words sufficiently thank you for giving us this *mahā-mantra.* I pray I will never forget that it is the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mantra* which can save not only me, but all the living beings in the universe. Only if they chant Hare Kṛṣṇa, only if they chant Hare Kṛṣṇa, only if they chant Hare Kṛṣṇa–there is no other alternative, there is no other alternative, there is no other alternative in this age of Kali. Now I am able to grasp this with my intellect, and I pray to follow it and gain practical realization, taking the *mahā-mantra* to my tongue at all times and working to propagate the name's glories. Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for bringing us the holy name: Hare Kṛṣṇa, Hare Kṛṣṇa, Kṛṣṇa Kṛṣṇa, Hare Hare / Hare Rāma, Hare Rāma, Rāma Rāma, Hare Hare.

You have also instructed us to cooperate amongst ourselves as a proof of our love for you. It is *māyā* if we fight, and you have said the danger of our own fighting within is much worse than the danger of demons fighting us. Let us keep this instruction in our hearts and actions. Let us be honest and humble with each other, like the sons and daughters who do not fight or noncooperate amongst themselves even if there is provocation, because they do not want to displease their father by such fighting. You want us to always preach and not be lazy. We will please you by doing some solid work for the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement, whether in book distribution, recruiting, farm development, festivals, Gurukula–there are, it seems, no end to the varieties of preaching in this movement.

On this day of your appearance, I pray to you as my savior and the savior of the world. Please bless me by allowing me always to remain in your eternal devotional service, life after life.

*na dhanaṁ na janaṁ na sundarīṁ kavitāṁ vā jagad-īśa kāmaye*  
*mama janmani janmanīśvare bhavatād bhaktir ahaitukī tvayi*

**"O my lord, Śrīla Prabhupāda, I do not want wealth, beautiful women, or followers. I only want your causeless devotional service, life after life."**

Satsvarūpa dāsa Goswami

Initiating *guru*  
GBC–Baltimore, Gītā-nāgarī farm

## Svarupa Damodara dāsa

*oṁ ajñāna-timirāndhasya jñānāñjana-śalākayā*  
*cakṣur unmīlitaṁ yena tasmai Śrī-gurave namaḥ*

*Most beloved Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

I was born in the darkest region of ignorance, yet you, my spiritual master, Oṁ Viṣṇupāda 108 Śrī Śrīmad His Divine Grace A. C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda, the founder-*ācārya* of the International Society for Krishna Consciousness, have so mercifully opened my eyes with the torchlight of knowledge. I bow down to your lotus feet again and again. I am eternally indebted to you.

One of the qualities of a pure devotee of Kṛṣṇa is that he is *dayāra sāgara,* an ocean of unlimited mercy. This ocean is exhibited in the form of his *līlās,* or pastimes, which depict his humility, compassion, tolerance, forgiveness, and so on. Your Divine Grace is perfect in all respects. Being your most insignificant servant, I am completely incompetent to describe the wonderful qualities you exhibited through your pastimes. Still, with your kind permission, and by your mercy, I shall try.

You displayed the symptoms of a perfect Vaiṣṇava at different times, especially at the time of your departure in Vṛndāvana, encircled by your disciples. By your causeless mercy, I was allowed to participate in personally serving your divine lotus feet just before your departure. Kindly put me in the mood of that time again and again, so that I can remember your wonderful qualities constantly. This remembrance will put me directly in touch with Kṛṣṇa, the Supreme Personality of Godhead. Śrīla Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura instructs us as follows:

*yasya prasādād bhagavat-prasādo*  
*yasyāprasādān na gatiḥ kuto 'pi*  
*dhyāyan stuvaṁs tasya yaśas tri-sandhyaṁ*  
*vande guroḥ śrī-caraṇāravindam*

**"By satisfying the spiritual master, the Supreme Personality of Godhead becomes satisfied, and by not satisfying the spiritual master, there is no chance of being promoted to the plane of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. I should therefore meditate on the spiritual master and pray for his mercy three times a day, and I should always offer my respectful obeisances unto him."**

Your lotus feet are my only shelter, Śrīla Prabhupāda. It is my great fortune that the all-merciful Lord, Śrī Kṛṣṇa, allowed me to participate in your activities while you were preparing to depart for Goloka Vṛndāvana. It is also my great fortune that you kindly allowed me to assist you in your divine mission.

By simply immersing ourselves in your pastimes, all your children can develop love for and faith in each other and become completely Kṛṣṇa conscious. You taught us by your personal example how to develop pure love of Godhead. Your mercy is the eternal thread that ties all of us together. Again and again I humbly pray to your divine lotus feet that this thread never weakens. In this way we can all push on your divine movement and thus perfect our lives.

Today is the most glorious day, because on this day you appeared on this earth to save the fallen millions drowning in the waves of *māyā.* You distributed the message of *Bhāgavata* as it is, just like Śrīla Vyāsadeva, the incarnation of Godhead. On this day, kindly allow me to offer a few remembrances at your divine lotus feet by collecting just a droplet from the ocean of your wonderful qualities that I experienced at the time of your departure.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, I remember that you wanted to make sure that we understood at least *sādhana-bhakti,* the gateway to full understanding of the goal of knowledge. Time and again you taught us that the goal of knowledge is Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa, and when we know Kṛṣṇa, we can go back to Godhead and reach the ultimate goal of life. At every moment you lived the principles of *bhaktivedānta,* which means "to attain the goal of knowledge through *bhakti,* devotion." I remember how, on the evening of October 21, 1977, you chanted the *mahā-mantra* very loudly just after drinking some fruit juice. You also chanted *śrī-kṛṣṇa-caitanya prabhu nityānanda,* etc. and *śarīra avidyā-jāla....* You wanted us to follow along with your tune, and we all did. And I remember, Śrīla Prabhupāda, how very satisfied you were that evening. Then you started lecturing: "If the tongue is controlled by eating *prasāda,* then the senses can be controlled properly. The senses have to be utilized for the service of Kṛṣṇa. This is transcendental. This is *bhakti-yoga."*

That evening, you were commenting on the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* Canto Ten, Chapter Thirteen, verses 54to 56, while lying on your bed. In this way, even in your very difficult physical situation, you worked ceaselessly for the benefit of us all. You preached to your disciples so forcefully and loudly that evening. You wanted to make sure that we understood the message of *Bhāgavata,* You said, "You should discuss *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* daily; then everything will be clear." You also remarked, "To die discussing *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* is glorious." In this way, Śrīla Prabhupāda, you showed the perfect example of *bhāgavata-sevā.* You are the greatest *mahā-bhāgavata,* Śrīla Prabhupāda.

I remember, Śrīla Prabhupāda, how extremely humorous you were, even at the height of your physical illness. On October 22, soon after the man who had massaged your divine body had left, you commented, "That man's hands are very rough. Those hands are for the horses." Śrīla Prabhupāda, I could never imagine that the day for your departure was nearing. I was always hoping that you would recover soon. On October 27 I told you, "Śrīla Prabhupāda, you look a little stronger." You replied, "Even at night you can say to a blind man, 'Get up, it's morning.' Day or night, the same."

Śrīla Prabhupāda, I remember you were very pleased by our immature expressions of feeling and appreciation for you. On the morning of October 26, the *śarat-pūrṇimā,* we took you in a palanquin on a twenty-minute *parikrama* around Kṛṣṇa-Balarāma Mandira. With intense feeling you performed *praṇāmas* to Kṛṣṇa-Balarāma with folded hands. When we brought you back to your room, you remarked, "I am refreshed." We said, "Śrīla Prabhupāda, today is a very special day–the *śarat-pūrṇimā.* On this day you look like a lotus flower." You looked around at all of us with your loving eyes and remarked, "Keep me surrounded by all of you like this, all the time. Don't leave me." However, in a few days many devotees began to leave, one after another, called away by pressing duties. How could we foresee the transcendental activities of a pure devotee?

Śrīla Prabhupāda, on many occasions you personally taught me how to understand *vairāgya-vidyā,* the science of renunciation. In this connection I will always remember the following incident. On the morning of October 26, I received a telegram from home: "Your grandfather died October 25." I was thinking of going home, since there were no senior family members to conduct the last ceremonial rites. "Śrīla Prabhupāda," I said, "I don't know whether it is proper for me to express this to you at this time–but I am thinking of going home to Manipur for a few days because my grandfather just died. There is no senior member in my family to perform the last ceremonial rites."

You looked intently at me and instructed, "When we take shelter of the lotus feet of Kṛṣṇa, we have no other obligations. Do you think it is necessary to go?" Then you quoted a long Sanskrit verse and said, "That is the Vedic version. Now you decide."

"Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda," I said.

Early the following morning, at one o'clock, when I was with you, you remembered our previous day's conversation and asked, "So, are you going to Manipur?"

"No, Śrīla Prabhupāda, I am not going to Manipur at this time. I will go there later and bring back some devotees to see you." You seemed very happy that I wasn't going to Manipur for my grandfather's funeral. Personally, I never knew how to serve your divine lotus feet properly. You were always forgiving. Your mercy is boundless.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, I remember that you always wanted to defeat the Māyāvādī philosophers. On October 31, at 1 a.m., you called me in and asked, "So, Svarupa Damodara, what did Dr. Kapoor say?" You recalled Dr. Kapoor's comments at our Life Comes From Life conference in Vṛndāvana, from October 14 to 16. His idea was that there is no such thing as matter; it is all imagination. You continued, "What did you answer?" I said my answer was that although matter, being one of the Lord's energies, can be thought of as spiritual, it is the Lord's *inferior* spiritual energy. The *superior* spiritual energy is composed of the living entities. In this way there are two types of spiritual energies–one superior, the other inferior. You were pleased with that answer. Then I requested whether I could ask you a question. "Yes," you said.

"If life is within the atoms of the material elements–say, hydrogen and oxygen–is that life *jīva*?"

"Yes. Matter means undeveloped consciousness, covered consciousness. Just like the flower bud: when it blooms, it is developed consciousness. It is a nice example. Is it clear?"

"Yes, Śrīla Prabhupāda. So, it seems that a living body is made up of innumerable *jīvas.* And among them one is highly conscious, or developed."

"Yes. Just like many worms in the body–full of life." Then you quoted the following verse from the *Gītā* (13.22):

*puruṣaḥ prakṛti-stho hi bhuṅkte prakṛti-jān guṇān*  
*kāraṇaṁ guṇa-saṅgo 'sya sad-asad-yoni-janmasu*

**"The living entity in material nature thus follows the ways of life, enjoying the three modes of nature. This is due to his association with that material nature. Thus he meets with good and evil among various species."**

You remarked, "This is very important. Is it clear? Being, in the atom, is an individual's *karma-phala.* Because of the influence of the three modes of material nature, the individual self has been put into such a condition. It is all due to his forgetfulness of Kṛṣṇa." In this way you were always concerned about how to train us properly in Kṛṣṇa consciousness, even in the middle of your physical illness. Whenever any disciple asked you a question, Śrīla Prabhupāda, you were always enthusiastic to answer it and enlighten him. You are the perfect master, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, I remember that you were always concerned about the physical health of your disciples, even while your own was failing. At midnight on November 2, when I was attending you, you asked me, "Have you all-night guard duty?"

"No, Śrīla Prabhupāda," I replied. "Bhavānanda Mahārāja will be coming at about one o'clock in the morning." You were worried that I would not take rest throughout the night.

The following midnight you again asked, "How long is your duty, Svarupa Damodara?"

"Up to about one-thirty in the morning."

"From when?"

"From 9 p.m., Śrīla Prabhupāda."

"And before that?"

"Upendra, Śrīla Prabhupāda."

In this way, you expressed deep concern for all of us. You were always thinking of our well-being, in spite of your own inconveniences. You wanted to make sure that we all had enough time to rest. Your kindness is boundless, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, I remember how you showed a perfect example of humility, just to train all your disciples. When Mr. Narayana (the ex-governor of Madras) and Mr. Ramakrishna Bajaj (the well-known industrialist) came to see you, you remarked, "Since I am in bed, I cannot receive you properly. Please kindly forgive me." And when your Godbrothers came to see you before your departure and they asked whether there was any last thing they could do for you, you simply remarked, "If I have made any offenses at your lotus feet, kindly forgive me." In this way you showed the perfect humility of a pure Vaiṣṇava.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, I remember your intense love for all your disciples. The following incidents give me a glimpse of your pastimes. On November 11, at eight o'clock in the morning, you called Śrīla Tamāla Kṛṣṇa Mahārāja and said, "I have no strength, and the left leg seems paralyzed. What is the use of lying like a lump of matter? What does the Kavirāja say?" Shortly thereafter the Kavirāja came in, and he told you that Lord Kṛṣṇa would very soon give you strength and enable you to recover. He requested that you take more milk, and you agreed. Your mood that morning seemed quite changed. You said to Śrīla Jayapatākā Mahārāja, "Everyone is leaving me because I'm infectious." All of us remained silent. After a little while, about fifty devotees came in to chant for you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Bharadrāja Prabhu led a sweet and melodious *kīrtana.* Then you asked whether all the chanters had taken *prasāda.* I answered, "Yes, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Many devotees come and go in shifts to chant for you, Śrīla Prabhupāda." A little later Lokanātha Mahārāja and his party came in, and he gave a nice report about his preaching activities. Śrīla Tamāla Kṛṣṇa Mahārāja then asked Lokanātha Mahārāja to lead the chanting, and you said, "He must be tired." Lokanātha Mahārāja replied, "For you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, no tiring. I came a long way to see you and chant for you."

Early in the afternoon, we were cast into an immense ocean of transcendental anxiety when you said that you would like to go on *parikrama* in a bullock cart around Vṛndāvana and Govardhana Hill. There were two transcendental opinions among your disciples. One group desired to fulfill your will immediately; the other said that we should kindly request Your Divine Grace not to undertake the *parikrama* at this time–especially on very poor roads in a bullock cart, and in your then extremely weak physical condition. Thus all the senior disciples, plus the Kavirāja, met at 3 p.m. in the lounge of the guesthouse reception room to decide what to do. There were strong arguments on both sides. The Kaviraja was completely against the *parikrama.* After a long time had passed with no decision, you called us in and said you wanted to participate in the discussion. You strongly indicated that you wanted to go on the *parikrama* in the bullock cart. It would have taken about six to nine hours. Most of us were completely bewildered at your decision, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Śrīla Tamāla Kṛṣṇa Mahārāja, Śrīla Bhavānanda Mahārāja, and myself strongly requested Your Divine Grace to kindly not undertake the *parikrama* at that time–to kindly postpone it for at least a few days until you got some strength. When I reminded you of the Bengali proverb that you had used when you decided not to go to Māyāpur–i.e., "When you are doubtful, don't do it"–you answered very strongly, "That is material." So all our arguments were completely defeated by your arguments, Śrīla Prabhupāda, and you were determined to go on *parikrama* to Govardhana. Being totally helpless, we were completely at your mercy, and we remarked, "Yes, Śrīla Prabhupāda, we are ready to follow your order. It is our life and soul to follow your order; whatever you decide is final, and we will follow."

So, at about six that evening, it was decided that you would go on *parikrama* on a bullock cart the next morning. Our Kavirāja was so upset that he was thinking of leaving Vṛndāvana as soon as possible. Lokanātha Mahārāja and Trivikrama Mahārāja were entrusted to procure the bullock carts from Mathurā, and we were supposed to start from Kṛṣṇa-Balarāma Mandira at about 5 a.m., just after *maṅgala-ārati.* We were simply depending on the mercy of Kṛṣṇa. Śrīla Prabhupāda, you said, "What is the use of lying here? Whether I die in the bullock cart or I die here in bed, it is the same."

The following is part of the conversation that took place that evening:

**Lokanātha Mahārāja:** Everyone should be prepared to stay overnight there.

**Śrīla Tamāla Kṛṣṇa Mahārāja:** Where?

**Lokanatha Mahārāja:** Under the trees.

**Haṁsadūta Mahārāja:** We are supposed to be *gosvāmīs.* We have to stay under the trees–under a different tree every night.

**Bhakticāru Mahārāja:** But with just one window open at night, Śrīla Prabhupāda feels cold, in spite of the blanket.

**Svarūpa Dāmodara:** It will be very cold in the early morning hours.

**Devotee:** Prabhupāda can stay in the van overnight. Prabhupāda is like a desire tree–he satisfies everyone. We can sleep around the van.

**Lokanātha Mahārāja:** With bamboo sticks we can turn the cart into a house.

**Svarūpa Dāmodara:** I think Lokanātha Svāmī should plan out the route completely–where we will picnic, etc. Everything should be arranged well ahead of time.

**Śrīla Prabhupāda:** Do it.

**Śrīla Tamāla Kṛṣṇa Mahārāja:** Yes, we'll prepare the cart tonight, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

**Svarūpa Dāmodara:** We should also request the Kavirāja to come along.

**Śrīla Prabhupāda:** Invite him.

**Devotee:** Then it's all decided. Do you want to take some rest, Śrīla Prabhupāda? *\[At that time Kṛṣṇadāsa Bābājī, Your Divine Grace's Godbrother, came into the room.\]*

**Kṛṣṇadāsa Bābājī:** Hare Kṛṣṇa.

**Śrīla Prabhupāda** ***\[in Bengali\]:*** Please come in. Will you please come along with me on *parikrama* tomorrow?

**Kṛṣṇadāsa Bābājī** ***\[in Bengali\]:*** Yes, I will go. What time?

**Śrīla Prabhupāda** ***\[in Bengali\]:*** Five o'clock in the morning. *\[To us:\]* Give some *prasāda* to Bābājī Mahārāja.

**Bhakticāru Mahārāja:** Śrīla Prabhupāda, can we request something? If you feel pain or experience any difficulty, then we can bring you back?

**Śrīla Prabhupāda:** Yes, I am not obstinate.

In this way, everybody was planning for the *parikrama* the next day, and Kṛṣṇadāsa Bābājī left. He told us that he would be back very soon; he had to go somewhere for some urgent work. It was then about seven o'clock, and I was planning to take a shower so I could be refreshed when I returned in a little while to attend you. Just then Śrīla Bhavānanda Mahārāja suggested to me that he would be coming with Śrīla Tamāla Kṛṣṇa Mahārāja at exactly one o'clock in the morning, when I would be alone with Your Divine Grace, and the three of us would fall at your lotus feet and request you to kindly not go on *parikrama* at that time. This was our secret plan. We hoped that Kṛṣṇa would give us the guidance and intelligence how to request Your Divine Grace. We also planned to ask Kṛṣṇadāsa Bābājī to request you not to go on *parikrama.* We were in so much anxiety, Śrīla Prabhupāda; I simply couldn't accept that you'd be going on *parikrama* the next day in a bullock cart.

\[*After about one hour Kṛṣṇadāsa Bābājī returned. We were already in great anxiety.*\]

Śrīla Tamāla Kṛṣṇa Mahārāja: Well, Śrīla Prabhupāda, I tell you, I'm getting so upset sitting in the room upstairs. I mean, I've just had it. I was walking around, and two devotees told me this road is so bad that if you go on it, you're going to be jolted back and forth. The road is so terrible. I mean, I just can't understand, Śrīla Prabhupāda, why it has to be tomorrow that we have to go. If anybody wants you to travel, I do; my whole desire is to take you all over the world. I want to take you on *parikrama,* but why do we have to go when you are in this condition? I can't understand it. I was standing outside. This Kavirāja... he has worked so hard. He is so much disappointed; he can't understand it, either. Today you have taken a half-kilo of milk. No mucus, no stool produced. Tomorrow he wants to give you medicine that will build this milk into muscles. He says very soon you'll be able to have strength. Why are we throwing everything out the window and going on *parikrama*? I cannot understand it, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

**Śrīla Prabhupāda:** I cannot refuse the requests of all of you.

**Śrīla Tamāla Kṛṣṇa Mahārāja:** And we cannot refuse your requests. We will take you to all the *tīrthas.* Just you get a little stronger. You'll be free from all managerial business; you'll simply go to the *tīrthas* and take *darśana* of all the Deities in India. Everyone will stay in their respective places and manage. They just want you to be stronger.

**Śrīla Prabhupāda:** All right. That will satisfy you? Bhavānanda, you are satisfied?

**Śrīla Bhavānanda Mahārāja:** Now I am, Śrīla Prabhupāda. I was in too much anxiety.

**Śrīla Prabhupāda:** I cannot put you in anxiety. \[*Then you said to Kṛṣṇadāsa Bābājī, about Śrīla Bhavānanda Mahārāja:*\] He has done so much. He has suffered in Māyāpur so much. I cannot put him in any difficulty anymore. \[*Then to us:*\] So I shall do what you desire. \[*Then to Kṛṣṇadāsa Bābājī in Bengali:*\] What is your feeling, Bābājī Mahārāja?

**Kṛṣṇadāsa Bābājī \[*****in Bengali*****\]:** What you say is true.

**Śrīla Prabhupāda:** They are giving arguments from left and right. \[*You laugh*.\] I cannot refuse.

**Kṛṣṇadāsa Bābājī \[*****in Bengali*****\]*****:*** This is right.

**Śrīla Tamāla Kṛṣṇa Mahārāja:** I'm amazed, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Half a kilo of milk you drank today; no mucus, no stool. That's wonderful. You could not have done this two weeks ago.

**Bhakticāru Mahārāja:** When the Kavirāja was told, he was also quite amazed. He told me not to give you any more milk. But you wanted more, so I am giving.

**Śrīla Prabhupāda:** Where is the Kavirāja?

**Śrīla Tamāla Kṛṣṇa Mahārāja:** He's outside again, to get medicine. This man *\[Adridharaṇa\]* says he sits up all night, worrying about you, thinking, taxing his brain how to give you just the right medicine.

**Bhakticāru Mahārāja:** Yesterday, when I went to call for him at four-thirty in the morning, I saw that he had been sitting on his bed all night.

**Śrīla Prabhupāda:** I cannot refuse. This is not my business. *\[You turn to Kṛṣṇadasa Bābājī and ask in Bengali:\]* What is your feeling, Bābājī Mahārāja?

**Kṛṣṇadāsa Bābājī:** Hare Kṛṣṇa.

**Śrīla Prabhupāda:** All right. \[70 *us:*\] You take Bābājī Mahārāja on *parikrama.*

**Śrīla Tamāla Kṛṣṇa Mahārāja:** Your bullock cart will go tomorrow to Govardhana. We will go on your behalf. But you will go one day. That we promise you. Our greatest pleasure is to take you to *tīrtha-yātrā,* Śrīla Prabhupāda. We want so much to go with you on that.

**Śrīla Prabhupāda:** Thank you very much.

**Śrīla Tamāla Kṛṣṇa Mahārāja:** Actually, Śrīla Prabhupāda, we are so much attached to you that you practically drive us to madness sometimes. Tonight we are becoming mad.

**Śrīla Prabhupāda:** No, no. I shall not do that. \[*To Kṛṣṇadāsa Bābājī:*\] You always express great appreciation for all the devotees. So, you will take bath in Śrī Rādhā-kuṇḍa on my behalf.

**Śrīla Tamāla Kṛṣṇa Mahārāja:** We'll get you better, Śrīla Prabhupāda. You will also be able to take bath personally there. We will see that you get better. Kṛṣṇa will make all our words come true, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Śrīla Prabhupāda, the way you deal with us makes us so deep in our attachment for you.

**Śrīla Prabhupāda:** That is my duty.

That was your duty; that was your greatness, Śrīla Prabhupāda. You are *mahā-mahā-bhāgavata.* The secret of your mission was revealed at the very point of your departure. I feel so sorry that I could never understand you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. One thing, though: I am fully convinced that attachment to your lotus feet will be the perfection of my life. Jaya, all glories to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

*Your loving servant, eternally,*  
Svarūpa Dāmodara dāsa

GBC–Manipur, Nagaland, Mizoram, Meghalaya, Arunachal Pradesh, Burma  
Co-GBC–Assam, Tripura, Bhaktivedanta Institute

## Tamāla Kṛṣṇa Goswami

In Goloka Dhāma  
Beyond the mundane cloud  
Amidst Kṛṣṇa's pastimes  
Richly endowed  
Śrī Gurudeva  
His form now anew  
Sits brilliant as the sun  
Golden in hue  
Most dear servitor  
You render service sublime  
Intimate associate  
In eternal pastime  
'Fore our darkened gaze  
Śrī Gurudeva came  
And opened our eyes  
With the Lord's Holy Name  
Rain of compassion  
From the merciful ocean  
You softened our hearts  
Planted seeds of devotion  
Small creepers grew  
As you watered the seeds  
An arbor of shelter  
Shaped by your deeds  
The garden of ISKCON which  
Thou hast wrought  
Is Mahāprabhu’s mission  
Thou hast brought  
Now those who with faith  
The garden's fruit do eat  
In Goloka Dhāma  
Your Divine Grace will meet

Tamāla Kṛṣṇa Goswami

Initiating *guru*  
GBC-Dallas, Houston, Chicago, St. Louis  
Co-GBC-China  
BBT Trustee

## Vāsudeva dāsa

*oṁ ajñāna-timirāndhasya jñānāñjana-śalākayā*  
*cakṣur unmīlitaṁ yena tasmai Śrī-gurave namaḥ*

*Our most beloved Gurudeva,*

Please accept our humble *daṇḍavat-praṇāmas* at your lotus feet again and again on the auspicious day of Vyāsa-pūjā.

We were such fallen souls, but by your causeless mercy you gave us shelter at your lotus feet, accepted us as your disciples, and engaged us in the devotional service of Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa-Kāliya. We are really very proud to be your disciples.

Right from the very beginning from our first meeting with you at Nadi Airport, our greatest pleasure was to serve you. The first instruction you gave us was to build a nice temple in Fiji. From that day our only wish was to fulfill your desire, and by the power of your words that desire was fulfilled. Śrīla Prabhupāda! It was only by your mercy that such a nice temple was built. Now many fallen souls have a chance to know about Kṛṣṇa and to serve Him and chant the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mahā-mantra.*

Dear Gurudeva, we are remembering you at every moment. Twice you came to Fiji, but because of some inconvenience you could not stay at our house, so we built a new house–"Prabhupāda Nivāsa"–and thought now we would be able to serve you very nicely. But we were so unfortunate that you could not come. Your separation is very painful for us. Whenever we think of your physical separation, it seems that someone is piercing our hearts with a spear, and this makes us cry.

Another wonderful thing you have done is that you have selected your foremost disciples and empowered them to be spiritual masters who can guide us as you were guiding. Now there is another chance for us to serve your representatives Śrīla Rāmeśvara Swami and Śrīla Tamāla Kṛṣṇa Goswami in your place. In that way we get the satisfaction of serving you. We will never forget the day we came to meet you at Māyāpur in 1976. That was a real pilgrimage!

O Gurudeva! Please give us shelter at your lotus feet, and be merciful on us so that we can get knowledge and strength to push on your mission in Fiji with the help of your present *ācāryas* Śrīla Rāmeśvara Swami and Śrīla Tamāla Kṛṣṇa Goswami Mahārāja.

Jaya Gurudeva.

*Your eternal servant,*  
Vāsudeva dāsa

GBC-Fiji

## Vyāsa-Pūjā Homages from ISKCON Centers Around the World

## Adelaide

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādī-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

It is with great difficulty that we try to compose some words in glorification of our spiritual master. His Divine Grace A. C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda. This age of Kali is described in the scriptures as being like an ocean of faults, and the Kali-yuga man is described there also as being very poorly endowed with the necessary facilities for spiritual progress, such as memory, intelligence, bodily strength, longevity, etc. Yet somehow or other we are being delivered by the causeless mercy of His Divine Grace, and thereby we can offer some words of gratitude by his mercy.

Śrīla Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura says in his prayers to the spiritual master, *dhyāyan stuvaṁs tasya yaśas tri-sandhyam:* "We should always remember our spiritual master, and at least three times a day we should offer our humble obeisances to his lotus feet." *Yasya prasādād bhagavat-prasādo yasyāprasādān na gatiḥ kuto 'pi:* "Without the spiritual master's mercy, one cannot make any progress."

The spiritual master is the authorized medium by which we fallen conditioned souls can gain audience with the Supreme Personality of Godhead. Therefore we owe our spiritual master an eternal debt which would seem impossible to repay, for he has come to us in our darkest ignorance and opened our hesitant eyes with shining transcendental knowledge. The debt that we owe is that we now know the ultimate goal of life, and that we are delivered from the ocean of anxiety that makes up material life.

The poor foolish *karmīs* are struggling so hard, thinking that they are enjoying, and making so many plans for future material happiness, not realizing that they are missing the priceless opportunity afforded by this human form–to take heed of the divine instructions of a bona fide *guru,* or one who is heavy with spiritual understanding, and return back to Godhead, where life is eternal, full of knowledge and bliss.

So not only should we offer our spiritual master regulated obeisances, but we should completely surrender all facilities at our disposal, i.e., body, mind, and words, for the satisfaction of his desires. What are the desires of our spiritual master? That we take the chanting of Hare Kṛṣṇa and spread it all over the world. Not that our spiritual master, Śrīla Prabhupāda, has to be personally present for us to glorify him and satisfy him, but we can please him and we can be with him by pushing on the *saṅkīrtana* movement. That is, there are two ways to be with the spiritual master– *vāṇī* and *vapuḥ,* his words and his personal presence. And of the two, Śrīla Prabhupāda said that the *vāṇī,* or instruction, is higher.

So on this Vyāsa-pūjā day, we are praying to our spiritual master to please keep us engaged in his service and thereby the service of Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa, and we pray that we may always remember that our relationship with him is based on following his instructions, and that without those instructions we would still be rotting in this material world.

*Your aspiring servants at Adelaide Mandira,*

## Amsterdam

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādī-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*Our dear lord and master Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

One not endowed with transcendental vision might think that you have left us brokenhearted by not being present before us. This is not, however, the case. Although you are our lord birth after birth, and you may embrace or reject us as you wish, still you remain our worshipful lord unconditionally. Your presence, like that of the Supreme Lord, is not something subject to the restrictive confines of time and space. It is a far more subtle and timeless phenomenon than can be expressed by such paltry concepts. Just as Queen Kuntī prays,

*namasye puruṣaṁ tvādyam īśvaraṁ prakṛteḥ param*  
*alakṣyaṁ sarva-bhūtānām antar bahir avasthitam*

**"O Kṛṣṇa, I offer my obeisances to You because You are the original personality and are unaffected by the qualities of the material world. You are existing both within and without everything, yet You are invisible to all."**

In a similar manner, although not apparent to the casual observer, your spiritual presence, O Prabhupāda, is keenly felt within everything by one whose eyes have been anointed with the salve of love. You are a Vaikuṇṭha person, and as such you are able to manifest yourself in ways inaccessible and incomprehensible to the uninitiated. It is said that the pure devotee reflects the opulences of the Supreme Lord, who pervades, by His unlimited energies, the entirety of time and space. So one may say, not to put too fine a point on it, that you, too, dear Gurudeva, are all-pervading.

The Lord, as Kṣīrodakaśāyī Viṣṇu, enters each and every atom and the heart of every single living entity, be he a microbe or a Brahma. Thus through countless crores of births. He is the witness and overseer of all their actions. Similarly, in the form of the divine instructions contained in your books and lectures, you are entering also the hearts of so many living entities and are becoming an integral part of their deliberations on the meaning and goal of life.

That day is not so distant when a man may not be called learned who has not read your books, and when in erudite circles all points of philosophical contention will be resolved by reference to the ultimate authority of the Bhaktivedanta purports. Men will say, "This statement is not supported by Bhaktivedanta, therefore I do not accept it."

In *Bhagavad-gītā* Kṛṣṇa says, *raso 'ham apsu kaunteya:* "I am the taste of water, I am the light of the sun and moon." The sages see Him in everything and everything in Him. In this way one may also remember you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. *Raso 'ham:* we remember the transcendental way in which you would drink water without touching the cup with your lips. *Prabhāsmi śaśi-sūryayoḥ:* upon seeing the splendor of morning sunlight, thousands of examples that you have given in lectures and discussions spring to mind. The sun being localized is the source of all sunlight, similarly Kṛṣṇa, although localized in Goloka Vṛndāvana, pervades the creation by His different energies. *Śabdaḥ khe:* "I am the sound in ether"–your transcendental voice, clear like crystal, cuts like a rapier through the fog of this Kali-yuga. *Pauruṣaṁ nṛṣu:* "I am the ability in man"–and whatever ability you may find in us is existing only by your mercy. *Bījaṁ māṁ sarva-bhūtānām:* you are the seed-giving father, planting the *bhakti-latā-bīja* in our hearts. *Tapaś cāsmi tapasviṣu:* to create within such fallen souls as us the ability to perform austerities is in itself a great wonder. Only out of love for you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, were we able to abandon our sinful, bestial activities and crawl out of the mire of the modes of ignorance and passion. Being bound by hundreds and thousands of desires, by lust and anger, our anxiety knew no end as we engaged in horrible works meant to destroy each other and the world, earning no other reward than the prospect of repeated birth among species of demoniac life. *Jīvanaṁ sarva-bhūtānām:* "I am the life of all that lives." Before meeting you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, we were lifeless, considered dead although apparently living. We were morose and black-faced, hopeless and with no future. But now we are dancing like jubilant peacocks, full of the life you have injected into these dead bodies.

In these and a million other ways we can remember you, O divine master, and by being absorbed in the ecstatic execution of your instructions–"Save your planets'–we are continually diving and surfacing in the ocean of your blissful association. By distributing your books we feel that you are at every moment by our sides to enliven and counsel us. Therefore we are not feeling too much brokenhearted by not having you present before us, because birth after birth we wish to associate with you by distributing your books and helping you to preach the sublime message of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu.

Jaya Gurudeva! Jaya Prabhupāda!

*Your servants in Amsterdam,*

## Atlanta / Tennessee Farm

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādī-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

The *Purāṇas* state that many *gurus* are very expert at taking their disciples' money, but very rare is the *'guru* who can take away his disciple's anxiety. We can, therefore, understand that you are the rarest *guru* of all. You have taken away the anxieties of countless people all over the world. Although you have disappeared from our vision, you have expertly left behind your disciples to continue this work on your behalf.

The word *guru* means "heavy"–heavy with knowledge–and knowledge means to understand God, Kṛṣṇa, ourselves, and our eternal relationship with Kṛṣṇa. Forgetfulness or ignorance of Kṛṣṇa and our eternal relationship with Him is the original source of our anxiety. One who claims to give perfect knowledge but cannot give knowledge of Kṛṣṇa is a cheater, but you have received knowledge through the bona fide disciplic succession from Kṛṣṇa Himself. Imperfect, so-called knowledge simply aggravates anxiety, because it causes one to forget Kṛṣṇa even more, but that which you give, Śrīla Prabhupāda, is the tonic to relieve all distress, because it puts one directly in touch with the Supreme Lord.

Knowledge, or relief from material distress, means to understand the difference between this temporary material world and the spiritual reality upon which it rests. The foolish materialistic leaders even admit their inability to mitigate the trauma of materialistic civilization, but being too proud to accept your guidance, they suffer unlimitedly, needlessly. We asked a local theologian about the soul's destination at death. He replied, "That is a great mystery."

To the uneducated materialists, the science of God remains a perpetual mystery. But to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, the answers are very clear. You are a swan among crows, and we dedicate our lives to distributing your soothing, comforting messages to the anxiety-filled hearts of the unfortunate, misguided people of the world.

Everyone is bewildered by the invincible force of *māyā,* but the words in your books can give eternal peace and satisfaction. We are yours, Śrīla Prabhupāda. No one may wish to hear us, but we will continue to give the fallen souls your mercy. Let the total anxiety of the world be destroyed by your mercy. Jaya Śrīla Prabhupāda!

**Atlanta**

## Auckland

We think of him  
Like a lotus sweet,  
His instructions  
Māyā’s defeat.  
Who was this man?  
We worship his feet,  
Our dearmost  
Śrīla Prabhupāda.

His residence?  
Śrī Vṛndāvana-dhāma,  
A blissful place  
Serene and calm,  
But the world was in need  
Of hari -nāma,  
And no one to help  
Śrīla Prabhupāda,

So in '65  
Out West he came,  
No friends, no money, Just kṛṣṇa-prema  
And a mission:  
The fallen souls to reclaim.  
So merciful,  
Śrīla Prabhupāda.

Down in the Bowery  
The lowest of men,  
A sinful place,  
Kali's den.  
No hope at all  
For salvation–but then  
Kṛṣṇa sent  
Śrīla Prabhupāda.

At first a struggle  
With little success,  
A little money,  
A printing press,  
Such humble beginnings  
But nevertheless  
Kṛṣṇa's own  
Śrīla Prabhupāda.

Chanting Hare Kṛṣṇa  
In Thompkins Square Park  
The first bold step  
The initial spark  
That would light the fire  
To destroy the dark.  
Our hero,  
Śrīla Prabhupāda.

Dancing, chanting,  
Taking prasāda,  
A little philosophy,  
Is it so hard?  
All bad habits  
You simply discard! Blissful process,  
Śrīla Prabhupāda.

And so one temple,  
Then two, then more, Devotees coming  
By the score  
Soon to expand  
From America's shore, Our intrepid  
Śrīla Prabhupāda.

Back to India  
The devotees led  
The Vedic culture, there  
Almost dead.  
Western sādhus!?  
"Not possible!" they said.  
Proven wrong by  
Śrīla Prabhupāda.

Gaining momentum  
Always pushing on,  
Huge order of books From Dai Nippon  
Back to the States  
Mahā-saṅkīrtana! Unrelenting  
Śrīla Prabhupāda.

Translating work  
Never decreasing,  
Volumes printed  
Somehow unceasing,  
Preaching fire  
Ever increasing,  
A miracle,  
Śrīla Prabhupāda.

"I never looked  
To the left or the right  
But straight ahead  
Into the fight."  
Such bold words  
Were Prabhupāda's might.  
All glories, all glories to  
Śrīla Prabhupāda.

And so from America  
Across the world  
The banner of Śrī  
Caitanya unfurled.  
1SKCON like a lotus  
Gracefully uncurled,  
Watered by  
Śrīla Prabhupāda.

Books the basis,  
Preaching the essence,  
Purity the force  
To conquer nescience.  
Utility the principle,  
Chanting the penance,  
Transcendental blueprint,  
Śrīla Prabhupāda.

The shining light  
Of this darkened age,  
His greatness still Impossible to gauge,  
Our lord and master  
The divine sage  
Purehearted  
Śrīla Prabhupāda.

So many temples  
So many farms,  
He opened the West  
To the holy dhāmas.  
So much he gave us,  
We have no qualms  
In worshiping him,  
Śrīla Prabhupāda.

The greatest master,  
At whose lotus feet  
All spiritual masters  
Desire their seat.  
The greatest preacher  
None could defeat,  
Deserving the name  
Śrīla Prabhupāda.

Now he has left us.  
So it would appear,  
But for those who read  
All his books and hear  
His transcendental instructions  
With a submissive ear  
He is always personally present,  
There is no need to fear.  
We take shelter of our beloved  
Śrīla Prabhupāda.

## Austria

All Glories to Śrīla Prabhupāda!  
All Glories to Śrī Śrī Guru and Gaurāṅga! All Glories to Śrī Kṛṣṇa-saṅkīrtana!

*My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādī-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Please accept my most humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet.

Unqualified, fallen, puffed up, and a perfect product of Kali-yuga as I am, I'm trying hard to glorify your boundless, ever-increasing glories, even though my tongue is jaundiced. Your glories are so unlimited and cannot just be described, even if every living entity of all the spiritual and material worlds had a million heads and tongues. (Unless, of course, they were empowered to do so by your causeless mercy.) Still, one will not be able to do any justice, for you have delivered *kṛṣṇa-prema–pure* love of God–to every living entity, which no one else has done in the history of *bhāgavatas* and *mahā-bhāgavatas,* and certainly you are the greatest devotee, and therefore the dearmost beloved of your spiritual master and Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa.

So what to speak of the most fallen of fallen souls like my wretched self, who even though I was an initiated disciple and undertook the vow of Kṛṣṇa consciousness have hardly done anything to please you, am but a miserable failure in Kṛṣṇa consciousness. I am so ashamed of myself, ashamed to lift my wretched face, for even though you gave me millions of opportunities to serve you when you were personally present in your beautiful, divine form, so many opportunities to purify my very existence and increase my attachment to your lotus feet, unfortunate as I am, I never had the desire to serve and become attached to you. And this is the criteria for going back home, back to Godhead. As I meditate on my wretched behavior, I feel the only punishment is to give up this body, worthy to become a worm in the stool. When I recall those memorable last months of your stay in Vṛndāvana, and especially those last moments of your stay in this mortal world, I always think as to why my heart was just like a stone or iron, as it could not melt to your forceful divine instructions, order, and form. It did not melt to serve every moment of your transcendental stay to purify the entire universe, and to partake enthusiastically in this glorious movement of yours, which is delivering perfect happiness to every living entity. No wonder you once rightly chastised me in the assembly of the devotees at the Hyderabad farm for being a dog, rascal, fool, an ass, and an idiot for forgetting everything of this wonderful Kṛṣṇa conscious culture I was born into.

When I look back, I can see no reason in all the three worlds except your causeless mercy that has saved and brought me by my ears to the lotus feet of Kṛṣṇa. Your auspicious appearance in this mortal world is to just deliver the rotting, conditioned souls in this material world back to Kṛṣṇa. From the *śāstras* we find how rare it is to find a pure devotee and how much rarer it is to come in contact with such a pure devotee like yourself. It is my good fortune only to come in contact with you, but rascal as I am, I neglected your valuable life-saving instructions, which are my only hope and are the only hope for every living entity who takes shelter of the merciless Māyādevī in the deep dark well of household life. I am fumbling to put into words how you could charm a snake like myself, who just cannot give up holding on to your lotus feet even though I lack this intense desire and undeviating determination to serve your lotus feet.

O my dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, please be merciful unto me, and kindly bestow your causeless mercy so that your instructions and order will strongly develop in my heart, and the desire to serve only your two lotus feet in all humility and tolerance as a blade of grass or a tree, free from all sorts of material desires for name, fame, profit, adoration, and sex life, free from the propensity of envy, faultfinding, criticizing, doubts, and mental speculation which arise. Even though I have come to you with my heart filled with material desires, please do not fulfill any of them. This is my only desire, for I have no other shelter here in the material world, as I have been rejected by these so-called friends, society, and family when I was somehow or other attracted by and to Your Divine Grace.

Please give to this wretched servant just one drop of your mercy. I am lower than a blade of grass. Give me all help, strength, protection, and intelligence to push on and distribute your causeless mercy with undeviating determination and unflinching faith wherever I am, in every town and village. Let me be as you are, without desires and aspiration, following in your footsteps just as you have always followed in the footsteps of your spiritual master. I can only fall at your lotus feet again and again, for thus I may have the energy to know you and your mission correctly. If you examine me, you will find no good qualities. Your mercy is all that I am made of. Without your causeless mercy I will be a prey to the strong power of *māyā,* and I will not be able to maintain my life. But I can see that still there is some hope, for you have, out of your causeless compassion on fallen souls like myself, left your divine order and your confidential servants. My only desire is to become the servant of the servant of your confidential servants, and to become your instrument to spread your glories and mercy and the glories of the holy name of Kṛṣṇa. So please accept me as a particle of dust at your lotus feet.

*Your most wretched and fallen servants,*

## Baltimore

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda.*

*We* offer our respectful obeisances millions of times at your lotus feet.

Often I have tried to appreciate, though my capacity to do so is tiny, the wondrous, transcendental achievements of your manifested presence on this earth. *Vaiṣṇavera kriyā-mudra vijñeha nā bujhāya.* Pondering upon the legacy that you left is truly staggering and awe-inspiring. And although in your sweet humility you would credit your Guru Mahārāja and your Lord, Śrī Kṛṣṇa, with all of your successes, you nonetheless were the recipient of *kṛṣṇa-śakti* because of your unfathomable compassion for all fallen souls and due to the intensity of your pure love for Kṛṣṇa. So, kindly allow us to glorify you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, because you are *viṣṇu-pāda* and *kṛṣṇa-preṣṭha.*

Everyone was amazed by your deeds, O Ācāryadeva! But those who were earnestly willing to put into practice the sublime science of *bhakti-yoga* which you taught received the proper knowledge by which a pure devotee could be actually appreciated. Yet the closer they would come to the proximity of your lotus feet, the more incalculable and inconceivable your glories and accomplishments would become.

In every moment of your life was contained the infinite depth of eternity; in your thoughts, words, and works, the foundation for the next ten thousand years of human culture was existing; and in you, the key to liberation and love of God for all souls was to be found. Your manifested *līlā* was perfect and complete.

Still, in thinking of you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, and about what you have done, my mind sometimes wonders about what you are doing now. All those who saw you on this earth planet, and even those who will hear about your magnanimous deeds, saw only a small segment of your eternal *līlā.* Seeing how much was done by you on behalf of Kṛṣṇa in that small segment that you so kindly showed to us, the mind is boggled to consider how much you had already done before appearing here in 1896... how many countless conditioned souls had you already delivered back to Kṛṣṇa? In how many planets and universes have you traversed, singing the glories of the Lord? And now that you have gone on to continue your preaching out of our sight, how many more millions *of jīvas* will you initiate into Kṛṣṇa consciousness? Thus, a small segment of your *līlā is so* extensive and complete, and when its eternal continuance is pondered, one is left breathless in awe of your all-time greatness in serving the Lord!

O supreme Gurudeva! You are the unlimited, inconceivable, unending manifestation of Lord Caitanya's mercy. You are indeed capable of lifting entire universes into the light of divine redemption, one after another. By your mercy and kindness, you chose to stop here for a short but concentratedly magnificent duration. By your mercy and infinite kindness, you have accepted a shameless fool such as myself as your disciple. Now I am begging, please give me the strength to deserve this great fortune. Please keep me as a particle of dust at your lotus feet.

*The grateful devotees at Baltimore ISKCON,*
