## Laguna Beach

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept our most humble obeisances unto your lotus feet. We pray for your blessings in making this offering unto you. Only by your mercy can we understand how to glorify you properly. It is stated in the *Bhagavad-gītā,*

*tad viddhi praṇipātena paripraṣnena sevayā*  
*upadekṣyanti te jñānaṁ jñāninas tattva-darśinaḥ*

**"Just try to learn the truth by approaching a spiritual master. Inquire from him submissively and render service unto him. The self-realized soul can impart knowledge unto you because he has seen the truth."**

This truth is known to you; therefore we fall prostrated at your lotus feet and beg you to reveal the secret.

To one who has implicit faith in the *guru* and Kṛṣṇa, all of the imports of Vedic knowledge are automatically revealed. By hearing your divine instructions, we get the knowledge to perform actions. And by following your directions, we get realized knowledge. This process is called faith. The beginning of faith is to hear from the bona fide spiritual master. Then the knowledge received must be acted upon. In other words, the instructions of the spiritual master must be served by the disciple, then by humble inquiries at the lotus feet of the *guru,* the knowledge is gradually revealed. It is not a matter of blind faith. Action done in knowledge brings more advancement in knowledge.

This knowledge we have all experienced in our lives. By your mercy, we have been successful in giving up the inferior engagement of the four types of sinful activities. By following your directions we are becoming more and more happy in Kṛṣṇa's service, and by neglecting your instructions we become confused and unhappy, as we were before coming to you. This is proof that you are the real bona fide *guru.* Year after year we are able to continue without satiation. Your instructions are infallible. Simply we have to follow them to get the desired result.

Māyāvādīs claim that the *guru* is a ladder which one can climb to attain liberation, at which time he himself becomes the *guru.* Then the *guru* is rejected–killed–by the disciple, no longer being necessary for further advancement. This is the position of one who neglects to serve your instructions–he is simply killing you.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, we are eternally indebted to you. Now our existence is only to attempt to liquidate this debt. Your orders are crystal clear. Our duty is to propagate the message of God, as given in *Bhagavad-gītā* and *Śrīmad -Bhāgavatam.* We must chant the prescribed number of rounds on our beads and avoid the four types of sinful activities. And we must engage in the specific devotional services which you have specified for each of us according to the rules and regulations of *bhakti-yoga.* It is all very clear. Simply we have to *do* it.

There is no question of asking you for anything. You have already given it to us. Whatever words we speak, whatever intelligence we have, whatever wealth we can acquire, and even our very lives, are all your property. Therefore, we, the devotees of the Laguna Beach temple, simply pray that you use us as your instruments in carrying out your mission to preach the message of Kṛṣṇa in this remote comer of your world. It is sure that if we allow ourselves to be used by you for this purpose, we will attain the ultimate happiness, perfection, and destination, and that your mission will be complete in the shortest possible time.

*Your eternal servants, the devotees of Laguna Beach temple,*

Agnideva dāsa / Pavamāna dāsa / Śyāmasundara dāsa / Baḍa-Hari dāsa / Drumila dāsa / Mukuṇḍa-mālā dāsa / Pāṇḍu dāsa / Bhakta Bri / Ratna-cintāmaṇi-devī dāsī / Kośarūpā-devī dāsī / Divya-maṇḍana-devī dāsī / Mokṣadā-devī dāsī / Bhaktin Chris / Bhaktin Sati

## Lima

What can the greatest and most famous historians understand?  
How can they classify it within their childish schemes?  
How can it be put together with their sophisticated word jugglery?

This *saṅkīrtana* movement, more than a historical fact, is the end of material history. All the demigods wait–concerned–anxious for the final victory, anticipating it, just as Nārada Muni anticipated the advent of Lord Kṛṣṇa. *Jaya, jaya saṅkīrtana!* The *saṅkīrtana* movement of Śrīla Prabhupāda is already manifested, and we are being called to it. What payment in reciprocation of such mercy can be made? So many possibilities for service. So much mercy! How can we properly take advantage of it all? There is a point at which everything ends, although it could continue. Inconceivably final and not final. In this way our strength ends in a cruel, inflexible point although able to continue. We can only offer a specific quantity and quality of service, and it will always be insufficient. Final but never final, the unsatiated final point. But it is said, "Serve to be perfect, and be perfect to serve." Please, Śrīla Prabhupāda, permit us to serve you, to be perfect; to serve you and serve you and serve you.

You left us under the care of your elder sons and then you departed. Under their shelter, under their loving, protective arms that diminish our feelings of separation, we remember you, we dream of you, we glorify you. O supreme transcendental swan! Tireless giver of mercy! You showed us dignity, truth, perfection, goodness, courage, renunciation, wisdom, strength, honesty, and love, true love. There was no difficulty in understanding it. You gave the most obvious example of the highest and most sublime sanctity. You conquered our hearts, rectified them, and placed them on the path back to Godhead. O Gurudeva! Give us the potency to traverse that path! Permit us to grasp the enlivening remembrance of you. Let your pure image destroy at once our foolish material desires. Let us run as carefree children, fearless, jumping, not caring for *māyā's* impediments. Let nothing else concern us. Just only run and run, sweating off our impurities, leaving behind, drop by drop, all our contamination. Only run and run after your beautiful lotus feet. Hearing you, remembering you, worshiping you, offering you our heartfelt prayers. Let this be our very life, expanding, all over the three worlds, your glorious *saṅkīrtana* movement.

*Your eternal worshipers from Lima Mandira,*

## London (Bhaktivedanta Manor)

Glories, glories to the Lord's pure devotee  
All glories to you, O vessel of beauty.  
O Prabhupāda, our savior, we bow down at your feet  
By your divine mercy the Lord we will meet.  
Our hearts we'd neglected, so empty a land  
With no water to sprout the poor seed in the sand.  
As dry as a desert we're calling for rain  
With the waters of mercy you relieve our pain.  
Our hearts were tight closed like flowers in the night  
But now you have risen we bathe in your light.  
With soft rays of mercy you give us new life,  
By your grace our hearts bloom, our faces shine bright.  
With walkways for you and fresh, fragrant air  
We're watering the creeper you planted so sure  
We're hoping you'll come and not leave us once more.  
If we keep our hearts pure, no weeds to allow  
If we bedeck love's creeper with the holy name's sound  
If we preach Kṛṣṇa's glories and spread His fame  
We're sure to win you, your company to gain.  
Glories, glories to the Lord's pure devotee  
All glories to you, O vessel of beauty.  
O Prabhupāda, our savior, we bow down at your feet  
By the Lord's divine mercy you one day we'll meet.

*Your servants at Bhaktivedanta Manor*

## London (Soho Street)

*Dearmost Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept our worthless obeisances at your sacred and divine lotus feet.

You have rescued the world from the depths of ignorance and destruction. We are so fortunate that in the midst of this crazy city, somewhere on this insignificant planet during its darkest hour, you appeared like the blazing sun. You have invited Their Lordships Śrī Śrī Rādhā-London-Īśvara to come and be with us, and because of your intense love, the Lord has appeared here in His most merciful and friendly mood to dispel all the gloom of this troubled age.  
In the shade of the Lord's presence we sit and bathe our hearts in the medicinal nectar of your instructions. Your precious words have carried us beyond the contradictions and intense misery of this time and place, and we are no longer affected by its sting. Now we are residents in the Lord's transcendental abode. You have made us so fortunate. We chant Hare Kṛṣṇa with ever-increasing relish and dance in ecstasy.

Now you will be pleased to know that the Lord has manifested His new temple right here in the very heart of London, where it definitely cannot be ignored. As if struck by an arrow of the Absolute Truth, the demoniac momentum of this so-called civilization has shuddered, its confidence lost, and it lies tottering on the brink of collapse. Now you have appeared with knowledge and you have given us Kṛṣṇa. Kṛṣṇa is actually here with us, and your books are here–the foundation of all future civilization. These facts are not yet fully perceived by the general public, but in their hearts they can sense it, and the innocent are becoming humble. Soon the true glory and importance of your appearance will be revealed. On the very edge of doom you have given to the earth a joyful future, and to those who follow your instructions you have given eternal, blissful life, reunited with Kṛṣṇa.

We can never repay our debt to you. Nor can we properly express our gratitude to you. We are useless Kali-yuga living entities–very weak and insignificant–and it is very sad that no one more suitable was available to help you. Actually, we cannot do anything. We're only children in the demon's lair. But like the cowherd boys who marched into the mouth of Aghāsura, we don't feel afraid, because we have great faith in your words and in Kṛṣṇa.

If we can just turn this temple into a preaching whirlwind and suck all the living entities into the still center at your lotus feet, if we can just dedicate our last few flapping moments in these dying bodies to preaching your message with determination, then our lives may become useful. And if we can try our very best to see to the care and happiness of your dearmost Lordships Śrī Śrī Rādhā-London-Īśvara, then we may relish the smile of your pleasure in our hearts and distribute your mercy to others unlimitedly.

*Your humble servants of Śrī Śrī Rādhā-London-Īśvara Dhāma,*

## Los Angeles

New Dvārakā. How is it possible to hear this name and not think of you, Śrīla Prabhupāda? You visited here almost two dozen times in the course of your famous world travels. Sometimes you stayed only one day, sometimes for seven months. In the beginning, you would give your lectures in people's garages, not even having a place of your own. You didn't care; you had full faith in your Guru Mahārāja's order and the desire of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu.

In the nineteenth century, Śrīla Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura stood on the balcony of his small house in Godruma and saw a city of Vaiṣṇavas in Māyāpur, a vision you later fulfilled. Having similar powers, you named your movement the International Society for Krishna Consciousness, even though you only had a small storefront on New York's Lower East Side. Later, you established your world headquarters while preaching in the garages of Los Angeles. In this humble way, tolerating all austere conditions, you created New Dvārakā.

To churn the ocean of your pastimes would take many lifetimes of narrations. The churning rod is now in the expert hands of your devoted servant Śrīla Satsvarūpa Mahārāja, as he painstakingly goes on recording these pastimes for your biography. Seeing this we will try to relate just a drop of the nectar from your later visits to New Dvārakā, leaving the rest of the ocean in his capable hands.

In 1975 you came with a demand: "There are seventeen books sitting unpublished. Complete them in two months!" We couldn't believe it. Just the day before, you told us that one book every two months was a good pace. Now you were demanding seventeen! We tried to explain how it was impossible to edit, typeset, proofread, lay out, shoot, and produce paintings for a book every three days for eight straight weeks. "*Impossible* is a word found in a fool's dictionary!" you replied, simultaneously giving us ecstasy and empowering us by your instructions alone. Devotees began manifesting amazing powers of strength and endurance. Our faith increased. A few days later we were on Venice Beach, the famous place of pilgrimage where you daily smashed the atheistic scientists. As the rising sun turned the sands pink, the devotees strained to hear your voice over the crashing of the waves. Foolishly thinking we had accomplished something, we told you that everything was going so well that we "should" be able to fulfill your order. Planting your cane firmly in the sand, you stopped abruptly, turned, and said, "Not 'should'; you *must"* In this way, you *forced* the ecstasy on us.

You explained this principle one morning on a walk in Delhi. Just a few devotees were staying with you at a life member's house in Cāṇakya Purī. As you walked through the residential area, one devotee asked you, "How does *kṛpā-siddhi* work, Śrīla Prabhupāda?" At this, you began repeatedly poking him in the stomach, and while he laughed in ecstasy you shouted, "You are not willing to take this, but I say, 'Take it! Take it! Take it!' Even you are unwilling, I give it in your pocket, push it. *That is kṛpā."* In this same manner, you forced us to accept the nectar of your mercy by your strict order. We had no choice. This was your mood in New Dvārakā in the summer of 1975.

In the summer of 1976, you made your final manifest appearance here. The mood was one of great jubilation. We discovered a new place to walk–a grassy park on a cliff near the ocean–which you proclaimed to be the best place to walk in L.A. These were the last days of your good health, so you gave many lively talks, visited F.A.T.E. and Golden Avatar, and met with many of your disciples. Your morning class was like a festival. As you entered the temple room through the green velvet curtains, hundreds of devotees would cheer, conchshells would blare, horns would sound, and the *guru-kula* children would dance in ecstasy. You put your hand on one boy's head, and then you laughed when you saw another boy fall over when he jumped in ecstasy upon seeing you. Like a transcendental celebrity, you waved to the cheering devotees in the balcony, motioned for everyone to dance with your upturned palm and captivating smile, and gracefully walked to the Deity doors, holding your two symbols of flower and cane. After leading us in worshiping your beloved Śrī Śrī Rukmiṇī-Dvārakādhīśa, the entire temple exploded into a *guru-pūjā kīrtana.* Previous conceptions of time and space disappeared. Bodily designations crumbled. Materialistic ideas vanished. In a similar situation of great ecstasy, you once turned and said, "Every day is like this in Vaikuṇṭha!" In this way, you gave us the higher taste.

In June of 1976, you were preparing to leave your beloved New Dvārakā for the last time. In a way, we were fortunate that we were unaware of the significance of this journey–you were on your way back to India, never again to appear in America in your golden *vapuḥ* form. Had we understood this, your departure would have been worse than death.

As all of your transcendental children ran every which way to help prepare for your departure, you began to calmly dress yourself for traveling. One by one, you carefully put on your shimmering silk garments, with the concentration of one who is about to meet the Supreme Personality of Godhead. We quickly waved downstairs to Śrīla Rāmeśvara Swami, who immediately ran up the stairs and into your *darśana* room to catch the nectar. As we stood there, staring in ecstasy at the door of your bedroom, you put on a magnificent reddish-gold wool *bandhī* that came down to your knees, and you topped everything off with your silk *sannyāsa* cloth. You presented a form as stunning as the Deity when He receives a new set of clothes. At this, we were so stunned with ecstasy, we blurted out, "Śrīla Prabhupāda, you look exactly like a king!" With your head tilted back in eternal peace, you turned to us with deep gravity and said quietly, "I am much more than king." We began to understand who was standing before us. In this way, the eternal resident of Goloka Vṛndāvana departed from New Dvārakā.

Everything is still here, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Śrī Śrī Rukmiṇī-Dvārakādhīśa are still worshiped with great opulence. The *saṅkīrtana* devotees are still going out every day, amidst great obstacles, to give your purports (your emotional ecstasies) to the conditioned souls. The Warehouse and Mail Order are sending your books to all parts of the world in ever-increasing quantities. F.A.T.E. still produces the dioramas which were so dear to your Guru Mahārāja. Spiritual Sky continues to support the Gurukula, the future of your movement. ISKCON Cinema and Golden Avatar are going on in their endeavors to purify the eyes and ears of the conditioned souls. The Legal Department continues to wage war on the demons who dare attempt to stop your preaching, and the Bhakta Program is continually guiding surrendered souls to the lotus feet of your representatives. Because you spoke about Kṛṣṇa twenty-four hours a day, the Press has so many tapes that it can continue to produce Your Divine Grace's books for many years to come, and what to speak of other languages. The fact that nothing has decreased since your disappearance is proof of your potency. From the days in Rādhā-Dāmodara temple, to the Bowery, to the garages of Los Angeles, to the creation of a worldwide movement, you have with unlimited determination proceeded to fulfill the orders of your Guru Mahārāja. You are so powerful that you are still managing the Society, despite the fact that you have shed your external body. You have created powerful representatives, and through their youthful bodies your preaching continues. You once admitted that when you wrote your purports, you considered that they would have to be appropriate for the next ten thousand years. This unlimited potency is gradually being revealed to your sincere disciples, and your potency will be realized more and more as time goes on. Lord Caitanya directly sent you to save the entire world. You have given us everything we need to carry out this awesome task. We simply have to remain faithful to all your instructions and become your tools in serving your Guru Mahārāja.

Please don't be in anxiety, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Your pure representatives have taken up the difficult task of managing your society. Everything is going on nicely by your mercy. But we must admit that some of us have a motive behind our service. We are hoping that if we serve you nicely, Kṛṣṇa will be pleased and will send us to that place where you are now executing your pastimes. Then we can once again see you laugh so hard that your whole body shakes. We can again hear your clear voice pierce the stillness of the morning with Bhaktivedanta purports, and we can once again meditate on the movement of your shoes as you proceed eternally on your morning walk. You once told us that we would have ISKCON in the spiritual sky. We all anxiously await that day.

*Your eternal servants,*

*The residents of New Dvārakā-dhāma*

## Manipur

*oṁ ajñāna-timirāndhasya jñānāñjana-śalākayā*  
*cakṣur unmīlitaṁ yena tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

**"I was born in the darkest ignorance, and my spiritual master opened my eyes with the torch of knowledge. I offer my respectful obeisances unto him."**

Śrīla Prabhupāda, your appearance on this planet was just like a flash of lightning, but in this flash you have illuminated everything: that we are eternal spiritual sparks, part and parcel of the Lord; that material existence is *duḥkhālayam aśāśvatam,* temporary and full of miseries. We have no qualifications at all to be able to understand your transcendental qualities, and we have no appreciation of your mercy. Like the moths who are attracted by fire and killed, we are being attracted by *māyā,* only to be mercilessly kicked. But you are the manifestation of Rādhārāṇī, the mercy feature of the Lord, and by your mercy you are freeing us from painful material existence and engaging us in the loving service of Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, although you were physically unable to visit this Vaiṣṇava state of Manipur, your divine voice–a voice that carries the saffron particles of dust from the lotus feet of the Lord–will always be heard here through your books. Being descendants of saintly persons described in the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* all the Vaiṣṇavas of Manipur shall continue their Vaiṣṇava spirit, revitalized by your unequivocal and detailed instructions. By following your divine precepts, we shall be able to have direct association with Their Lordships Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa.

In your purports to the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* you have said that when the spiritual master appears, he brings order to chaos, and when he leaves, the chaos returns. But as long as we constantly look to your instructions and always remain faithful to you, you will always remain with us, and by your presence in the form of your *vāṇī,* the order which you have established in society will continue for thousands of years to come.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, your mercy is all that we are made of. We beg that you always keep us under your two lotus feet and give us the strength to fulfill your desire–to make Manipur the first all-Vaiṣṇava state in the world.

*Your eternal servants,*

Sarvabhāvana dāsa adhikārī / Mahamān dāsa brahmacārī / Manu dāsa brahmacārī / Vanamālī dāsa brahmacārī / Puruṣottama dāsa brahmacārī / Bhakta Goṣṭhavihārī / Bhakta Ibotomba / Bhakta Ibopishak / Bhakta Ibochouba / Bhakta Principal Birmangol / Bhaktin Piryobala Devi

## Mauritius

When, oh, when will that day be mine?  
When my offenses ceasing, taste for the name increasing,  
When in my heart will your mercy shine,  
When, oh, when will that day be mine ?

That day will be mine at my Guru's command,  
Not one minute sooner; it's all in his hand.  
When he's pleased by my labor the Lord he'll entreat,  
Then treasure revealed, the Lord's lotus feet.  
Our Guru's position, an unscalable peak,  
We can but marvel and constantly speak.  
In a furious ocean constantly dashed,  
Save by him, the captain, we hopelessly thrashed.

The demigods pray for one human birth,  
They hope to meet you again on the earth,  
This dog awaits sorrowfully and eagerly looks,  
Can I stay here too and distribute your books?

A darkness intense pervades my stone heart,  
Only you, Śrī Guru, can break it apart.  
Vivasvān is confined to the external sphere,  
His rays are but spoiled when they venture in here.

This darkness amounts to a mountain of dust,  
Which is sin upon sin from long ages past.  
Vāyu cleanses the universe with wind and with air,  
But to remove this dust mountain he could only despair.

To the justice of Yamarāja all must submit,  
Now under your feet and instructions he'll quit.  
The lord of the oceans, Varuṇa, can stand  
Amidst birth and death with you in command.

The demigods control all space, matter, and fate,  
All directions and residences, ruling, so great,  
But to enter Goloka is to you entrusted,  
I pay my obeisances, Śrī Guru, dear Prabhupāda.

*Your disciples at New Śvetadvīpa-dhāma (ISKCON Mauritius),*

Śrīniketana dāsa / Kapila dāsa / Śaṅkhaśa dāsa / Naṭavara dāsa / Somendranātha dāsa

## Mayapur

**"Just Try to Understand"**

"Just Try to Understand"–Śrīla Prabhupāda's familiar refrain.  
Just try to understand his effort to transport us to Kṛṣṇa 's transcendental land.  
Contemplate his mercy, indiscriminate as the rain.

Just try to understand our position,  
We, just like moths, who had already entered the flame.  
We were already burned to ashes in māyā's dark, hopeless night  
When Śrīla Prabhupāda, on guru and Kṛṣṇa's order, set out on his compassionate transcendental flight.  
Just try to understand Śrīla Prabhupāda's spiritual qualities, which were just like jewels;  
His endeavor to "wash the coal," to deliver an entire universe of fools;  
His endeavor to gradually bring us to the ultimate, supreme goal,  
Which neither we desired, and neither did we know.

We, contemplating our desires, attached and full of lust,  
Met our fortune in His Divine Grace's saṅkīrtana thrust.  
Undecisive, petty, and weak, devoid of bhakti and a future bleak,  
Lazy, crazy, dull, and disturbed,  
Surrendered to māyā, following the deathlike herd.  
– Then enter Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda, who arrived on Kṛṣṇa's order of descent,  
To save the day and save the world from a course hell-bent.

Just try to understand the Supreme Absolute Truth,  
Curved in three places and holding a flute–  
Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa, the Supreme Person, the all-attractive, beautiful youth.  
Just try to understand the power at Śrīla Prabhupāda's command,  
For he is no less than Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s right hand–Namo mahā-vadānyāya Śrī Māyāpur Candrodaya,  
The most merciful moonlike avatāra.

*And just try to understand that Śrīla Prabhupāda is Lord Caitanya's brightest MERCY STAR!*

*The devotees of Śrī Māyāpur Candrodaya Mandira*

## Melbourne

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*Please accept our most humble obeisances at your divine lotus feet.

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādī-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

**"I offer my respectful obeisances unto His Divine Grace A. C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda, who is very dear to Lord Kṛṣṇa, having taken shelter at His lotus feet. Our respectful obeisances are unto you, O spiritual master, servant of Sarasvatī Gosvāmī. You are kindly preaching the message of Lord Caitanya-deva and are delivering the Western countries, which are filled with impersonalism and voidism."**

While pondering the miracle of your appearance and activities, we are speechless before your wondrous, blissful, all-knowing, soul-changing countenance. Certainly you were disguised as a meek Indian holy man. Yet you let forth an ocean of knowledge of Kṛṣṇa which is flooding the world with love for Him.

We are so insignificant. We saw you. We heard you. You smiled and spoke serene, nectarean knowledge, just as Lord Kṛṣṇa spoke to Arjuna in the *Bhagavad-gītā,* which you described to be just like a river of nectar flowing into Arjuna's ears. But we have no comprehension of your greatness. You clarified all doubts about *gurus* and teachers. A real spiritual master is in disciplic succession from Kṛṣṇa Himself. He always repeats the words of Kṛṣṇa and the previous *ācāryas.* He has complete control of the senses (tongue, belly, genital, mind, anger, and words). He always thinks of Kṛṣṇa. He is always chanting Kṛṣṇa's glories. He has understood the conclusions of Vedic knowledge and is able to convince others of them. He dispells the darkness of illusion just by his words and presence. He is very kind to the fallen souls. He is the representative of Kṛṣṇa–Kṛṣṇa's mercy manifestation. He is nondifferent from the Supersoul seated in everyone's heart (from whom all knowledge, remembrance, and forgetfulness come). The purpose of life, realizing God, can be achieved by his mercy alone. By faith in him, by pleasing him, one can know the Supreme Lord. These are some of the features of a real spiritual master. You are all of these things, and more. Who could doubt your position as spiritual master, having heard these descriptions from you, which are just according to the scriptural guidelines?

But still, there is wonder in our hearts, for you are incredible, amazing. Of spiritual masters, do some stand supreme? After all, there are many spiritual masters in the disciplic chain, and still more in the other disciplic lines. Lord Kṛṣṇa came about five thousand years ago, and later there were numerous spiritual masters–Madhva, Rāmānuja, Nimbārka, Viṣṇu Svāmī . Lord Caitanya came and declared the *saṅkīrtana* movement would spread all over the world.

*pṛthivīte ache yata nagarādi-grāma*  
*sarvatra pracār haibe mora nāma*  
*\[CB Antya-khaṇḍa 4.126\]*

Rūpa Gosvāmī declared,

*namo mahā-vadānyāya kṛṣṇa-prema-pradāya te*  
*kṛṣṇāya kṛṣṇa-caitanya-nāmne gaura-tviṣe namaḥ*

**"I offer my respectful obeisances unto Lord Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, who is the most merciful** ***avatāra.*** **He is even more merciful than Kṛṣṇa Himself, because He is freely distributing pure love of God."**

After Lord Caitanya were the six Gosvāmīs of Vṛndāvana, the great spiritual masters like Jagannātha dāsa Bābājī, Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura, Gaura Kiśora dāsa Bābājī, and your own spiritual master, Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Gosvāmī.

But you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, are representing them all. You are even more merciful than Lord Caitanya, for you alone have taken up the message of the *ācāryas* and spread the mercy of Lord Caitanya all over the world. By Lord Caitanya's mercy you touched your feet to the United States, South America, Canada, Mexico, England, France, Germany, Australia, Fiji, Indonesia, Malaysia, Japan, Hong Kong, Africa, Russia, and all the other places you went. And you personally inspired thousands of disciples to assist you in preparing, publishing, and distributing over eighty-five million books in over thirty languages. You inspired them to distribute uncountable quantities of the supremely tasting pleasure food, *kṛṣṇa-prasāda.* By your personal guidance they started and still maintain temples, farms, restaurants, schools, and traveling *saṅkīrtana* parties all over the world.

The wondrous gifts of your kindness are ever expanding, revealing a greater and greater plan. You inspired some to paint incredible paintings of Kṛṣṇa, revealing the most confidential world of His pastimes. You inspired some to make films, slide shows, tapes, and television programs, bringing Kṛṣṇa's pastimes into schools, homes, and offices. Even the demons who hate Kṛṣṇa must think of Him constantly now. Wherever there is an opportunity, in the airports, cities, even in the mailboxes, Kṛṣṇa is dancing there. You inspired some to worship the Lord in His Deity form in the temples, where He appeared by your pure devotion in wondrous, heartrending beauty. What mercy upon the fallen souls who somehow get the fortunate chance to come before Him in our temples!

You inspired some to teach young children to be fresh, pure, and intelligent in transcendental knowledge, to preach even more to the people of this fallen age of Kali. You inspired scientists to form an institute to unseat the atheistic test-tube kings. You inspired parties of devotees to chant Hare Kṛṣṇa all day in the world's city streets.

We can't help wonder, Śrīla Prabhupāda, how you did all of this and more. Your rose each morning at one or two a.m. to translate your books. You went for a walk at sunrise and gave us so much instruction, then you gave lectures to us in the temples. You instructed ISKCON leaders, met guests, consoled parents, defeated philosophers, took bath, *prasāda,* and rest in the afternoon. At night you spoke on and on, to more guests or devotees, and rested only a few hours at night.

Although always busy, Śrīla Prabhupāda, you always seemed relaxed and able to get everything done. What was it that made your speaking so powerful that just by preaching in so many ways your words carried the impact that moved mountains of impediments from the path of spreading Kṛṣṇa consciousness, and turned the hearts of so many, and still do so today?

You once wrote, "There are two kinds of strength– *daiva* and *puruṣākāra. Daiva* refers to strength achieved from the Transcendence, and *puruṣākāra* refers to the strength organized by one's own intelligence and power. Transcendental power is always superior to the power of the materialist." *(Bhāg.* 9.10.19purport) Herein lies the key to understanding your strength. The *daiva* power is the power of Kṛṣṇa, the reservoir of unlimited strength. Just as Arjuna overcame insurmountable odds by defeating so many warriors in the Battle of Kurukṣetra, you stepped into America, alone, and defeated the invincible grip *māyā* holds over the people of this age, who are in a fixed trance of sense gratification. You could do it because your power is the power of Kṛṣṇa.

As stated in *Caitanya-caritāmṛta (Antya* 2.13-14), "Unless one is empowered by the Supreme Personality of Godhead, Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, one cannot spread the holy names of the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mahā-mantra* throughout the world. Persons who do so are empowered. Therefore they are sometimes called *āveśa-avatāra,* or incarnations, for they are endowed with the power of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu." Also, it is written in *Caitanya-caritāmṛta (Madhya* 19.114), "The Supreme Lord has multipotencies, which the Lord bestows on His fortunate devotees. The Lord has a special potency by which He spreads the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement. This is also explained in the *Caitanya-caritāmṛta* (*Antya* 7.11)*, kṛṣṇa-śakti vinā nahe tāra pravartana:* one cannot spread the holy name of Kṛṣṇa without being specifically empowered by Lord Kṛṣṇa. A devotee who receives this power from the Lord must be considered most fortunate. The Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement is spreading to enlighten people about their real position, their original relationship with Kṛṣṇa. One requires Kṛṣṇa's special power in order to be able to do this.... The Lord again comes as Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu to induce people to take to Kṛṣṇa consciousness. The Lord also empowers a special devotee to teach people their constitutional position."

So, Śrīla Prabhupāda, we must conclude that you are an *āveśa-avatāra,* or incarnation of the Lord. You are a very, very special devotee, whose position is inconceivable to us. You are a very, very dear servant of Kṛṣṇa, come to help us due to your limitless kindness or causeless mercy. How dear you are to Kṛṣṇa and what your relationship with Him is, we cannot truly say, but of all the spiritual masters, Kṛṣṇa empowered you to take Kṛṣṇa consciousness all over the world.

You are called "Prabhupāda," for many masters sit at your feet. These masters now strike forth your message so boldly that the world shakes under the spiritual might. Watching all of this, we can only wonder in awe as we dance into your eternal *saṅkīrtana* party. You have gone, but your mercy is just beginning to show its effect. And as we see Kṛṣṇa consciousness sprouting everywhere (for you planted innumerable spiritual seeds), we know you are still here with us, your kind and gentle face looking over your devotees with a beaming smile as they dance, chanting Hare Kṛṣṇa in cooperation in the great *saṅkīrtana* across the world. Being pleased upon us, you are the life of our eternal bliss and ecstasy. You are ever present, ever merciful, most gentle, overpoweringly wonderful, and very dear to Lord Kṛṣṇa. Just by speaking the transcendental sounds of Kṛṣṇa's name and knowledge you benedicted the world, and your words are so powerful that by speaking them or publishing them we give everyone the same direct mercy of Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu. We revel in the ecstatic power of your words.

*Your tiny servants of ISK.CON Melbourne,*

## Mexico

**"You May Either Live or Die"**

A saintly person may either live or die, for during his life he is engaged in serving the Lord, and after his death he also serves the Lord. Thus this life and the next are the same for a saintly devotee, for in both he serves the Lord. The pure devotee lives constantly in the abode of the Supreme Lord, even in the present body, which apparently belongs to this world.

The pure devotee is not exactly on the bodily plane, since he is absorbed in the transcendental thought of the Supreme. Even though a pure devotee is present on this mortal planet, he is here in relation to the Lord for engagement in transcendental loving service, and not for any material cause. A living entity can live either on the material plane or in the transcendental abode of the Lord in accordance with his existential condition. The conditional changes of the living entity are explained in the *Caitanya-caritāmṛta* in the instructions given to Śrīla Rūpa Gosvāmī by Lord Śrī Caitanya.

*brahmāṇḍa bhramite kona bhāgyavān jīva*  
*guru-kṛṣṇa-prasāde pāya bhakti-latā-bīja*

The living entities all over the universe are enjoying the effects of the respective fruitive results of their own work, life after life, and out of all of them, some may be influenced by the association of pure devotees. It is said that man is a social animal, and according to our association we can mold our character, just as businessmen associate with one another to develop their business capabilities. There are many different types of association, and association brings about bondage to a particular thing. Therefore it is said,

*bhavāpavargo bhramato yadā bhavej janasya tarhy acyuta sat-samāgamaḥ*  
*sat-saṅgamo yarhi tadaiva sad-gatau parāvareśe tvayi jāyate ratiḥ*

**"When a person wandering throughout the universes becomes eligible for liberation from material existence, he gets an opportunity to associate with a devotee."**

When he associates with devotees, his attraction for self-realization and Godhead are awakened. In this regard, in the *Caitanya-caritāmṛta* it is said,

*sādhu-saṅge kṛṣṇa-bhaktye śraddhā yadi haya*  
*bhakti-phala 'prema' haya saṁsāra yāya kṣaya*

**"By associating with a devotee, one awakens his faith in devotional service to Kṛṣṇa. Because of devotional service, one's dormant love for Kṛṣṇa awakens, and thus one's material, conditional existence comes to an end."**

One cannot acquire devotional service by any amount of pious activities. Devotional service can be attained only by the mercy of a pure devotee. Without a pure devotee's mercy, one cannot even escape the bondage of material existence. It is said in *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,*

*naiṣāṁ matis tāvad urukramāṅghriṁ*  
*spṛśaty anarthāpagamo yad-arthaḥ*  
*mahīyasāṁ pāda-rajo-’bhiṣekaṁ*  
*niṣkiñcanānāṁ na vṛṇīta yāvat*

**"Unless human society accepts the dust of the lotus feet of a great** ***mahātmā,*** **a great devotee who has nothing to do with material possessions, mankind cannot turn its attention to the lotus feet of Kṛṣṇa. Those lotus feet can vanquish all unwanted miserable conditions of material life."**

It is the verdict of all *śāstras* and great personalities that by service to a pure devotee, one attains the path of liberation.

*sarva-deśa-kāla-daśāya janera kartavya*  
*guru-pāśe sei bhakti praṣṭavya, śrotavya*

**"It is therefore the duty of every man in every country, in every circumstance, and at all times to accept the bona fide spiritual master, question him about devotional service, and listen to him explain the process."**

*tāra madhye 'sthāvara' 'jaṅgama'–dui bheda*  
*jaṅgame tiryak-jala-sthalacara vibheda*

**"The unlimited living entities can be divided into two divisions: those that can move and those which cannot move."**

Both of them are lamenting because Śrīla Prabhupāda has departed.

Bhūmi is lamenting because she is no longer feeling his divine lotus feet on her back. And he is no longer relieving her itching sensation, because the end of his cane is no longer scratching her back.

Vāyu is not very pleased because he cannot carry Śrīla Prabhupāda's divine words anymore.

The living beings within the sea are hopeless because His Divine Grace is no longer walking on the shore.

The nonmoving living entities in the park are hopeless of liberation because His Divine Grace no longer goes there every morning.

Vṛndāvana is feeling pain because its most exalted preacher is no longer walking on her streets. Also, all the vrajavāsīs are crying out loud out of separation from His Divine Grace.

Kṛṣṇa-Balarāma are feeling separation because His Divine Grace no longer receives Them every morning.

O brothers, let us, on this Vyāsa-pūjā day, renew our promise to his Divine Grace to dedicate our lives to spreading his mission, and let us bow down at his lotus feet, begging his mercy.

Let us beg His Divine Grace's mercy to be able to compose nice prayers in his glorification on this divine day. Our only inspiration can come from his divine lotus feet. May His Divine Grace give us protection in all circumstances, because he is the witness of our lives.

This dangerous ocean of darkness and ignorance cannot be crossed by anyone, even if one is a great demigod or a great sage (and certainly not by those who are unintelligent like animals), without the mercy of His Divine Grace. May His Divine Grace give us the shade of his divine lotus feet.

His Divine Grace has no material birth, activities, name, form, qualities, or faults. His desire is simply to fulfill the purpose of Kṛṣṇa. He comes to the material world because of his boundless compassion. He has come to deliver the fallen souls. He is, therefore, the friend of the poor and suffering conditioned souls. We offer our respectful obeisances unto him. He is the supreme master of the devotees' life. He is always acting on transcendental existence. He is the bestower of uncontaminated happiness, and he comes from the transcendental world. Therefore we offer our respectful obeisances unto him.

We offer our respectful obeisances to His Divine Grace, who is delivering knowledge of the different forms of the Supreme Lord: the fierce form of Lord Nṛsiṁha-deva; the Lord's form as a boar, Lord Varāhadeva; the Lord's form of Dattātreya; the Lord's form of Buddha; and many other incarnations. Therefore let us surrender to his divine lotus feet.

My lord, you are the guidance of all activities. Without your mercy there is no possibility of solving the problem of doubt. Without your direction we cannot take even a step forward. This is the conclusion of all Vedic knowledge contained in the *śāstras* like the *Itihāsa, Purāṇas,* etc. You are the only shelter for us. Let us offer our respectful obeisances unto you. You were living in this material world. Your mind, however, was not attentive to the activities of the modes of nature. You are not subject to the regulative principles directed by the Vedic literatures. Because you have within your pure mind the Supreme Lord, we offer our respectful obeisances unto you.

We are being delivered from the animal condition of life. Certainly you will release us from this dangerous material existence. Aside from being extremely merciful upon us, you incessantly deliver instructions. This is because you are the external manifestation of the Paramātmā. That is acknowledged in all revealed scriptures, and therefore we offer our respectful obeisances unto you.

You are free from all material contamination, and you are always meditating upon the Supreme Personality of Godhead. Wandering for so many years in the universe, now we have the opportunity of association which is uncontaminated by the modes of nature. §rila Prabhupāda, you are the reservoir of all enlightenment. We therefore offer our respectful obeisances unto you. May His Divine Grace, who is unlimitedly merciful, bestow upon us the benediction of liberation from this present dangerous material way of life. You are an unalloyed devotee, and you have no desire other than to serve the Lord. You are simply engaged in-worshiping Him in full surrender and always hearing and chanting about His activities, which are wonderful and auspicious. Thus you are always merged in an ocean of transcendental bliss. We offer our respectful obeisances unto you.

We do not wish to live any more material life after meeting you. What is the use of external human form covered by ignorance? Now we fully desire release from material life. We simply wish to have your service and liberation from the coverings of ignorance. We offer our respectful obeisances.

Due to the illusory energy, although we are part and parcel of God, we forget our real identity because of the bodily concept of life. Therefore we offer our respectful obeisances unto you, because you are the only one who liberates us from conditional life. We take shelter at your lotus feet, O you whose glories are difficult to understand.

*Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Madana-gopāla Mandira*

## Miami

We came to this world with hopes to enjoy,  
The position of Kṛṣṇa we tried to destroy.  
Experiencing birth, death, old age, and disease,  
And always we're kicked by threefold miseries.  
Still thinking we're happy and acting like fools,  
All puffed up with knowledge we've learned in our schools.  
Addicted to senses and all that's mundane,  
Accepting as reality a world that's insane.  
Our sinful activities are too many to tell,  
Traversing a path which was leading to hell.  
But then by your mercy and unlimited grace.  
You came here to save us with Caitanya's mace.  
Following the order of your Gurudeva,  
You came to the West to find souls to save.  
With no one to help you and in your old age,  
You struggled alone till success was made.  
A perfect example has been set by you,  
How a Vaiṣṇava should serve his beloved guru.  
The age now is Kali, where irreligion grows,  
But your method is preaching–the truth you expose.  
The solution is chanting the Lord's holy name,  
And that's what you're giving as your only claim.  
So now this great mission you've given us all,  
We must beg you for strength not to stumble or fall.  
But now you have left, as some people say,  
We know you're still here to guide us each day.  
You live in your teachings and deity form too.  
But still we are crying in separation from you.

## Mombassa

**Africa's Our Stand**

Sailing from Vṛndāvana in nineteen-sixty-five,  
Ignited by the love which keeps real love alive.

Pure love to Kṛṣṇa was your message to teach.  
No compromise–dogs and hogs not beyond reach.

Sometimes you played like you wanted to give up,  
Too much difficult work. Next ship–finish up.

But we know now you knew that the dawn was to start  
That would cause we most fallen to keep you in our hearts.

But for you we could only rot and decay.  
Glory to you, Gurudeva, for helping this way!

You live as the torch of our dark cave-life.  
Without you–ignorance, bad luck, and strife.

All glories deserved by Your Revered Divine Grace.  
World fortune insured by your words by the case.

You humbly say that you deserve no credit;  
Preserving the words of your guru–no edit.

But this glory's the greatest in this modern day,  
For we see most gurus give Gītā another way.

How can we begin to hope to reimburse  
Your love for us men who were born with a curse.

This debt is so dear that our lives will be spent  
Just trying to make the first big installment.

We're directed to serve by your guiding voice.  
Now helplessly, Africa's the field of our choice.

You also went to a strange and foreign place.  
We're trying intently–how we'll crack this case?

A difficult work, being here without you,  
To give them the message of Mahāprabhu.

The people here have a fresh, friendly spirit;  
Quite a change from the land of "We don't wanna hear it."

While distributing books in these small town streets,  
Everyone takes one, and even though they eat meat,

It's not such a hindrance on their path of love.  
They seem to remember there's God up above.

Civilization has left them one step behind.  
Surprised in remote hut, your books we did find.

It gives us such joy in this faraway place  
To know that your vāṇī is setting the pace.

To follow your steps is the difficult thing.  
How can a pauper ever act like a king?

What's the key for all men and tribes to unite?  
The knowledge of ātmā can only be right.

So we're selling your books, and men seem quite willing.  
Please bless them with kṛṣṇa-prema for a shilling.

Some come to our classes to see what we do.  
But because of our lust we lack power from you.

We beg from you knowledge of a Caitanya trick  
That can bring all these dead souls back to life quick.

Please give us this knowledge, in darkness we grope,  
Undeserving we, just your mercy gives hope.

O Śrīla Prabhupāda, we know how you care,  
But helpless we feel with consciousness "out there."

Just let us keep clinging to your lotus feet.  
Of all things in life there is nothing so sweet.

Please don't let us act like crazy men and hide,  
No matter how fully our minds may be fried.

To shake hands and say "Jambo" is not sufficient.  
In making bhaktas we must be proficient.

We pray this dark land will be blessed by your light,  
And we hope that the day is not out of sight.

Caitanya predicted for ten thousand years  
Every man will be drowned who lives, breathes, and hears.

Gurudeva, you're the one who can stop our fears.  
When will our chanting culminate in real tears ?

Without you, Prabhupāda, it's difficult work  
Being pure in our lives–mind's always berserk.

Just let us continue in this jungle of sin–  
We're willing to preach to Rais Idi Amin.\*

Your purehearted goal was to make one man pure,  
And sad so much nonsense you had to endure.

Still we pray and get down and beg for results:  
Let us preach like you preached and bear all insults.

One man from this land with a staff in his hand–  
Till we see such result, Africa's our stand.

*Prabhupāda, please allow us to be your eternal servants wherever you preach,*

Nava-yogendra Svāmī / Janakīnātha dāsa / Caṇḍīdāsa dāsa / Abhayāśraya dāsa / Naṭavara dāsa / Bhakta Gaurhari / Bhakta El Amin Mohammed Kabali / Bhaktin Mrs. Slalima Jamal / Vaiṣṇavī-devī dāsī / Nara-nārāyaṇa Ṛṣi dāsa adhikārī, Vijaya-devī dāsī, Shreyash, Śrutidevī

\*President of Uganda famous for eating his opponents' livers and feeding missionaries to hungry crocodiles in Lake Victoria.

## Montreal

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

On this most auspicious day, you will find us most unqualified to glorify your transcendental qualities, hidden treasures of your pure devotion. Just like Dhruva Is Mahārāja, who fell flat on the ground, speechless, when the Lord appeared before him, we can only fall at your feet and beg you to show compassion on the awkward attempt we are about to make to praise you. Actually, the only way we can give a glimpse of what your exalted position is, is simply to repeat what you have taught us.

*guru kṛṣṇa-rūpa hana śāstrera pramāṇe*  
*guru-rūpe kṛṣṇa kṛpā karena bhakta-gaṇe*

**"According to the deliberate opinion of all revealed scriptures, the spiritual master is nondifferent from Kṛṣṇa. Lord Kṛṣṇa in the form of the spiritual master delivers His devotees." (Cc.** ***Ādi*** **1.45)**

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are the personification of the Lord's mercy. How we came to Kṛṣṇa consciousness and made progress, day by day, is due only to your unlimited potency. Just like the sun, which can separate all good elements from dirt, you were able to find whatever little spark of desire for Kṛṣṇa was left in us, and make it into a blazing fire.

Trapped in a cage of burning lust, we could not find happiness any more than a fish out of water. All of our material qualifications would show to be only fallible soldiers in understanding who we are. The clouds of impersonalism and materialism had covered our original consciousness, and we could not even realize our fallen condition as *mlecchas* and *yavanas.* But by your pure desire to distribute Lord Caitanya's unlimited mercy and please your spiritual master, you were able to attract us to real spiritual life. Just like iron becomes gold by contact with the touchstone, so we immediately started to experience the sublime nectar of Kṛṣṇa consciousness by your association.

One day Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura made a prophecy that a great spiritual master would soon come and spread the glories of Lord Kṛṣṇa all over the world. So now we can understand that you are the object of this prophecy, and that you have fulfilled the purport of the mission of all the great *ācāryas* and incarnations of the past. Everywhere, in all parts of the world, people are hearing about Kṛṣṇa, taking Kṛṣṇa's *prasāda,* associating with devotees, and chanting the holy names, because of you.

Even though you have subtracted your physical presence from us, we can only feel your presence more and more in the ever-growing amazement of what you have left us. Just as we can perceive the presence of the Lord in the manifestations of His energies, we cannot forget you even for a moment, being completely immersed in the ocean of devotional service to your lotus feet. Be pleased to bestow upon us the benediction to serve you eternally. We cannot repay you for what you have given us, but we want to please you by first of all becoming ourselves pure devotees, and thus bring so many others, all the inhabitants of all the universes, to the altar of your lotus feet. May that be so.

*Your most unworthy servants,*

ISKCON devotees of Montreal's Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Manohara Mandira

## Murwillumbah

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādī-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Beloved spiritual father, please accept my eternal prostrated obeisances at your divine lotus feet. Please excuse me from all offenses and give me shelter from the clutches of *māyā's* embrace.

As I read your biography and hear of your experiences and difficulties in the early days, I feel ashamed at my feeble attempts to spread your mission. Fearlessly you pursued the task given to you by your Guru Mahārāja. Being a pure, unalloyed devotee of the Lord, you depended wholeheartedly upon the Supreme Lord in all circumstances. Because of your absolute conviction in Kṛṣṇa being the Supreme Personality of Godhead, there were never any real obstacles to your mission. Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have performed the greatest of all miracles. In the midst of the darkness of Kali-yuga, singlehandedly you have delivered the world from the shackles of *māyā*. Somehow, by the mercy of the Lord, I was able to associate with you. First you came to me in your books. Then for a few short years I was fortunate to personally associate with you on some precious occasions. Now your association comes in the form of your instructions. O master, please be merciful to me so I can take these instructions as my life and soul. Without your guidance there is no life. I beg for your mercy so that my intelligence becomes rightly situated in Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Without your mercy I am destitute. I have no good qualities and have no ability to preach. But I do sincerely desire to surrender my life to you, my beloved father.

On behalf of the inhabitants of New Govardhana farm, I would like to express our deephearted gratitude for your giving us Kṛṣṇa consciousness, the perfection of life. Only after the performance of millions of lifetimes of pious activities and association with thousands of pure devotees have we become qualified to receive a moment of your association.

## Nairobi

All glories to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are the spiritual master of the entire universe.

Jaya Prabhupāda  
There is no one as pure as you.  
In all the things that you do.  
In every move that you make,  
There is never any mistake,

Jaya Prabhupāda  
You appeared like a lightning flash,  
You came and went so fast.  
Counting your glories we've begun,  
Your glorious deeds must be sung.

Jaya Prabhupāda  
The simplest move that you make  
Causes the demons' hearts to shake.  
And by the purity of your glance  
You make the devotees want to dance.

Jaya Prabhupāda,  
Against may a you made war,  
Alone you pounded down her door.  
The holy name your main weapon,  
Then you expanded into eleven.

Jaya Prabhupāda,  
With the tenderness of your touch  
Stone-covered hearts became crushed.  
By your divine grace we learn love,  
Material illusion you are above.

O Prabhupāda  
With surrender and submission by us  
In our heart you will put loving service.  
You are the medium, you guide what we do,  
Kṛṣṇa will accept us if we try to please you.

Thank you, Prabhupāda  
On the horizon, the sun morningtime rose,  
So the time and place of your pastimes you chose.  
As the sun goes down at the end of the day  
You completed your mission and then went away.

Jaya, Śrīla Prabhupāda  
You changed our lives as if touched by a wand,  
Of your spiritual form you made us so fond.  
To see that form our hearts now lament  
Our heart asks us where that lotus face went.

*Devotees of ISKCON Nairobi*

## New Orleans

*Jayate jagad-guru!*

We fall at your lotus feet, simply desiring to be allowed to do so eternally. You are saving this world by your causeless mercy. You have picked us up from the lowest regions of consciousness. Your glories are unlimited!

O universal spiritual master, you have already changed the face of the earth! We see that your plans for the future of this movement are inevitable. Our position is simply that we can take part or not.

Your tremendous plan for a Kṛṣṇa conscious world is being enacted! "The brainwashing books are already out," and we will distribute un-limitedly more as you desire. The farms are here just waiting for the inhabitation! All over the world people are realizing that this is the positive alternative to their futile lives. Your society of *brāhmaṇas* is here, ready and waiting to receive the masses!

Śrīla Prabhupāda, we can see you manifested in the ISKCON devotees. They are all worshipable, by your mercy. They are taking up the business you gave them, and what to speak of the powerful *gurus* you have appointed! They are undoubtedly pure devotees, especially empowered by you to preach. We especially see you in them. You have given us everything by your mercy, and now we are using this equipment against the forces of *māyā.*

tvaṁ naḥ sandarśito dhātrā dustaraṁ nistitīrṣatām  
kaliṁ sattva-haraṁ puṁsāṁ karṇa-dhāra ivārṇavam

"We think that we have met Your Grace by the will of Providence, and thus we accept you as captain of the ship for those who desire to cross over the dangerous ocean of Kali, which destroys all the good qualities of a human being."

Gurudeva, we are puppets in your hands. Please make us dance. Your mercy is all that we are made of, and if we cannot obtain it, we are less than nothing. Please, O Śrīla Prabhupāda, give us the mercy of your glance!

*Your servants at Saṅkīrtana-pura,*

## New Vrindaban

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Every year it becomes more difficult to say something in your glorification. Your greatness is constantly increasing, and by comparison we are becoming more and more insignificant. So how can we honor you properly?

As you have so often quoted, the *Vedas* enjoin *tad-vijñān*ā*rthaṁ sa gurum evābhigacchet.* In order to understand the goal of life one must approach the bona fide spiritual master. The truth of this assertion has been demonstrated so clearly by your life and mission. Before your coming, this country, which is supposed to be the most advanced by all material calculations, was simply producing a class of frustrated and disillusioned young men. They were wealthy, beautiful, from good families, and well-educated, but they did not know the goal of life, and therefore they were all suffering miserably.

Out of your great compassion you came to the West and preached purely the message of *Bhagavad-gītā,* as it is, without interpretation or alteration. You have delivered such fallen souls as ourselves to the lotus feet of Kṛṣṇa. You are the savior of the entire universe, and this fact is becoming increasingly manifest, beyond anyone's imagination.

There is nothing we can do to add to your fame. Our only wish is that you allow us to always be engaged in serving your lotus feet. Your service is the sum and substance of our lives; we have no other goal beyond that.

*The Inmates of New Vrindaban,*

## New York

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādī-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Lead-colored streets match the tattered walls,  
while old gift-shop signs hang half worn.  
Who could imagine that this dismal place  
would be first blessed by your golden form?

Dog stool, exhaust fumes, and stale beer  
add to the stench of New York City,  
yet amid this swill, over a decade ago,  
walked the sweet rose, a pure devotee.

The echoes of sirens, horns, and vulgar cries  
form the noise produced by the lame.  
From among the sounds of the blue-jeaned boys  
came your voice, singing the Lord's holy name.

Slippery sidewalks of snow-covered drunks  
marked the path you had to beat,  
but boldly you strode, touching your beads,  
shelter by Govinda 's lotus feet.

You spoke the words from the Bhagavad-gītā  
in an eloquent and changeless manner.  
These verses cut down, in a gentle way,  
our Māyāvāda and voidist banter.

Every step you placed among the fallen  
turned into a dance that uplifted their hearts.  
You taught us that step, the Vaikuṇṭha gait;  
with hands raised, we tried to follow the part.

Every word you spoke was a transcendental song  
that cleansed the listener's mind of alt pollution.  
All the words you wrote were divinely inspired,  
and they have inspired a spiritual revolution.

*Your eternal servants at Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Govinda Mandira,*

## Ottawa

*Our beloved Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

By your great mercy you are allowing countless *jīvas* to cross over the ocean of nescience on the transcendental boat of your instructions. Since you are fully invested with the power of devotional service, your association benedicts everyone with transcendental; enlivenment. Those fortunate souls who follow in the footsteps of such a *mahā-bhāgavata* as you find no difficulty in relishing the bliss of serving those instructions. Under your direction, we are receiving the highest satisfaction as we enter deeper and deeper into the ecstatic *samādhi* of constant engagement in the service of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu.

You are the bona fide spiritual master because you are teaching the science of *bhakti-yoga* in the disciplic succession of the Madhva-Gaudiya Vaiṣṇava *sampradāya.* All your disciples know this because we've always seen you one-hundred-percent fixed in spreading Śrī Kṛṣṇa's glories, never demanding any respect for yourself. Like Mādhavendra Purī, reputation ordained by providence is coming to you. Through your transcendental literatures your glories will become known throughout the entire universe as we distribute them more and more. You are certainly of the status of such exalted personalities as Śrīla Vyāsadeva, Kṛṣṇadāsa Kavirāja Mahārāja, and the six Gosvāmīs of Vṛndāvana. Your books were dictated within your heart directly from Kṛṣṇa Himself, and therefore each word is surcharged with *bhakti-śakti,* to convince us that we are not these material bodies but actually eternal loving servants of Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa.

Śrīla Prabhupāda! You are the master at whose feet all other masters sit, and it is very rare for such a personality as you to appear in this material world. You are empowering us to offer shelter to millions of people. The might and power of your words dispel the darkness of their ignorance. As your disciples and the disciples of your disciples, our happiness lies in undeviatingly following your instructions to extend Śrī Caitanya's preaching activities all over the world. Our advancement in Kṛṣṇa consciousness depends upon our taking to the spirit of your instructions. By your vivid example, in just a few short years we have witnessed how you became most successful by taking the spirit of your Guru Mahārāja Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta’s instructions. You took his instruction to print and distribute the Vaiṣṇava literatures as your life and soul.

Having suffered so much before meeting you, we've become aware of the progress of Kali-yuga, and we are trying to share in your anxiety to not allow even one soul to remain in this universe who is not chanting Kṛṣṇa's name. We pray to become fearless in spreading your message. By developing just a drop of your compassion for the suffering souls we can be triumphant over *māyā* in all directions. Taking shelter and inspiration from your lotus feet is to take up the spirit of your instructions. And to take up the spirit of your instructions means to distribute your books and preach like anything all over the world. By making a clean place in our hearts for Lord Kṛṣṇa, Lord Caitanya will also reside there. Before Śrī Kṛṣṇa gives us pure love of Godhead, He must be sure that we are cent-percent devoted to your instructions.

Our dear lord and master, we take shelter of you. If you so desire, we can speak, and if you don't–then what can we say? We are totally dependent on you for our existence. How could we feel that you are not with us? As you once said, "My spiritual master is not lying in the *samādhi,* but at every moment he is by my side, and he is always helping me." To see Kṛṣṇa consciousness spreading throughout the world like wildfire, we know that you are always by our side.

## Panama

*oṁ ajñāna-timirāndhasya jñānāñjana-śalākayā*  
*cakṣur unmīlitaṁ yena tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

**"I offer my respectful obeisances unto ray spiritual master, who has opened my eyes, which were blinded by the darkness of ignorance, with the torchlight of knowledge."**

*Dearmost Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

On this most auspicious day of your divine appearance in this world, we would like to worship you with this offering and offer our roost fallen obeisances at your lotus feet. For us to completely describe your glories is not possible, for even if one chanted for millions of years, he could not even begin to fully glorify your mercy, your patience, your humility, your strength, and your supreme devotion to Lord Kṛṣṇa.

Your causeless mercy was so wonderfully exhibited when you accepted the great austerity of leaving Lord Kṛṣṇa's land of Vṛndāvana and coming to the most horrible places, just to save the fallen conditioned souls. Just as when the sun shines on urine, it is not contaminated but instead purifies that place, you came into the most contaminated places, and instead of being contaminated, you purified them. And after coming into our association, you did not just leave us, but exhibited such patience by kindly guiding us at every step, just like small children. You tolerated so many offenses and so much nonsense, and remained our best well-wisher.

And when we finally began to understand a spark of your glories and glorified you in an incomplete way, you remained the example of humility, simply a servant of the servant of the servant of Kṛṣṇa. But oh, what a servant. You fully understood the desires of your master and always acted to fulfill those desires. Lord Caitanya says in His *Śrī Śikṣāṣṭaka,* "One should chant the holy name of the Lord in a humble state of mind, thinking oneself lower than the straw in the street; one should be more tolerant than a tree, devoid of all sense of false prestige, and be ready to offer all respect to others. In such a state of mind one can chant the holy name of the Lord constantly." And you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, were always chanting the holy name.

As your disciples, we took shelter of your inconceivable strength and watched you defeat one philosophy after another, one rascal after another, and even within our own minds, one doubt after another. Even though, by material standards, your body was old, you exhibited such spiritual strength that no rascal could stand in your presence.

Your supreme devotion to Lord Kṛṣṇa was exhibited by your very life. Every action was done as an offering to Kṛṣṇa, every word was to glorify Kṛṣṇa, and every instruction was to help others to come to Kṛṣṇa. In such a world filled with impersonalism and voidism, your devotion shone through like the sunshine, and you established *bhakti-yoga* as supreme. When we were engaged in serving our senses with devotion you taught us how to dovetail this devotional service to Kṛṣṇa. Surely you are the personification of devotion to Kṛṣṇa.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, please accept our insignificant prayers and consider us your humble servants.

*Your devotees at ISKCON Panama*

## Philadelphia

*añjali añjali bhari' phele catur-diśe*  
*daridra kuḍāñā khāya, mālākāra hāse*

**"The transcendental gardener, Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, distributed handful after handful of fruit in all directions, and when the poor hungry people ate the fruit, the gardener smiled with great pleasure." (Cc.** ***Ādi*** **9.30)**

To you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, the topmost swanlike devotee of the Lord, who have breathed life into a dead world, given vision to those who were blind, and created a society of devotees from the refuse of a forgotten age–to you we offer our most humble obeisances thousands upon thousands of times.

Here is your garden, now fourteen years old, filled with the ripe, rich bounty of your labor. Your own self is manifested here, your countless fine qualities covering the land like an endless carpet of fresh grasses beneath trees laden with fruits and blossoms. These trees that shade ISKCON are your senior disciples, stalwart Vaiṣṇavas, decorated with the twenty-six devotional qualities and rooted solidly in the firm ground of the *Vedānta-darśana.*

The air here is cool, fresh, and soothing to the touch. Birds such as the *cakravāka,* peacock, and crane move here and there, creating a joyful atmosphere with their cooing and cries, just as the ISK.CON temples are constantly filled with the joyful sounds of *kṛṣṇa-kīrtana.* In this garden a lake displays varieties of lotus flowers, attracting the attention of swans, *kāraṇḍava,* and other water birds. You have explained in *Kṛṣṇa* book that, as there are different types of human beings according to the three modes of material nature, there are similarly different types of birds, bees, trees, etc. But this lake, which is your own pure mind, has attracted only birds like swans and cranes, who enjoy clear waters and lotus flowers.

There are bumblebees humming all about, and the trees appear jolly due to the sweet humming. ISKCON has its bumblebeelike children, and if they have been properly trained according to your instructions, we find they are only interested in the honey, the sweet nectar of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Their presence renders the entire Vaiṣṇava community jolly, for they are distinct from the misguided, troublesome children of the material world.

The sky above this garden is unique, for here the sun of Śrī Kṛṣṇa and the moon of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu are visible simultaneously. The air is clean, as are the hearts of your disciples, and due to ample blossoming flowers there is a fresh, aromatic breeze, giving relief to persons scorched by the blazing fire of material existence. At every step, remembrance of your lotus feet is the food that nourishes this garden. Protected by high walls of śāstric evidence, it is impenetrable by *māyā's* influence, for any inconsistencies are easily seen and rooted out. Personal ambitions, lust, and greed have no room to grow, no place to reside.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, when you took us from the cesspool and brought us to this transcendental place, it was difficult to imagine a time when you would not be personally present to guide and reassure us. The thought of your departure was a sinister cloud that loomed off in the distance, threatening to blot out the sun. But clearly you have not left; now we know that you have remained with us as your instructions, as your eleven representatives, and as ISKCON itself. In fact, everything serves to remind us of you, and we live in service to your memory. You are the sounds that fill our ears, the words from our mouths, the thoughts in our minds. Everything has come from you, and everything finds its perfection in you–how we sit, eat, sleep, see, speak. Perception of the world is through your senses, contemplation through your mind, speech through your words. You are our wealth, our sustenance, and our happiness. When we preach, the pleasure we feel is in knowing that preaching is your pleasure; and so with everything we do and know, for our lives are fully dependent on you.

If you see fit, then kindly accept these insignificant souls as your own property, and do with them as you wish. In some small way, we are hoping to be of service to the propagation of the *saṅkīrtana* movement.

*Your servants at New Nīlācala-dhāma,*

## Philippines

*My Dear Spiritual Father,*

Please accept our most insignificant obeisances at your lotus feet a thousand billion times, and then again. Except for the dust of your lotus feet, there is nothing to be had in this material world. Wherever one goes in this material world, it is the same thing–temporary pleasure only. However, when one goes to your lotus feet and gathers some dust, he becomes eternally satisfied, and real pleasure begins. Why? Because your lotus feet are not of this material world. Just like the governor who sometimes visits the prison house, you came to this material world. The governor has no business in the prison, but he feels compassion for the prisoners. Similarly, because of your overwhelming compassion for us conditioned souls, you came to this miserable place. We have no power to appreciate your limitless compassion. We think that we must first get a taste of Kṛṣṇa consciousness, but this requires surrender, and although we must surrender at every moment to some control, we are not surrendering unto you. Śrīla Prabhupāda, please hear our prayer. Allow us to surrender to you at every moment, and in this way perhaps a small portion of your magnetic compassion will manifest in our cold hearts.

My dear spiritual master, you are great and we are very tiny. If we have any ability in this material world, that is due to Kṛṣṇa– *pauruṣaṁ nṛṣu:* "I am the ability in man." If we have any strength in this material world, that is due to Kṛṣṇa– *balaṁ balavatāṁ cāham:* "I am the strength of the strong." Whatever exists in this material world is due to Kṛṣṇa– *bījaṁ māṁ sarva-bhūtānām:* "I am the original seed of all existences." And if we have any spiritual qualification, then that is because of your causeless mercy. If that mercy is taken away, then we may have only material qualities–but these were also given by Kṛṣṇa. And what are we? An insignificant part of Kṛṣṇa's unlimited energy. The only thing we can do is desire, and although we can desire, we don't know how. Consequently we are worthless without you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, just like the hand cut off from the body. Who cares what happens to that hand–it is just a lump of flesh. As always, we are just beggars and have nothing of our own to give, but please, although we are useless, keep dragging us back home, back to Godhead.

## Pittsburgh

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

*mūkaṁ karoti vācālaṁ paṅguṁ laṅghayate girim*  
*yat-kṛpā tam ahaṁ vande śrī-guruṁ dīna-tāraṇam*

**"By the mercy of the** ***guru*** **even a dumb man can become the greatest orator, and even a lame man can cross mountains."**

Only by your association have we come to know of such wonderful things as *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam, kīrtana, prasāda,* Ratha-yātrā, Deity worship, *guru-pūjā,* and Sunday feasts. You convinced us that the false glitter of materialistic pleasure would only cause us pain, and you practically gave us the example of how to become free from material entanglement. You so patiently watched as we stumbled and fell like small children learning to walk. With your magic smile you gave us strength and encouraged us to keep on fighting through *māyā's* attacks. You brought tears to our blinded materialistic eyes and softened our hardened hearts as you sang *Jaya Rādhā-Mādhava* before Their Lordships. Hearing you speak *Bhāgavata* philosophy and seeing how even the most puffed-up speculator would immediately become submissive and inquire from you convinced us that you were the bona fide spiritual master.

Our lives were changed by you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Totally engrossed in chewing the already chewed, we were miserably carrying on our existence, experiencing only the flickering so-called happiness of material life. But by Kṛṣṇa's causeless mercy we met you, and now the purpose of this life is clear.

Please give us the strength to take up your mission–to give everyone the opportunity to hear about Kṛṣṇa. Please make this our goal. Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for your kindness.

*Your unworthy servants,*

## Portland

It was predicted by Ṭhākura Bhaktivinoda  
there will be a personality very soon  
descending from the transcendental abode  
among the stars to be the moon  
who will spread the holy name,  
Kṛṣṇa's pastimes, eternal fame,  
knowledge of God, human life's aim  
in countries where no Vaiṣṇavas ever came.

The best among the swans decided  
to leave his scenic, beautiful lake  
for a place where the crows resided,  
renouncing all for a higher sake  
to transform into swans the crows;  
in their filthy, polluted sphere,  
asked them to follow whatever he shows,  
in the midst of them, he never knew fear.

You succeeded in an impossible task,  
giving us more than we could ask,  
bestowing your mercy millionfold,  
looking for filth, you gave us gold.

When the helm is broken, a captainless boat  
out of control will helplessly floot,  
hitting a rock will finally burst.  
Mad with desires, eternally cursed,  
we purchased our ticket,  
lusty, greedy, and wicked,  
to travel towards hell,  
no shame under illusion's spell.  
Ready to spend tremendous fee  
the booking was on guarantee,  
still it was cancelled by thee.

Please help us to never forget  
that there is no way  
to pay back our debt.  
In this way we humbly pray,  
fallen souls, wretched and crude,  
please strengthen our fortitude  
to carry on spreading your mission  
in pure paramparā tradition,  
for which we have no qualification.  
Still we do have a little hope,  
for your compassion has no limitation,  
for your mercy has a boundless scope.

Your books and publications  
changed the course of history,  
dispelling scholars' hallucinations,  
bestowing knowledge, bliss, eternity.  
These books are like cintāmaṇi stones,  
transforming, into gold the blessed ones  
who received the devotional seed  
taking shelter at your lotus feet.  
To those who read, inquire submissively,  
you deliver the truth vigorously.

Among the topmost souls, you are the best.  
Not able to give, we humbly request  
You to kindly accept this poetry,  
although composed in hypocrisy.

*Your humble servants at Portland temple,*
