## Puerto Rico

Let us first begin by offering our obeisances to our beloved Guru Mahārāja, His Divine Grace A. C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda.

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādī-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*My Dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

I am thinking how someone so fallen as myself could even attempt to write something in commemoration of your greatness, your life being a shining example to your disciples and to the world. I am finding it difficult to describe your transcendental qualities in words–you, who love Kṛṣṇa so much. We did not know Kṛṣṇa, but out of your causeless mercy you gave us Kṛṣṇa through your divine utterances and writings. Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are Kṛṣṇa's dearest and most intimate devotee, and on His request you came to this material world to deliver us from the pangs of material existence. In one million breaths of Mahā-Viṣṇu, we will never be able to repay you for this. Your love of Kṛṣṇa is such an inspiration to behold, as is your indefatigable, uninterrupted service at His lotus feet. You taught us by your example the price one must be willing to pay for going back home, back to Godhead. Śrīla Prabhupāda, you came to us disguised as an old man, and out of your compassion you spoke the teachings of *Bhagavad-gītā* and *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam.* We beg that one day we will, with great determination, take up your order, and that that order might become our life and soul. Our life, because without your order our existence was merely void and aimless. And our soul, because you enlightened us about the soul's eternal identity as servant of Kṛṣṇa. Factually, there is no way we can ever hope to repay you for this gift. You have taught us how to become immortal, simply by surrendering and rendering devotional service at the lotus feet of Śrī Kṛṣṇa, the Supreme Personality of Godhead–the beautiful cowherd boy whose head is bedecked with a peacock feather. You have given us everything. There is nothing in our lives that is not coming from Your Divine Grace. Anything that we have that is worthwhile is simply your causeless mercy upon us. We are begging to always stay engaged in your devotional service with mind, body, and words firmly fixed at your lotus feet and to carry out your order to preach this cult of Caitanya Mahāprabhu in every town and village. It is the desire of Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu, it is the desire of your Guru Mahārāja, and it is your desire. O Gurudeva! Let this desire become our desire. Let your wish become our wish, and let your command become our command.

We are feeling ourselves as very insignificant and fallen disciples, and we are hoping that somehow or other we can please you and in some manner bring a smile to your lotus face. These are not very much words of glorification, but rather a supplication at your lotus feet.

*yogyatā-vicāre, kichu nāhi pāi*  
*tomāra karuṇa sāra*  
*karuṇā nā hoile, kāṅdiyā kāṅdiyā,*  
*prāṇa na rākhibo āra*

Śrīla Prabhupāda, if you examine us, you will find no qualities. Your mercy is all that we are made of. If you are not merciful unto us, we can only weep, and we will not be able to maintain our lives.

On this holy day of your appearance, Śrīla Prabhupāda, we are simply beggars, begging that you will never leave us–that we will not become so covered by this illusion of *māyā* that we cease to remember your lotus feet. Please let us keep your lotus feet always in the core of our hearts. In this way we will always be safe from the onslaught of the material energy, and one day also our hard hearts will melt into ecstatic love of Kṛṣṇa. Let your words like a firebrand leave their indelible mark of transcendental knowledge in the innermost regions of our minds, so that we may never devīate, even slightly, from your holy order.

What can be said in glorification of your magnitude? Mere words seem so barren. You are so much beyond anything we can comprehend with our puny brains. But we were so fortunate to have had your holy association, and we have been infected by that association, because now we can never forget you. Although we cannot understand Kṛṣṇa and we do not really know Kṛṣṇa, we know His most intimate servant–you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Simply by your purity you could deliver the unlimited number of *jīvas* within the entire universe to the transcendental abode of Rādhā-Govinda. You are beyond the scope of us mere mortals who did not know anything at all about the transcendental cowherd boy, Śrī Kṛṣṇa, until you illuminated our hearts. You have given us Kṛṣṇa Himself in the form of *Bhagavad-gītā* and *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam–* the embodiment of the highest Absolute Truth. It is our duty to preach the gospel of these transcendental literatures and benedict all conditioned souls as you have done. So, on the strength of the knowledge that you have given us, we are pleading–here at New Gandhamadana–that you constantly give us your association, and we wish never to forget your lotus feet. Never, never, never. May the dust of your lotus feet become our only sustenance.

*We beg to remain your humble servants,*

*ISKCON Puerto Rico*

## Quito

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

We were wandering hopelessly through millions of different bodies, searching in vain for some lasting pleasure, and becoming completely frustrated at every step. Kṛṣṇa, seeing us in such a piteous condition, sent us our only hope, His most merciful devotee–you. Just as a raincloud carries a volume of water to extinguish a forest fire, you have given us your unlimited mercy to soothe the intense burning of our unlimited material desires. We did nothing to deserve this benediction. Therefore, Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are our only qualification. We cannot leave your service for one minute, because your lotus feet are our only protection.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you so mercifully accepted us as your disciples, but that was not enough. You stayed in this miserable material world, accepting all discomfort, just to expertly guide us on the path of *bhakti.* You spent all of your days and nights translating the śā*stras* so they might serve as the absolute authority in the future. But in the midst of so much sacrifice, you always were blissfully merged in Kṛṣṇa consciousness, never forgetting Kṛṣṇa for a moment.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you always were a perfect example, and still are, in the instructions of your books. What can we do to repay you? Even if we struggle for millions of years, our meager endeavor will never amount to anything compared to what you have given to us. The only thing we can possibly do is to try to satisfy your simple request: "Just chant Hare Kṛṣṇa and be happy." And the formula for that happiness you also told us. Be absorbed in chanting, and distribute these books. You gave your entire lifetime to bringing this most valuable knowledge to the Western world. Now our only duty is to fulfill your instruction. We must spend every moment absorbed in spreading this movement which you have brought. You are our life and soul. Our very existence depends on our sincere desire to serve you. If we do anything else but increase our service to you, we are deserters. You have given us so much. *So 'haṁ kathaṁ nu visṛje tava bhṛtya-sev*ā*m:* "How can I leave the service of my spiritual master, who has given me the transcendental opportunity to associate with the Lord? My first duty is to serve him."

On this most auspicious day of your appearance, we can only fall at your lotus feet and beg forgiveness for our countless offenses. We beg to be forever engaged in expanding this movement, birth after birth. We can only hope that our insignificant effort can give you some transcendental pleasure. *Your eternal servants in Quito, Ecuador,*

Guru-prasāda dāsa / Mṛganātha dāsa / Lokabhāvana dāsa / Gopīkānta dāsa / Bhakta Joselito / Bhakta Jorge / Mādhavī-devī dāsī

## St. Louis

Obeisances, obeisances millions of times at your lotus feet.  
All glories to you who have performed the miraculous feat  
Of turning mlecchas into brāhmaṇas  
Of producing devotees from yavanas  
And of saving lives that were on the verge of-defeat.  
Śrī Caitanya exhibited miracles in Jhārikhaṇḍa forest  
No greater than yours of elevating the poorest  
Of us who were destined  
To take birth as less than  
Animals after Yama's agents made their arrest.  
Over seventy books you have written from the cream of Vedic thought.  
Out of this world of illusion you forcibly wrought  
One hundred temples of Kṛṣṇa devotion  
Simply out of your pure emotion  
Of love for those who in māyā's clutches were caught.  
Your every moment dedicated to Kṛṣṇa's service.  
Seeing Him everywhere you felt transcendental bliss.  
We saw it in you  
And wanted it too.  
Kind as you are, you gave it without prejudice.  
You managed this diverse society, ISKCON, with grace.  
You smashed impersonalists with logic and wit as your mace,  
Then expanding yourself into eleven  
guru forms, you empowered them  
To spread kṛṣṇa-bhakti at your incredible spiritual pace.

Your personal presence was a touchstone to ecstasy,  
Even for those who, attached to this fantasy  
Life of illusion with no conclusion,  
Tried to defeat you, but fell at your feet, too.  
Wherever there is Kṛṣṇa there can only be victory.  
You gave us great faith by showing us how  
In depending on Kṛṣṇa He will allow  
Us, in spite of all difficulty,  
To do everything perfectly  
So long as this preaching becomes our life's vow.  
We shall now accept any danger with cheerfulness,  
Preaching to please you and think of your kindness  
To us who were low  
But are trying to know  
Your unlimited glories, which none can assess.

*St. Louis temple,*

## San Diego

*Dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept our prostrated obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet. You explain in the purport of verse 34 of the Fourth Chapter of the *Bhagavad-gītā,* "A bona fide spiritual master is by nature kind toward the disciples. Therefore, when the student is submissive and is always ready to render service, the reciprocation of knowledge and inquiries becomes perfect." And regarding this reciprocation, you have cited the personal example of your own relationship with your Guru Mahārāja in your concluding words to the *Śrī Caitanya-caritāmṛta:* "I think that His Divine Grace Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura is always seeing my activities and guiding me within my heart by his words. As it is said in *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam, tene brahma hṛdā ya ādi-kavaye.* Spiritual inspiration comes from within the heart, wherein the Supreme Personality of Godhead, in His Paramātmā feature, is always sitting with all His devotees and associates. It is to be admitted that whatever translation work I have done is through the inspiration of my spiritual master, because personally I am most insignificant and imcompetent to do this materially impossible work. I do not think myself a very learned scholar, but I have full faith in the service of my spiritual master, His Divine Grace Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura. If there is any credit to my activities of translating, it is all due to His Divine Grace." Thus you are helping us come to understand the blissful nature of the transcendental bond of service between the *guru* and disciple, and you are enthusing us to endeavor to approach that platform. We are realizing that by helping Your Divine Grace fulfill your mission, taking whatever risks necessary, we can always be situated in your divine association. And this shelter that you so mercifully benedict us with can fill our hearts with unlimited reservoirs of *kṛṣṇa-prema* and render us free from all experience of calamity and pain–miseries which all others are forced to accept as their only reality.

We will never be able to repay Your Divine Grace for your kindness upon us. At least, however, we can pray to someday be able to act beyond the platform of fruitively motivated service, so that we can properly reflect and fulfill your pure desire to flood the world with the ocean of ecstatic love of God.

And just where, O Gurudeva, can your lotus feet be found,  
The shade of which we cannot bear to live without?  
They are to be found always dancing in the ecstatic pastimes  
Of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu–  
Far beyond the dualities of this mundane world.  
And how are we to take their shelter?  
By always dancing bravely behind you.

*Your most unworthy servants at New Govardhana,*

## San Salvador

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādī-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

At least three times daily we offer our respectful prostrated obeisances at your lotus feet. Now on this most auspicious day of your appearance, we are falling down flat at your feet, and as your most insignificant servants, we are humbly begging to receive a drop of mercy by which we can always strictly adhere to your instructions. By just one drop of mercy we have been able to escape the entanglement of fruitive activities. We have become attached to your lotus feet. By such attachment we are situated in the perfection of life.

Just as the sun can purify the most decayed substance by shining upon it, and can dissipate darkness to reveal the true color and life of the material nature, you, our most beloved spiritual master, are bringing light and life into our obscure existence. You came to cut off all of our attachments for this insipid material world. As the bona fide representative of the Supreme Lord, you only needed to say a few words to awaken the most inert persons. Through your lotus mouth you delivered the transcendental sound of Kṛṣṇa's message to His devotees.

O Gurudeva, you are like touchstone, because you have been able to transform *mlecchas* into Vaiṣṇavas. You appeared to free us from our unfortunate material condition. You have taught us about *tattva,* the Absolute Truth. The Supreme Lord can make gigantic trees grow from small seeds, and they in turn produce more trees. In the same way, Śrīla Prabhupāda, you planted the seed of *bhakti* in our hearts. That seed has now grown into a tree and born fruit. By your mercy, that fruit will bear the seeds which we can plant in all the remaining conditioned souls. You can make us satisfy your most urgent desire by your potency: that we continue preaching and expanding the *saṅkīrtana* movement inaugurated by Lord Caitanya.

*Kṛṣṇa-śakti vinā nahe tāra pravartana:* unless one is empowered by Kṛṣṇa, he cannot spread the *saṅkīrtana* movement.

Ācāryadeva, again we are fervently praying to you to give us the force to maintain our unity, thus enabling us to develop our devotional service for the pleasure of Your Divine Grace. Śrīla Prabhupāda, please hear our petitions and remain as our preceptor, guide, protector, and eternal master.

Thank you, O dearest spiritual master, for everything you have done for us.

*Your eternal servants in San Salvador Mandira,*

## Santiago

*O beloved Spiritual Master,*

You said "Our master told us to preach. Let the neophytes remain in the temple and simply ring the bells!" (*Vaiśiṣṭyāṣṭaka 30)*

Give us strength to carry on with this mission and satisfy your will. Take us to preach, without fear or attachment, all over the three worlds. If we are not able to satisfy you, our life has no sense, and we cannot reach perfection or any happiness. The power to push on this movement comes from pure knowledge, so please fill our hearts with the pure nectarean knowledge which always flows from your lotus mouth. Bring us close to your message and your wishes, and make us be fearful of not satisfying you enough. Please free us from all types of fear and attachments. Just like you, who stood against incalculable opposition to save us, please make us rightful representatives of you, and give us strength to fight similar oppositions.

O beloved master! O fountainhead of infinite goodness and mercy! You are our father, mother, son, and friend. It is impossible to live one second separated from you. Please, fill us with devotional service to your lotus feet, and please give us perfect intelligence to satisfy you correctly. Bring us close to you, just like a banyan tree to the ground, like a fish to the water, or the light to the sun.

You also said, "Everyone has become a witch by the influence of the age. Everyone is unhappy due to the absence of the mercy of the Vaiṣṇavas." (*Vaiśiṣṭyāṣṭaka* 60)

Our hearts were hard like rocks, but still you planted in them the seed of *bhakti,* and you spray it constantly with the mercy of your lotus words. You are the personified mercy of all the Vaiṣṇavas. All of your life was dedicated to cooling the fire of material existence surrounding our hearts. You were always preaching, always standing up against our poor minds and crushing the demoniac arguments with the merciful wish of cleansing the weeds of mental speculation within our hearts.

Now that this ocean of your mercy is at our disposal, permit us to fill the whole universe, for the good of the conditioned souls and for your pleasure, with the ambrosial nectar of this Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

Finally, you said, "Any independence we could have, let us throw it to the waters." Please make us completely dependent on you, like the small boy to his mother. You are in every corner of our life, and you maintain us with your mercy.

Permit us to glorify your name always,

*Your most fallen servants from Santiago Mandira*

## Sao Paulo

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

*oṁ ajñāna-timirāndhasya jñānāñjana-śalākayā*  
*cakṣur unmīlitaṁ yena tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādī-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Not too long ago, we were living just like animals, drying to enjoy our material senses and the mind and to make some arrangement to attain unending happiness and knowledge. We were certainly covered by illusion, identifying with that which is *not–māyā,* the illusory energy of the omnipotent Lord.

Lost in the desert of material existence, we were running, hither and thither, after the mirages of friendship, love, and society, thinking that they would be the ultimate solution to our miserable existence.

One day, however, by superior arrangement, we found beauty, poetry, love, utility, and meaning to our lives as we ran our eyes, carrying the mind and intelligence, over the nectarean words of your books. Then we met your disciples, and we could understand that only a great soul could have such beautiful, intelligent, and well-behaved disciples. Before coming in touch with you, we were like orphans in this world. You took real care of us, giving us that which is most important–knowledge of God and of our personal relationship with Him. You revealed to us that the Absolute is sentient, a person, the supreme controller and enjoyer, and our best friend. Thus you gave us the right to eternity, knowledge, and bliss.

O Śrīla Prabhupāda, we thank you because you are always with us. Reading your books (pure nectar), remembering them, and telling others about them, we can feel you are present. Actually, we are now closer to you than ever before, when we could only have a glimpse of your *vapuḥ,* traveling so fast in this world, always surrounded by magnificent demigods, intimate helpers of your sublime mission. You are certainly protecting us always, whispering wise instructions.

The greatest liar will say that you have died, but we can see that you are now living even closer to us in our hearts, and your sublime pastime of giving out *kṛṣṇa-prema* has expanded many times as you left us eleven *gurus.*

O Śrīla Prabhupāda, O reservoir of all good qualities, you are the perfect person. Your personal example has given us new hope. Purity was once a lost Utopia. Now we have witnessed your miracles as you changed our degraded lives. You are Jagad-guru, and your power is so great. You are giving us the tools and the instructions by which we can conquer over envy.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, beloved spiritual master, our only desire is to be able to be useful for helping you expand your divine mission. Keep us always engaged in preaching Kṛṣṇa consciousness to all. We have learned from you that this is the best service we can do.

*Your disciples and granddisciples from Nova Gokula,*

## Seattle

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

On Sunday, November 10, 1974, you wrote: "Although according to material vision His Divine Grace Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura Prabhupāda passed away from this material world... I still consider His Divine Grace to be always present with me by his *vāṇī,* his words.... I think that His Divine Grace Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura is always seeing my activities and guiding me within my heart by his words."

After reading this passage, written on the completion of the *Caitanya-caritāmṛta,* we can realize to some degree, at least, just how powerful is the influence of the spiritual master's words upon the disciple. While we are bereft of your personal association in the mundane world, we are at once associating with you on the transcendental plane through your *vāṇī.* Kṛṣṇa consciousness bewilders the nondevotees because it defies "rational" logic by forcing the living entity to realize that separation in the transcendental world intensifies one's attachment for the person no longer present, and such attachment increases more and more as time passes, unlike in the material world, where separation causes eventual forgetfulness. Now more than a year has passed since your disappearance, and your disciples continue to push on this *saṅkīrtana* movement, especially through book distribution. Consequently, we can understand that, while it is undoubtedly painful for us that we no longer see your personal form, so elegant and sublime as it was, we can experience an even more blissful relationship with you through your words, because there is nothing that can separate us from your words, unlike what happened when we were separated from your personal form. The happiness we feel is not coldhearted, but rather it is felt by understanding that you live through your books, and that as long as we read, publish, and distribute your books, we will associate with you, devoid of any fear of separation. Prabhupāda, we pray that we will always associate with you through your books by reading them, by seeing them published, and by distributing them.

On this most blessed event commemorating your merciful appearance in this mortal world, we continue to increase our transcendental attachment for you, which increases unlimitedly despite your so-called "disappearance."

*Your worthless servants at ISKCON Seattle,*

## South Africa

*oṁ ajñāna-timirāndhasya jñānāñjana-śalākayā*  
*cakṣur unmīlitaṁ yena tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

**"I was born in the darkest ignorance, and my spiritual master opened my eyes with the torch of knowledge. I offer my respectful obeisances unto him."**

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

It is without any qualification at all that we have become your disciples. We have been floundering in an ocean of suffering, imagining the temporary pleasures and last-class enjoyments of the senses to be the all-in-all. Ignorant of any of the higher principles of life, we were thinking ourselves very intelligent, advanced, and cultured. Engrossed in the bodily misconception, we were feeling ourselves to be very beautiful people, the apex of the evolutionary chain. Absorbed in all sinful and abominable activities, we were certain that this civilization was the perfection of enlightenment. Just as there is no measuring the extent of the illusion covering us, so there is no measuring the boundless mercy of Kṛṣṇa.

Your appearance in the West is certainly the causeless mercy of Kṛṣṇa. All the qualities inimical to spiritual life are abounding here–arrogance, rebelliousness, desire to lord it over and enjoy. One can hardly conceive of a less fertile atmosphere for the propagation of spiritual knowledge or of less qualified candidates for spiritual life–we, who are the pampered, quarrelsome, and illusioned waste-products of the so-called modern age.

It is certainly your purity alone that has created our qualification. In fact, contrary to Vedic practice, which demands that the disciple seek out a spiritual master, you have sought us out and with the light of *śāstra* dispelled our ignorance and given us Kṛṣṇa. Only the pure devotee can deliver Kṛṣṇa, and by so doing even the most fallen can be saved from the miseries of birth, old age, disease, and death. It is your presentation of the *mahā-mantra* in the line of Lord Caitanya that is saving countless living entities from the misery of *saṁsāra.*

*śrī guru karuṇā sindhu adhama janāra bandhu*  
*lokanāth lokera jīvana*

**"O beloved Gurudeva, you are the ocean of mercy, friend of the poor, lord and master of the devotees."**

We can only pray to Kṛṣṇa for the strength and determination to become the willing servants of your desires, for by pleasing you we are pleasing Kṛṣṇa. You are our link with the disciplic succession, and without your mercy, all our endeavors are fruitless.

It is certainly beyond our capabilities to offer you anything really worthwhile on this Vyāsa-pūjā. We can only pray for your continued guidance and direction. Your lotus feet are our only shelter; do not kick us away, even though we remain so unwilling and inept.

*Your servants in South Africa,*

## Śrī Lanka

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādī-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept our most fallen obeisances at your lotus feet. We pray by your grace that we may never deviate from your order, and in this way we may obtain your association forevermore.

Throughout millions of years and an equal amount of lives we have been searching for the perfect person who could relieve us from the suffering condition of this material world. So many false prophets, teachers, so-called masters, and cheaters took us down blind alleys which led only to despair and disillusion. But you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, have given us the highest gift–love of Kṛṣṇa. Out of your causeless mercy you have come here to deliver us rascals who were so full of voidism and impersonalism. Your mercy is all that we are made of. Without your grace our lives would be meaningless.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, we are yours; we cannot belong to anyone else, for you are our lord and master. All we know is what you've taught us. All we speak is what we have heard from you. All we do is what you've shown us. Therefore, we are alive only because you have made us alive. Without you we are dead men only, bereft of everything. Therefore, you are our life and soul, the life of our lives. Turning away from you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, is walking directly into the hands of death.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, our humble prayers are that you forget all our offenses because of our not being serious in trying to please your desires. We offer our fallen *daṇḍavats* at your holy lotus feet. All glories to Śrīla Prabhupāda!

Mahākartā dāsa brahmacārī / Bhakta Viṣṇudāsa / Bhakta Dharmaśrī / Bhakta Devendra / Bhakta Karma-mohana / Bhakta Gopī-Rāmakṛṣṇa / Bhakta Rājaliṅgam / Nandarāṇī-devī dāsī / Bhaktin Parameśvarī / Bhaktin Ṣaḍguṇeśvarī

## State College, Pa.

***"Had Śrīla Prabhupāda given up, as would most persons his age, there would be nothing further to tell about the Hare Kṛṣṇa movement." (The Biography of a Pure Devotee by Śrīla Satsvarūpa dāsa Goswami, BTG Vol. 14-4)***

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Now we have some little realization of the Vaiṣṇava’s statement, "Your mercy is all that I am made of." If we consider what our lives would be *minus* your influence–no Hare Kṛṣṇa movement, no knowledge of the self, no knowledge of Kṛṣṇa or of His service, abode, name, form, qualities, pastimes, etc., no temples, no Deities, no *prasāda,* no holy association, no meaning in life, and no knowledge of what to expect after death–if we try to consider what it would be like without all of these manifestations of your grace, then we can easily see that we would be forced to give up our lives. Your mercy is the very life of our lives, and it is therefore more essential to us than life itself.

O Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are our transcendental correspondence. How could we ever begin to repay you for saving our lives in this way? Words come very cheaply, and our actions are even more contaminated by self-interest. But you have said, "If you at all wish to repay me, then preach vigorously like me."

We have not one ounce of sincerity or compassion for others, but somehow, by your association, we are coming to desire these qualities a little bit, and we are beginning to try to preach. This is like our holding a candle to the sun, for whatever we may say worth hearing is only your words, and whatever ability we may have comes as your mercy.

Now we are praying to be your instruments in carrying your benedictions to so many suffering, conditioned living entities. Sometimes it seems that almost no one wants these benedictions, especially here, where these students squander their good fortune of health, beauty, intelligence, and finances in forgetfulness of Kṛṣṇa. It often appears that we are so far from a God conscious society that the mind boggles to wonder how we will ever be able to actually, practically bring it about. But we know, due to your example, that we simply have to deliver this message of the holy name, or the pastimes of Śrī Kṛṣṇa: *śṛṇvatāṁ sva-kathāḥ kṛṣṇaḥ (Bhāg.* 1.2.17). If they will only hear this message.

We are feeling very helpless in being able to do anything, seeing the size and scope of this demoniac civilization. What can a few men do in the midst of a vast ocean of sinful, demoniac living entities? Your words again force us to remember that we are not here to do some magic on our own. We've come to deliver this message which, if it's heard, will make people pious. It will break apart the attachment that they have for the material modes of nature. It is Lord Kṛṣṇa's power and your grace that will act through us if we can just deliver to them the message of Kṛṣṇa. At least we have to try; that is our duty. And we are practically witnessing your miracle in seeing their lives being changed in this way. And seeing this, our stonelike hearts are melting, too. Without this love of God actually being felt, our intelligence is simply the fabric of *māyā.*

O Śrīla Prabhupāda, we are like little children. We are embarrassed by our thoughts, words, and deeds when we realize that you are watching and hearing. How kind you are to these fools. Dear lord, your mission is our life and soul. When we struggle back into its mainstream, we feel ourselves shooting along with the current, caught up in and amazed by its vitality and significance in an otherwise dead world. You are always making big plans on behalf of the Supreme Personality of Godhead.

Please let us hold on to this preaching mission. This is not a right to be obtained even by the severest austerities and penances, but the rarest privilege extended only by your causeless mercy. Please, if you see us threatening to leave its blissful sunshine to wander in the shadows with false pride, just grasp us firmly and shake us to our senses. You have let us taste the nectar of your service. Please don't ever take it away.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, please give us the ability to speak this philosophy nicely and warmly, in a realized way, with honor and concern for all living entities, and please help us to give up any desire to enjoy Lord Kṛṣṇa's energies, and to be satisfied with just the holy name. Please grant us the humility to understand that we are just your servants.

Wherever we go, we feel you glowing in our hearts like a bright lamp, and we see that others also perceive its warmth and brightness and are attracted. We think that only Kṛṣṇa's pure devotee can influence these conditioned souls to surrender to Him. Therefore, we are praying that you will take over our hearts and, throwing out our separate existence (false ego) altogether, let us carry you with us forever, that the whole world may become charmed by your glorious qualities.

*Your eternal servants at Śrī Kṛṣṇa-nāma-pracārita Mandira,*

## Sweden

Transcendental father!  
More than earthly father did you your children love,  
And still your fondest mercy continues from above.  
"I will never leave you, "you often used to say,  
And this reassurance is revealed to us more from day to day.  
When spirit leaves the body and moves on to future strife  
Dull matter remains only and no longer is there life.  
So as we see that ISKCON lives and grows apace,  
We know that you're still with us and giving us your grace.

We are forever joined to you through the simple things you taught–  
How to worship Kṛṣṇa through every action, word, and thought,  
How to sing in holy crowd the Lord's sweet name divine,  
To bow before His form bedecked with flowers and costumes fine;  
Then offer Him His favorite food and of His remnants eat,  
And how to dance in joy for Him while khola and karatāla beat.  
How to read together your eternal words of truth  
That tell of Kṛṣṇa's beauty and of His world give proof.  
How to drink our heart's content of these simple pastimes pure  
And to others gladly giving them, expand them more and more.

Through all these things the flavor of your selfless love is found  
Because you loved to do them and give them to all around.  
By continuing on this path our love for you we show. You simply want to see us  
find true happiness, we know.  
You're an ocean of compassion, your desire is only one,  
That to the lotus feet of Kṛṣṇa all the world may come.

To cry from sorrow or weep with joy, distinction is hard to make,  
But our vow to push your mission onward–that we'll never break.

All glories to Your Divine Grace.

*New Rādhākuṇḍa,*

## Switzerland

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādī-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Once again, Vyāsa-pūjā affords me the opportunity to glorify Your Divine Grace as well as to realize my inability to ever adequately do so. I stare foolishly at the blank page in front of me, remembering your unparalleled transcendental character, words, and activities. I am quickly overwhelmed by your glories. Were I to have crores of chances to offer Vyāsa-pūjā prayers to you, nonetheless I could hardly begin to describe the miracles that you have wrought.

You have woken all the millions and billions of living entities out of the nightmare of society, friendship, and so-called love by bestowing profusely the mercy of the *saṅkīrtana* movement. You have single-handedly unfurled Lord Caitanya's banner of *kṛṣṇa-prema* all over this bleak world. That is your right. Service unto the *saṅkīrtana* movement under your guidance is itself the only refuge available to the living entity at any time or in any place. Such a unique opportunity to satisfy Kṛṣṇa may not come after countless lifetimes of austerity and sacrifice within this universe. I find myself so ill equipped to take advantage of your causeless mercy, Śrīla Prabhupāda, so I humbly request you to show me how. Please let me always remain on the *saṅkīrtana* party.

In your *vapuḥ* form you have departed from my mundane vision, but you remain eternally present in your *vāṇī* form. I pray that this *vāṇī, gaura-vāṇī,* may become the only sustenance of my life. "In connection with the verse *vyavasāyātmikā buddhiḥ,* Śrīla Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura gives his commentary that we should take up the words of the spiritual master as our life and soul. We should try to carry out the specific instruction of the spiritual master very rigidly, without caring for our personal benefit or loss."

The Supreme Personality of Godhead has sent you among us, and you have fulfilled His mission in every way through the will of your Guru Mahārāja. Thus, you are *kṛṣṇa-preṣṭha,* the most dear to the Lord. You are Vaiṣṇava Ṭhākura, that rarest pure devotee of Kṛṣṇa who can deliver Kṛṣṇa to the most fallen and wretched. *Tomāra kukkur boliyā jana.* Let me become your dog and serve you unconditionally, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Chastise me whenever you wish. Beat me severely. You are free to do with me as you like. I am most hellish and fallen. But if I may just petition one thing, O Gurudeva, I am begging, imploring, that you never kick me away from your lotus feet. Their moonlike beams have illuminated my dark heart, and I can no longer live without them.

*Your most useless disciples,*

## Sydney

His Divine Grace came and enlightened this rascal: "You are servant, that you can't deny."

Who can deny the infallible words of the servant of God? Let them step forward! His Divine Grace will smash all their false arguments, logic, and speculations.

It's not dependent on your belief or blind faith! It's simply a fact. Let the fools step forward and deny my *guru,* but I request all honest men just to examine His Divine Grace. We must accept his honest message and execute his mission.

The dealings of *māyā,* however, want to disturb this mind through contemplation of sense objects, and drag the soul down to a lower service in the name of so-called happiness or love. My simple prayer to His Divine Grace is,

"Thank you very much for your honest dealing,  
for preaching and māyā-stealing.

"Thank you for personal service in bliss,  
for proving the Māyāvādī amiss.

"Thank you for going to so much trouble for the human race,  
for taking birth in this abominable place.

"Thank you for Kṛṣṇa, who you did deliver,  
for being gentle and tolerant, a magnanimous forgiver.

"Thank you for being determined, a thunderbolt strong,  
for your courage, not tolerating wrong.

"Thank you for being as soft as a rose,  
for bringing my doggish life to a close.

"Thank you for being transparent, pure, and kind,  
for all your qualities, a treasure to find."

*Your fallen servants at Bhaktivedanta Āśrama, New Koladvīpa,*

## Tehran

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

We greatly felt your absence and suffered most from your separation in this year of revolution, worries, Allah-u-Akbar, shootings, inquisitions, great hopes, street *saṅkīrtana,* and other revelations.

We pray that you fill the void within our lives and hearts.

*Your disciples at the revolutionary Islamic Republic of Iran*

## Toronto

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept our humble obeisances at your lotus feet. We were born in the darkest of ignorance, and you ^opened our eyes with the torch of transcendental knowledge. We offer our respectful obeisances unto your lotus feet. You are the mercy representative of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, and you have delivered the most fallen persons according to His order.

When most men are retiring from active life, you began your rigorous preaching program for Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu. Because you completely surrendered everything to fulfill the order of your spiritual master, Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu empowered you to become the greatest preacher of Kṛṣṇa consciousness in the history of the universe. In this age, when there are so many bogus spiritual masters, you appeared as the pure representative of Lord Kṛṣṇa. In twelve short years you made *Kṛṣṇa* a household word throughout the world, and by your transcendental potency, the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement is being spread to every town and village, fulfilling the prophecy of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu.

As the years pass since your departure from this planet, people will come to recognize more and more your greatness and your glories. Without discrimination, you have distributed love of God to every living being on this planet. It is impossible for us to estimate your transcendental position from our neophyte understanding, but gradually, as your preaching work effects all the people of the world, we can see a glimmer of your greatness. The question comes to mind. How were we chosen, being so fallen, to be blessed by you, who are so exalted? How is it that we are able to render service at your lotus feet? You raised us from the lowest position to the highest in such a short amount of time. It is all by your infinite mercy. Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have given us the most valuable gift that anyone could ever receive– *kṛṣṇa-prema-pradāyate,* following in the footsteps of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu. We can never repay you for the mercy you have shown us, we who are the most fallen. It is beyond our range of perception. We beg you to simply let us remain your humble servants., birth after birth. Never let us leave the service of your lotus feet, and please bless us with your causeless mercy. We are in a hopeless position without you, and therefore we beg you to continue to shower your mercy upon us, for that is our only salvation. As Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura has sung, "If you examine me you will find no good qualities. Your mercy is all that I am made of. If you are not merciful unto me, I can only weep and cannot maintain my life."

Please let us serve you and Rādhā-Kṣīracorā-gopīnātha for the rest of our lives, without devīation. Jaya Gurudeva. Jaya Śrīla Prabhupāda.

*New Remuṇā Dhāma, Toronto, Canada*

## Trinidad

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda.*

Please accept our fallen obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet. We are unworthy of serving such an exalted soul as you, Prabhupāda–at whose feet all masters sit–but you do not consider who is.fit or who is not fit to receive your mercy. You are freely giving everyone an opportunity to be sheltered under your lotus feet. This is your legacy. Even though we are fools and rascals, you have left us this golden opportunity in your divine instructions. Sad to say, in this material world we are always falsely proud, thinking ourselves as masters. However, you have shown us that the real master is he who is firmly fixedat the lotus feet of his spiritual master–To become a leader is not difficult, provided one is prepared to follow the instructions of the bona fide *guru."* Therefore, since we are so foolishly bent on being masters, we fervently pray that you allow us to take pride only in your words of direction and always serve you by remembering your divine instructions.

*We remain, your unworthy servants at ISKCON Trinidad*  
*(Kimpuruṣa-varṣa),*

## Vancouver

*Our dear Gurudeva,*

Let us fall at your lotus feet and offer our prostrated obeisances again and again.

It has now been a year and half, by mundane calculations, since you have left our vision, Śrīla Prabhupāda, but the evidence of your glorious presence continues to unfold throughout your wonderful expansion–the International Society for Krishna Consciousness. This year in Māyāpur it was an awesome experience to see your great visionary sons–the inheritors of your spiritual opulences–thunder forth the messages of the Absolute Truth from eleven *vyāsāsanas.* This is your glory! You must be very proud to see what stalwart devotees have emerged under your loving and strict guidance to carry out the great mission of Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu. Without faltering they have accepted the full weight of the responsibility that you had so expertly prepared them for.

It was most satisfying and reassuring to see your trusted disciples flex their spiritual muscles and easily quell the "uprising" of doubt and illusion that struck at Vṛndāvana this year. Exhibiting your qualities of fearless strength combined with the magnanimity and compassion of a bona fide Vaiṣṇava saint, your GBC men and *gurus* showed their "stuff" and ability to work together and rectify inner problems and to unify the devotees in the great task of carrying out your mission.

It has been pointed out that the true indication of a great leader is that his influence and contribution do not atrophy on his departure. The growth of the Hare Kṛṣṇa movement has been miraculous (you have compared it to the form of Lord Varāha appearing so tiny from Lord Brahma's nostril and then growing and growing) and the greatest miracle is that it is continuing to grow and expand its influence throughout the world. If there is any question of glorification for any great devotee in this movement, then you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, must be the proper recipient of that glorification. You are the founder-ācārya. You are the real friend and well-wisher of all suffering beings on this planet. As the most surrendered and intimate servant of Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa, you are returning so many of his rebellious sons to their proper home. You have inspired a whole society of devotees to take up this task and deliver the medicine of *hari-nāma* to every town and village. You have completely illuminated the meaning *of prabhupāda* as so many great *mahā-bhāgavatas* have gathered at your lotus feet.

As the story of your first days and months in America, the dreadful land of the living dead, is told, a new and deeper appreciation of your saintly and transcendental character must be evoked in each devotee. The unflinching faith in the order and desire of your Gurudeva, and the fortitude, patience, endurance, and perseverance with which you carried out that order, sets an eternal, perfect standard for all preachers. You labored alone–with Kṛṣṇa–so that we might reap the shelter of all the preaching facility and association that you created for us. You have given us so many preaching opportunities so that we may also come alive by contact with the *saṅkīrtana* movement!

We take shelter at your lotus feet, Śrīla Prabhupāda. May your glories be spread throughout the three worlds!

*Your servants at New Gokula-dhāma,*

## Vṛndāvana

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādī-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*oṁ ajñāna-timirāndhasya jñānāñjana-śalākayā*  
*cakṣur unmīlitaṁ yena tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

*tad viddhi praṇipātena, paripraśnena sevayā*  
*upadekṣyanti te jñānaṁ, jñāninas tattva-darśinaḥ*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
Your Divine Grace is the Supreme Personality of Servitor Godhead,  
the Jagad-guru of the universe, the swanlike mendicant  
and the greatest individual in this material world, thoroughly  
transcendental to material reality–the greatest person we know.  
You are the perfect person, from your appearance to the end  
of your stay with us and onward to eternity.  
Your every action was example; your every word was instruction.  
Your every glance was lighting up our hearts so cold, now quickened  
with the fire of bhakti.  
Your every step an invitation to take up the fight for Lord Caitanya.  
"Come on!" you said. And we followed you somehow.  
And, Your Divine Grace let us follow you!  
When we had no qualities, no gentleness, no courage, no purity,  
no knowledge of our real position as your eternal servants,  
"Come on, "you said, smiling, and we followed you.  
We followed you through many years of time,  
but it all passed in a second, so sweet it was,  
your ringing voice sublimely setting us on line,  
setting us upright again after lifetime's nights had knocked us down.  
You took us in, you accepted us, taught us the Absolute Truth as it is,  
and turned us loose on saṅkīrtana to shake the world  
on its foundations before long.  
By giving us the holy name, the mahā-mantra, "Great Mantra of  
Deliverance,"  
you severed our past forever, Śrīla Prabhupāda.  
Lord Caitanya sent you to reclaim every town,  
and now it's happened, the enlightenment has at last begun,  
resounding like a thunderbolt throughout the populations of the earth.  
And when the presses were rolling, the knowledge was flowing out  
in all directions to the peoples of this world,  
you headed for India to show us more.  
To show us what no foreigner had ever known before this time–  
the India of pure devotion,  
the India no conqueror from distant oceans could understand,  
Lord Kṛṣṇa 's secret transcendental land: Vṛndāvana.  
Personally, Your Divine Grace took us to each holy place  
in the withering sun and glare.  
Personally you insisted we get land and build  
when we hardly knew even where we were, or how to bear  
the austerity of living there.  
But you knew it would all go on and on: the Kṛṣṇa-Balarāma Mandira,  
the Guesthouse, and the Gurukula for hundreds of young men–  
your energy filled the tiny patch of land,  
you would not rest, you planned and managed every detail,  
guided perfectly by Lord Caitanya's golden hand.  
Dear master, how fast you worked, bow relentlessly late into the night  
your light was on, your dictaphone recording your careful words.  
So late at night you translated. Yet all day was never free  
from management and taking each of us to task,  
gently dragging us back home.  
We are at a loss to imitate your energy, your resolve to be  
nothing less than Lord Kṛṣṇa's instrument at last.  
When the temple opened you marched upon the Chattikara road in  
the blinding dust of April's afternoon.  
To the roar of drums you walked for miles through the twisted streets  
past Vṛndāvana’s crowds, just to show us how it's done.  
And at the end you journeyed back again, yes, years later you returned  
back to your kuṭira, back to your home in Vṛndāvana to end this life,  
to finish your pastime with flawless action that we cannot comprehend.  
Your Divine Grace did so much. And you said that we should do ten times more.  
We know you were empowered by the Lord with strength beyond compare.  
You told us that by following exactly in the line  
that the paramparā would guide us, that the energy is there  
in transcendental service, ever greater than before.  
But we pray to you, dear master, it is you we want to please;  
our service can go on only by your grace.  
We still live here in Vṛndāvana, living here with you.  
The Deities are cared for, your house is clean, and now  
your Guesthouse runs on smoothly;  
your Gurukula echoes śāstra in the early morning dew.  
We're at our posts you gave us, in the knowledge that you showed us.  
All that grew here comes from you, Śrīla Prabhupāda.  
All the children and the cows, the new bhaktas and the crowds,  
come to see you in the passage of the day.  
Your samādhi is progressing, and the new land we're acquiring  
just to please you, just to please you in a realistic way.  
You're our shelter, please don't leave us. Through you alone,  
by your instructions, we see beyond the veil  
of our senses to the transcendental realm.  
You're our shelter and the transparent media we have in our frail lives  
to understand Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa by Their grace, and in Vṛndāvana to live on.  
Please allow us to serve Their lotus feet.  
Today we remember that the office of ācārya  
coming from Vyāsa, came to you.  
On this day you appeared to deliver the world from all concoction,  
to fulfill the plan that Lord Kṛṣṇa had in view. Today in Vṛndāvana is a very special day; it is your day of days.  
And as your humble children, we fall before your lotus feet,  
thanking you again and again for the miracle of your appearance.

All of Vṛndāvana and the world is remembering you this day, as  
ISKCON rings your glories everywhere.  
But we hold you tightly in our hearts, eternal master; your countenance is  
with us very near.  
Eternal glories belong to you, dear master,  
Ācāryadeva, teacher, and ever well-wisher to these humble few.  
Eternal glories belong again and again to you.

*The residents of Śrī Śrī Kṛṣṇa-Balarāma Mandira*

## Washington, D. C.

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādī-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

**FATHER**

O eternal father, Śrīla Prabhupāda, your guidance has the supreme eternal result. You have always emphasized that we should be attached to your *vāṇī* (instructions), and we are finding at every moment that these instructions are maintaining us in every way on this journey back to Godhead. Your every action is always linked with some sublime instruction, because you are the *ācārya–one* who teaches by perfect example. Your great legacy, the sublime books on the science of Kṛṣṇa consciousness, have become our instruction manuals for survival, as well as the transcendental foundation of our preaching activities. You have given us the perfect, eternal life-insurance policy in the form of your wonderful books. As our father, you've given us the greatest inheritance. As your sons and daughters, we will increase the ocean of your mercy by going to the remotest places on your behalf, following your example, to give the conditioned souls the message of Vāsudeva. Just as you entered the hostile and alien jungle of New York City in that momentous year, 1965, to deliver us, we will follow in your footsteps, tenaciously determined to satisfy you by chanting Hare Kṛṣṇa, taking *prasāda,* and preaching the glories of Kṛṣṇa everywhere–in every town and village.

**GRANDFATHER**

O Śrīla Prabhupāda, your offspring are giving birth to a large new, healthy generation. The eleven successor initiating *gurus,* whom you have asked to accept disciples, are sincerely and mercifully pursuing your orders. They are giving birth to so many grandchildren. These grandchildren are so anxious to please their *gurus,* because they know this pleases you. It is said that the grandfather is even more merciful upon the grandchildren than the father. Everywhere we see these grandchildren looking so healthy and bright and succeeding so vigorously in preaching the glories of the *saṅkīrtana* movement. They are relishing your association *(vāṇī)* beyond the limits of this small material world. The world sees that they are turning their grandfather's factory of love of Godhead into a vast conglomerate touching every heart in every land. They are applying the De's Ointment of love of Godhead on the eyes of the world by the expert application of book distribution, in all of its diverse forms. The master plan of yours is being carried out by your grandchildren with renewed life and vitality. They know you as their grandfather, His Divine Grace A. C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda–Jagat-guru.

**JAGAT-GURU**

*bhārata-bhūmite haila manuṣya-janma yāra*  
*janma sārthaka kari' kara para-upakāra*  
*(Cc. Ādi 9.41)*

O Śrīla Prabhupāda! Śrī Kṛṣṇa Caitanya Mahāprabhu has predicted that this Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement would go to every town and village, and you are fulfilling the desire of your Guru Mahārāja, His Divine Grace Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Mahārāja, to spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness in the Western countries. Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, who is called the merciful incarnation of Godhead for this age of Kali because He distributes love of God to everyone regardless of their qualifications, predicted that the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement would cover the world. You have not only spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness in the Western countries, in the English language, as your Guru Mahārāja requested, but you have also introduced Kṛṣṇa consciousness in all the major countries of the world and in every major language and cultural grouping. You alone have taken this mission seriously, to save all the conditioned souls by the spreading of the *saṅkīrtana* movement, and therefore, you alone have the right to be called Jagat-guru–the *guru* for the whole world. There has never been a *guru* more merciful than you, and this is why we, your disciples and followers, consider ourselves to be the most fortunate. We have no qualification, but you have given us the touchstone of success–the Hare Kṛṣṇa movement–and we are forced to bow down before Kṛṣṇa, the Supreme Personality of Godhead, and you, His greatest devotee.

*The residents of New Hastināpura-dhāma, Washington, D. C.*
